## Physical Geography

### 1. Solar System

- **Origin & Age:** Formed ~**4.6 billion years ago** from a rotating cloud of gas and dust (**Nebular Hypothesis** — Immanuel Kant & Pierre-Simon Laplace; **Big Bang Theory** — Georges Lemaître, ~13.8 billion years ago for the Universe).
- **The Sun:**
  - Comprises **99.86%** of the total mass of the Solar System.
  - **Composition:** ~**71–73% Hydrogen**, ~**25–27% Helium**, ~2% heavier elements.
  - **Energy Source:** **Nuclear Fusion** (4 Hydrogen nuclei fuse into 1 Helium nucleus in the core).
  - **Temperature:** Core ~**15 million °C**; Photosphere (visible surface) ~**5,500°C (5,778 K)**; Corona (outermost layer, visible during total solar eclipse) >1 million °C.
  - **Distance from Earth:** **149.6 million km** = **1 Astronomical Unit (AU)**. Sunlight reaches Earth in **8 minutes 20 seconds (~500 seconds)**.
  - **Nearest Star after Sun:** **Proxima Centauri** (~4.24 light-years). **Brightest star in night sky:** **Sirius (Dog Star)**.
- **Mnemonics for Planet Order from Sun:**
  - **M**y **V**ery **E**ducated **M**other **J**ust **S**erved **U**s **N**oodles *(Mercury, Venus, Earth, Mars, Jupiter, Saturn, Uranus, Neptune)*
- **Size Order (Largest to Smallest):** **Jupiter > Saturn > Uranus > Neptune > Earth > Venus > Mars > Mercury**
- **Classification of Planets:**
  - **Inner / Terrestrial / Rocky Planets:** Mercury, Venus, Earth, Mars (inside Asteroid Belt; high density, rocky crust, few/no moons, no rings).
  - **Outer / Jovian Planets:** Jupiter, Saturn (*Gas Giants*); Uranus, Neptune (*Ice Giants*) (outside Asteroid Belt; low density, thick H₂/He/methane atmospheres, many moons, ring systems).

#### All 8 Planets — Exam Comparison Table

| Planet | Distance (AU) | Revolution Period | Rotation Period | Moons | Key Exam Facts & Nicknames |
| :--- | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :--- |
| **Mercury** | 0.39 | **88 days** *(Fastest)* | 58.6 days | **0** | **Smallest planet**; no atmosphere; extreme temp range (+430°C to -180°C); no rings. |
| **Venus** | 0.72 | 225 days | **243 days** *(Slowest; Retrograde)* | **0** | **Hottest planet** (~465°C, 96.5% CO₂ runaway greenhouse); **Brightest planet** (*Morning/Evening Star*); **Earth's Twin**; rotates **East to West**; day > year. |
| **Earth** | 1.00 | 365.25 days | 23h 56m 4s | **1** *(Moon)* | **Blue Planet** (71% water); **Densest planet** (~5.51 g/cm³); axial tilt **23.5°**; supports life. |
| **Mars** | 1.52 | 687 days | 24.6 hours | **2** *(Phobos, Deimos)* | **Red Planet** (iron oxide/ferric oxide); **Olympus Mons** (~21.9 km, highest volcano/mountain in Solar System); Valles Marineris; India's *Mangalyaan (MOM)*. |
| **Jupiter** | 5.20 | 11.86 years | **9h 55m** *(Fastest)* | **95** | **Largest planet**; **shortest day**; **Great Red Spot** (giant storm); faint rings; Galilean moons discovered by Galileo (1610). |
| **Saturn** | 9.54 | 29.45 years | 10.7 hours | **146+** *(274 incl. 2025 IAU; Highest)* | **Least dense planet** (~0.687 g/cm³ — floats on water); prominent icy **rings**; **most moons** in Solar System. |
| **Uranus** | 19.2 | 84 years | 17.2 hours *(Retrograde)* | **28** | **Ice Giant** (methane gives **blue-green/cyan** color); discovered by **William Herschel (1781)**; **98° axial tilt** (*Rolling/Sideways Planet*); rotates **East to West**; coldest planetary atmosphere (-224°C). |
| **Neptune** | 30.1 | **164.8 years** *(Longest)* | 16.1 hours | **16** | **Farthest planet**; deep blue (methane); discovered via math prediction (**Le Verrier & Adams**, observed by **Johann Galle, 1846**); **strongest winds** (~2,100 km/h). |

> **SSC Exam Tip — Retrograde Planets:** Only **Venus** and **Uranus** rotate clockwise (**East to West**) when viewed from above the North Pole (Sun rises in the West here).

#### Dwarf Planets (IAU 2006 Classification)
According to the **International Astronomical Union (IAU, August 2006)**, a dwarf planet orbits the Sun and is nearly round due to gravity, but **has NOT cleared its orbital neighborhood** of other debris.

| Dwarf Planet | Location | Discovered By / Year | Key Exam Facts |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Pluto** | Kuiper Belt | Clyde Tombaugh (1930) | Demoted from 9th planet to dwarf planet in **2006**; orbit ~248 yrs; **5 moons** (largest: **Charon**); visited by **New Horizons (2015)**. |
| **Ceres** | **Asteroid Belt** (Mars–Jupiter) | Giuseppe Piazzi (1801) | **Only dwarf planet in the inner Solar System**; largest object in Asteroid Belt; visited by NASA's **Dawn** mission. |
| **Eris** | Scattered Disc / Beyond Kuiper Belt | Mike Brown et al. (2005) | **Most massive** dwarf planet; moon **Dysnomia**; its discovery triggered Pluto's reclassification. |
| **Makemake** | Kuiper Belt | Mike Brown et al. (2005) | 2nd brightest Kuiper Belt object after Pluto; reddish surface; 1 moon (MK2). |
| **Haumea** | Kuiper Belt | 2004 (Brown / Ortiz) | **Football/ellipsoid shape** due to ultra-fast rotation (~3.9 hrs); has a faint ring & 2 moons (Hi'iaka, Namaka). |

#### Important Satellites (Moons)

| Satellite | Parent Planet | Key Exam Facts |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Moon (Luna)** | Earth | Diameter ~3,474 km (~1/4 Earth); distance ~**384,400 km**; gravity **1/6th of Earth**; light reaches Earth in **~1.3 sec**; **tidally locked** (same side faces Earth; rotation = revolution = **27.3 days** sidereal; **29.5 days** synodic/full moon cycle). Study = **Selenology**. |
| **Phobos & Deimos** | Mars | Small, irregular captured-asteroid-like moons; **Phobos** is larger & orbits faster than Mars rotates (rises in West, sets in East). |
| **Ganymede** | Jupiter | **Largest moon in the entire Solar System** (~5,268 km — larger than Mercury & Pluto); only moon with its own **magnetic field**. |
| **Io** | Jupiter | **Most volcanically active body** in the Solar System (due to tidal heating). |
| **Europa** | Jupiter | Smooth icy surface hiding a vast **subsurface liquid saltwater ocean** (prime target in search for extraterrestrial life). |
| **Callisto** | Jupiter | 3rd largest moon in Solar System; **most heavily cratered** ancient surface. |
| **Titan** | Saturn | **2nd largest moon** (~5,150 km — larger than Mercury); **only moon with a dense atmosphere** (N₂ + CH₄) and liquid methane/ethane lakes. |
| **Enceladus** | Saturn | Emits water-ice geysers from a subsurface ocean; highest reflectivity (albedo) in Solar System. |
| **Triton** | Neptune | Neptune's largest moon; **only large moon with a retrograde orbit**; active nitrogen geysers. |
| **Charon** | Pluto | Largest moon relative to its parent body (~half Pluto's diameter); mutually tidally locked (binary dwarf-planet system). |

#### Comets, Asteroids, Meteors & Meteorites

| Celestial Body | Composition & Origin | Key Exam Facts |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Asteroids (Planetoids)** | Rocky & metallic remnants; **Asteroid Belt** between **Mars & Jupiter**. | Largest: Vesta, Pallas, Hygiea (Ceres reclassified as dwarf planet). Trojan asteroids share Jupiter's orbit. |
| **Comets** | "Dirty snowballs" (ice, frozen gases + dust/rock); originate in **Kuiper Belt** (short-period) & **Oort Cloud** (long-period). | Parts: Nucleus, Coma, Tail. **Tail ALWAYS points AWAY from the Sun** due to solar wind & radiation pressure. **Halley's Comet:** Period **75–76 years**; last seen **1986**; next appearance **2061**. |
| **Meteoroid** | Small rocky/metallic fragment moving in **outer space**. | Smaller than an asteroid. |
| **Meteor ("Shooting Star")** | Meteoroid entering **Earth's atmosphere** and vaporizing due to friction. | Burns up primarily in the **Mesosphere**; produces a streak of light. |
| **Meteorite** | Meteoroid that survives atmospheric burning and **hits Earth's surface**. | **Lonar Lake (Buldhana, Maharashtra)** is a famous meteorite impact crater lake in basalt rock. |

### 2. Earth Facts

#### Basic Physical Dimensions & Motions

| Parameter | Value / Fact |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Shape** | **Oblate Spheroid / Geoid** (flattened at poles, bulging at equator due to rotation). |
| **Age** | ~**4.54 billion years** (~4.5–4.6 billion years). |
| **Equatorial Diameter** | **12,756 km** |
| **Polar Diameter** | **12,714 km** *(Difference = ~42–43 km)* |
| **Mean Diameter / Radius** | Diameter: **12,742 km** \| Mean Radius: **6,371 km** |
| **Equatorial Circumference** | **40,075 km** *(Polar Circumference: **40,008 km**)* |
| **Mean Density** | **5.51 g/cm³** *(Highest among all planets)* |
| **Escape Velocity** | **11.2 km/s** |
| **Surface Ratio** | **70.8% Water** (Hydrosphere) \| **29.2% Land** (Lithosphere) |
| **Rotation (Daily Motion)** | **West to East**; Sidereal Day = **23h 56m 4s**; Solar Day = **24h**; Equatorial speed ~**1,670 km/h** (0 at poles). Causes **Day & Night**, **Coriolis Effect**, Tides. |
| **Revolution (Annual Motion)** | Elliptical orbit around Sun; speed ~**29.8 km/s**; Period = **365 days 5 hrs 48 min 46 sec** (~365.25 days → Leap Year every 4 yrs). Causes **Seasons** & varying day/night lengths. |
| **Axial Tilt (Obliquity)** | **23.5° (23°26′)** to perpendicular of orbital plane; **66.5° (66°34′)** angle with the orbital (ecliptic) plane. |
| **Perihelion (Closest to Sun)** | **January 3** (~147.1 million km) |
| **Aphelion (Farthest from Sun)** | **July 4** (~152.1 million km) |

#### Earth's Internal Layers & Seismic Discontinuities

| Layer | Depth (km) | Volume / Mass | Composition & Physical State | Key Features |
| :--- | :---: | :---: | :--- | :--- |
| **Crust** | **0 – 35 km** *(5–10 km Oceanic; 30–70 km Continental)* | ~1% Vol / <1% Mass | **Solid**. Continental = **SIAL** (Silica + Aluminium, granite, ~2.7 g/cm³); Oceanic = **SIMA** (Silica + Magnesium, basalt, ~3.0 g/cm³). | **Crust Elements (%):** **Oxygen (46.6%) > Silicon (27.7%) > Aluminium (8.1%) > Iron (5.0%) > Calcium (3.6%)**. |
| **Mantle** | **35 – 2,900 km** | **~84% Vol** / ~68% Mass | **Mg & Fe Silicates** (Olivine, Pyroxene). Upper mantle includes **Asthenosphere** (100–400 km, semi-molten/plastic); Lower mantle is solid/rigid. | **Lithosphere** = Crust + uppermost rigid Mantle (0–100 km). Asthenosphere is the source of magma & drives plate tectonics. |
| **Outer Core** | **2,900 – 5,150 km** | ~15% Vol (Total Core) / ~32% Mass | **NIFE** (Nickel + Iron) in **Liquid / Molten** state. | **S-waves cannot pass** through it; convective motion generates **Earth's magnetic field (Geodynamo)**. |
| **Inner Core** | **5,150 – 6,371 km** | *(Part of Core)* | **NIFE** (Nickel + Iron) in **Solid** state. | Temp ~5,000–6,000°C; remains **solid due to extreme pressure**. Overall **Whole Earth** most abundant element = **Iron (~32–35%)**. |

**Seismic Discontinuities (Top to Bottom — Mnemonic: *C-M-R-G-L*):**

| Discontinuity | Separates | Approx. Depth |
| :--- | :--- | :---: |
| **Conrad Discontinuity** | Upper Crust (SIAL) & Lower Crust (SIMA) | ~15–20 km |
| **Mohorovičić (Moho) Discontinuity** | Crust & Mantle | ~35 km |
| **Repetti Discontinuity** | Upper Mantle & Lower Mantle | ~670–700 km |
| **Gutenberg Discontinuity** | Lower Mantle & Outer Core | **2,900 km** |
| **Lehmann Discontinuity** | Outer Core & Inner Core | **5,150 km** |

#### Latitude & Longitude System, Prime Meridian & International Date Line

| Concept | Key Details & SSC Facts |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Latitudes (Parallels)** | Angular distance North/South of **Equator (0°)**. Total **181 parallels** (179 circles + 2 pole points). Parallel to each other; **1° latitude ≈ 111 km** (constant everywhere). Only the **Equator** is a **Great Circle** (~40,075 km). |
| **Important Parallels & Heat Zones** | **Equator (0°)**, **Tropic of Cancer (23.5° N)**, **Tropic of Capricorn (23.5° S)**, **Arctic Circle (66.5° N)**, **Antarctic Circle (66.5° S)**. **Torrid Zone:** 23.5°N–23.5°S; **Temperate Zone:** 23.5°–66.5° N/S; **Frigid Zone:** 66.5°–90° N/S. **Horse Latitudes:** ~30°–35° N/S (subtropical high-pressure calm belts). |
| **Longitudes (Meridians)** | Angular distance East/West of Prime Meridian (0°). **360 meridians** (semi-circles converging at poles; opposite meridians form Great Circles). Spacing is **maximum at Equator (~111.32 km)** and **0 km at Poles**. |
| **Time–Longitude Relation** | 360° = 24 hours → **15° = 1 hour** → **1° = 4 minutes**. Moving **East (+)** gains time; moving **West (−)** loses time. |
| **Prime Meridian (0°)** | Passes through **Greenwich (London, UK)** — basis of **GMT / UTC**. Crosses 8 countries: **UK, France, Spain, Algeria, Mali, Burkina Faso, Togo, Ghana** (+ Antarctica). Intersects **Equator (0°)** in the **Gulf of Guinea** (Atlantic Ocean). |
| **International Date Line (IDL)** | Roughly follows **180° longitude** in the Pacific Ocean (zig-zags at Bering Strait, Aleutian Islands, Fiji, Tonga, Kiribati to avoid splitting countries). Crossing **West → East (Asia to Americas)**: **Gain / Repeat 1 day** (subtract 1 date, e.g., Monday → Sunday). Crossing **East → West (Americas to Asia)**: **Lose / Skip 1 day** (add 1 date, e.g., Sunday → Monday). |

#### Eclipses, Equinoxes & Solstices

| Phenomenon | Alignment / Date | Key Exam Facts |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Solar Eclipse** | **Sun – Moon – Earth (S-M-E)** | Moon blocks Sun; occurs **ONLY on New Moon (Amavasya)** (not monthly due to 5.1° orbital tilt of Moon). Types: Total, Partial, Annular (*Ring of Fire*). Corona, *Baily's Beads* & *Diamond Ring* visible during totality (max ~7.5 min). |
| **Lunar Eclipse** | **Sun – Earth – Moon (S-E-M)** | Earth's shadow falls on Moon; occurs **ONLY on Full Moon (Purnima)**. Total eclipse creates **"Blood Moon"** (reddish hue from Rayleigh scattering of sunlight through Earth's atmosphere). |
| **Syzygy** | Straight-line **S–E–M / S–M–E** | Occurs on New & Full Moon; produces **Spring Tides** (highest high tides). At quadrature (90°), produces **Neap Tides**. |
| **Vernal (Spring) Equinox** | **21 March** (Mar 20/21) | Sun directly overhead at **Equator (0°)**; **Equal Day & Night (12 hrs each)** globally. |
| **Autumnal Equinox** | **23 September** (Sep 22/23) | Sun directly overhead at **Equator (0°)**; **Equal Day & Night (12 hrs each)** globally. |
| **Summer Solstice (N. Hem.)** | **21 June** (Jun 20/21) | Sun overhead at **Tropic of Cancer (23.5° N)**; **longest day & shortest night** in Northern Hemisphere; 24-hr daylight beyond Arctic Circle (*Norway — Land of Midnight Sun*). |
| **Winter Solstice (N. Hem.)** | **22 December** (Dec 21/22) | Sun overhead at **Tropic of Capricorn (23.5° S)**; **shortest day & longest night** in Northern Hemisphere; longest day in Southern Hemisphere. |

### 3. Atmosphere

#### Composition of Dry Atmosphere

| Gas | Percentage (by Volume) | Key Exam Role |
| :--- | :---: | :--- |
| **Nitrogen ($\text{N}_2$)** | **78.08%** | Most abundant gas; dilutes O₂, prevents rapid combustion; essential for plant proteins via nitrogen fixation. |
| **Oxygen ($\text{O}_2$)** | **20.95% (~21%)** | Essential for respiration and combustion. |
| **Argon ($\text{Ar}$)** | **0.93%** | **Most abundant noble/inert gas** in the atmosphere. |
| **Carbon Dioxide ($\text{CO}_2$)** | **0.036% – 0.04%** *(NCERT: 0.03%)* | Transparent to incoming solar radiation, **absorbs outgoing terrestrial (infrared) radiation** → Greenhouse Effect; used in photosynthesis. |
| **Trace Gases** | ~0.003% | Neon (0.0018%), Helium (0.0005%), Methane, Krypton, Hydrogen, Ozone, Xenon. |
| **Water Vapor & Dust** | Variable (0–4% H₂O) | Restricted mostly to lower troposphere; acts as blanket; dust/salt particles act as **hygroscopic condensation nuclei** for clouds. |

#### Layers of the Atmosphere

| Layer | Altitude | Temperature Behavior | Upper Boundary | Key Features & Exam Facts |
| :--- | :---: | :--- | :---: | :--- |
| **1. Troposphere** | **0 – 8 km** (Poles) to **18 km** (Equator); Avg ~13 km | **Decreases** with height at **Normal Lapse Rate = 6.5°C/km** *(1°C per 165 m)* | **Tropopause** (~ -80°C at Equator, -45°C at Poles) | Densest layer (~75–80% mass, 99% water vapor); **ALL weather phenomena** (rain, clouds, storms, lightning, winds) occur here; tallest over Equator due to strong convection. |
| **2. Stratosphere** | **13/18 km – 50 km** | **Increases** with height (due to UV absorption by Ozone) | **Stratopause** (~0°C) | Contains **Ozone Layer (Ozonosphere)** mainly at **15–35 km** (peak ~20–25 km); cloud-free & calm → **ideal for jet aircrafts**; rare *nacreous (mother-of-pearl)* clouds. |
| **3. Mesosphere** | **50 – 80/85 km** | **Decreases** rapidly down to **-90°C to -100°C** | **Mesopause** | **Coldest layer** of the atmosphere; **meteors burn up** here due to friction; *noctilucent clouds*. |
| **4. Thermosphere (incl. Ionosphere)** | **80/85 km – ~400–700 km** *(Ionosphere: 80–400 km)* | **Increases** sharply (up to 1,500°C+; air too thin to feel hot) | **Thermopause** | **Ionosphere** (D, E, F layers) contains charged ions → **reflects radio waves back to Earth**; **Aurora Borealis & Aurora Australis** occur here; **ISS** orbits here (~400 km); **Kármán Line (100 km)** marks space boundary. |
| **5. Exosphere** | **Above ~700 km – 10,000 km** | Gradually merges into space | Merges with outer space | Extremely rarefied **Hydrogen & Helium**; merges into interplanetary space. |

#### Greenhouse Effect & Ozone Layer Depletion

- **Greenhouse Effect:** Incoming **short-wave solar radiation** warms Earth's surface, which emits **long-wave infrared (terrestrial) radiation**. Greenhouse gases trap part of this outgoing heat, keeping Earth's mean surface temperature at **~15°C** (instead of **-18°C** without it).
  - **Major Greenhouse Gases (GHGs):** Water vapor ($\text{H}_2\text{O}$, highest natural share), Carbon dioxide ($\text{CO}_2$, highest anthropogenic share), Methane ($\text{CH}_4$), Nitrous oxide ($\text{N}_2\text{O}$), Ozone ($\text{O}_3$), CFCs/HFCs, $\text{SF}_6$. *(Note: $\text{N}_2$, $\text{O}_2$, $\text{Ar}$ are NOT GHGs).*
  - **Global Treaties:** **Kyoto Protocol (1997, effective 2005)** to curb GHG emissions; **Paris Agreement (2015)** to limit warming below 2°C (aim 1.5°C).
- **Ozone Layer & Depletion:**
  - **Stratospheric Ozone ("Good Ozone"):** Shields life from harmful **UV-B & UV-C** radiation (skin cancer, cataracts, DNA damage). **Tropospheric Ozone ("Bad Ozone"):** Pollutant in photochemical smog.
  - **Measurement Unit:** **Dobson Unit (DU)** (Normal ~300 DU; "Ozone Hole" <220 DU, discovered over **Antarctica in 1985** by Farman, Gardiner & Shanklin).
  - **Ozone-Depleting Substances (ODS):** **CFCs (Chlorofluorocarbons / Freons)** used in ACs, refrigerators, aerosols; Halons; Methyl bromide; $\text{CCl}_4$. One Chlorine radical destroys up to ~100,000 $\text{O}_3$ molecules.
  - **Montreal Protocol:** Signed **16 September 1987** (effective 1989) to phase out ODS. **World Ozone Day = 16 September**. **Kigali Amendment (2016)** phases down HFCs (non-ODS potent GHGs).

### 4. Rocks and Minerals

Study of rocks = **Petrology**.

#### Classification of Rocks

| Rock Type | Formation Process | Key Characteristics | Examples |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **1. Igneous Rocks** *(Primary / Parent Rocks)* | Cooling & solidification of molten **magma** (underground) or **lava** (surface). | Crystalline, hard, non-porous, **no layers/strata**, **NO fossils**. | **Intrusive (Plutonic — slow cooling, big crystals):** **Granite, Gabbro, Diorite, Pegmatite**. *(Forms: Batholith, Laccolith, Sill, Dyke)*.<br>**Extrusive (Volcanic — fast cooling, fine/glassy):** **Basalt** *(Deccan Trap)*, **Obsidian, Pumice, Rhyolite, Andesite**. |
| **2. Sedimentary Rocks** *(Secondary / Stratified)* | Weathering, deposition, compaction & cementation (**lithification**) of sediments. | **Layered (stratified)**, porous, softer, **CONTAIN FOSSILS**; cover ~75% of land area (5% of crust volume). | **Mechanically formed:** **Sandstone, Shale, Conglomerate, Loess**.<br>**Chemically formed:** **Rock Salt (Halite), Gypsum, Potash, Chert**.<br>**Organically formed:** **Coal** *(Peat → Lignite → Bituminous → Anthracite)*, **Limestone, Chalk, Dolomite**. *(Petroleum is also found here)*. |
| **3. Metamorphic Rocks** *(Transformed Rocks)* | Alteration of pre-existing rocks under intense **heat and/or pressure** without complete melting. | Hard, recrystallized, often **foliated/banded** (Gneiss, Schist) or non-foliated (Marble, Quartzite); fossils destroyed. | **Gneiss, Schist, Marble, Quartzite, Slate, Phyllite, Anthracite, Diamond**. |

#### Important Metamorphic Conversions (SSC Frequent Questions)

| Original Rock | Type of Original | Metamorphic Rock Formed |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Granite** | Igneous | **Gneiss** |
| **Basalt / Gabbro** | Igneous | **Schist / Amphibolite** |
| **Limestone / Dolomite** | Sedimentary | **Marble** |
| **Sandstone** | Sedimentary | **Quartzite** |
| **Shale / Clay** | Sedimentary | **Slate** *(→ Phyllite → Schist)* |
| **Bituminous Coal** | Sedimentary | **Anthracite / Graphite / Diamond** |

#### The Rock Cycle
Continuous transformation of rocks across the three types:
1. **Magma** cools & crystallizes → **Igneous Rock**.
2. Igneous/Metamorphic/Sedimentary rocks undergo weathering, erosion, transport & lithification → **Sedimentary Rock**.
3. Igneous or Sedimentary rocks undergo intense heat & pressure → **Metamorphic Rock**.
4. Any rock pushed deep underground melts back into **Magma**, restarting the cycle.

#### Important Minerals & Their Ores

| Metal / Element | Major Ores & Chemical Formulas / Exam Notes |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Iron ($\text{Fe}$)** | **Hematite** ($\text{Fe}_2\text{O}_3$ — most abundant in India), **Magnetite** ($\text{Fe}_3\text{O}_4$ — highest iron content ~72%, best quality, magnetic), **Limonite**, **Siderite**, Iron Pyrites ($\text{FeS}_2$ — *"Fool's Gold"*). |
| **Aluminium ($\text{Al}$)** | **Bauxite** ($\text{Al}_2\text{O}_3 \cdot 2\text{H}_2\text{O}$ — primary ore), **Cryolite**, **Corundum** ($\text{Al}_2\text{O}_3$). |
| **Copper ($\text{Cu}$)** | **Chalcopyrite / Copper Pyrites** ($\text{CuFeS}_2$), **Cuprite**, **Malachite**, **Azurite**, Chalcocite. |
| **Lead ($\text{Pb}$)** | **Galena** ($\text{PbS}$), Cerussite, Anglesite. |
| **Zinc ($\text{Zn}$)** | **Sphalerite / Zinc Blende** ($\text{ZnS}$), **Calamine** ($\text{ZnCO}_3$), Zincite. |
| **Mercury ($\text{Hg}$)** | **Cinnabar** ($\text{HgS}$). |
| **Tin ($\text{Sn}$)** | **Cassiterite** ($\text{SnO}_2$). |
| **Silver ($\text{Ag}$)** | **Argentite** ($\text{Ag}_2\text{S}$), Horn Silver (Chlorargyrite). |
| **Gold ($\text{Au}$)** | Native state (free metal), **Calaverite**, Sylvanite. |
| **Uranium ($\text{U}$)** | **Pitchblende (Uraninite)**, Carnotite. |
| **Thorium ($\text{Th}$)** | **Monazite** *(abundant in Kerala coastal sands)*. |
| **Manganese ($\text{Mn}$)** | **Pyrolusite** ($\text{MnO}_2$), Psilomelane. |
| **Magnesium ($\text{Mg}$)** | **Magnesite**, **Dolomite**, **Carnallite**, Epsom salt. |
| **Calcium ($\text{Ca}$)** | **Limestone** ($\text{CaCO}_3$), **Gypsum** ($\text{CaSO}_4 \cdot 2\text{H}_2\text{O}$), Fluorspar. |
| **Sodium ($\text{Na}$)** | **Halite / Rock Salt** ($\text{NaCl}$), **Chile Saltpetre** ($\text{NaNO}_3$), Borax. |
| **Potassium ($\text{K}$)** | **Sylvite**, **Carnallite**, Nitre / Saltpetre ($\text{KNO}_3$). |
| **Titanium ($\text{Ti}$)** | **Ilmenite**, **Rutile**. |
| **Chromium ($\text{Cr}$)** | **Chromite**. |
| **Nickel ($\text{Ni}$)** | **Pentlandite**, Garnierite. |
| **Tungsten ($\text{W}$)** | **Wolframite**, Scheelite. |

> **Mohs Hardness Scale:** Softest = **Talc (1)** < Gypsum (2) < Calcite (3) < Fluorite (4) < Apatite (5) < Orthoclase (6) < Quartz (7) < Topaz (8) < Corundum (9) < Hardest = **Diamond (10)**.

---

### 5. Earthquakes and Volcanoes

#### Earthquakes — Basics & Types
- **Seismology:** Study of earthquakes; **Seismograph:** Instrument recording seismic waves (output = *Seismogram*).
- **Focus (Hypocenter):** Point **inside** Earth's crust where earthquake originates.
- **Epicenter:** Point on **Earth's surface** vertically above the focus (first to feel tremor, max intensity).
- **Isoseismal Line:** Connects places of equal earthquake intensity. **Homoseismal Line:** Connects places experiencing shock at the same time.
- **Types of Earthquakes:**
  1. **Tectonic Earthquakes:** Most common (~90%); caused by sudden slip along fault lines at plate boundaries.
  2. **Volcanic Earthquakes:** Triggered by magma movement or explosive eruptions.
  3. **Collapse Earthquakes:** Collapse of roofs in underground mines/caverns.
  4. **Explosion Earthquakes:** Nuclear/chemical detonations.
  5. **Reservoir-Induced Seismicity (RIS):** Weight of water in large dams (e.g., **Koyna Dam, Maharashtra, 1967**).

#### Seismic Waves Comparison

| Feature | P-Waves *(Primary)* | S-Waves *(Secondary)* | L-Waves *(Surface / Love & Rayleigh)* |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Speed & Arrival** | **Fastest** (~6–8 km/s); arrives **1st**. | Slower (~3.5–4.5 km/s); arrives **2nd**. | **Slowest** (~2–3.5 km/s); arrives **last**. |
| **Wave Type** | **Longitudinal / Compressional** (parallel to direction, like sound). | **Transverse / Shear** (perpendicular to direction, like light). | Surface shear (Love) & rolling elliptical (Rayleigh). |
| **Medium** | **Solids, Liquids & Gases**. | **ONLY Solids** *(cannot pass through liquid Outer Core)*. | Earth's **surface only**. |
| **Shadow Zone** | **105° – 145°** from epicenter. | **Beyond 105°** (105°–105° across antipode — much larger). | — |
| **Destructiveness** | Least destructive. | Moderate. | **MOST DESTRUCTIVE** (maximum structural damage). |

#### Measurement Scales
- **Richter Scale (1935, Charles F. Richter):** Measures **Magnitude** (energy released). Logarithmic (0–10): each **+1 unit = 10× wave amplitude** and **~31.6× (~32×) energy released**.
- **Moment Magnitude Scale ($M_w$):** Modern scale accurate for huge earthquakes (>7).
- **Modified Mercalli Intensity (MMI) Scale:** Measures **Intensity / visible damage** on a **I to XII (1–12)** scale.

#### Seismic Zones of India (BIS IS 1893 — 4 Zones; Zone I merged with Zone II)

| Seismic Zone | Risk Level & Intensity | Land Area (%) | Major Regions Covered |
| :--- | :--- | :---: | :--- |
| **Zone V** | **Very High Risk** (MSK IX+) | ~11% | Entire **North-East India**, **Rann of Kutch (Gujarat)**, **Uttarakhand**, **Himachal Pradesh**, parts of **J&K & Ladakh**, **Northern Bihar**, **Andaman & Nicobar Islands**. |
| **Zone IV** | **High Risk** (MSK VIII) | ~18% | **Delhi-NCR**, **Sikkim**, remaining **J&K, Ladakh, HP, Uttarakhand**, northern **UP, Bihar, West Bengal (Darjeeling)**, **Punjab, Haryana**, **Koyna/Latur (Maharashtra)**, parts of Gujarat. |
| **Zone III** | **Moderate Risk** (MSK VII) | ~30% | **Kerala, Goa, Lakshadweep**, remaining **UP, Bihar, WB, Gujarat, Punjab**, parts of **Maharashtra, MP, Rajasthan, Jharkhand, Chhattisgarh, Odisha, AP, TN, Telangana**. |
| **Zone II** | **Low Risk** (MSK VI or less) | ~41% | Stable **Peninsular / Deccan Shield** (parts of Karnataka, Telangana, AP, TN, MP, Chhattisgarh, Odisha, Rajasthan). |

#### Volcanoes — Types, Ring of Fire & Major Eruptions
- **Magma** (subsurface molten rock) vs **Lava** (surface molten rock). **Crater** (vent opening) vs **Caldera** (vast collapsed basin).
- **By Activity:**
  - **Active:** Erupt frequently/recently — **Mt. Etna & Stromboli** (*"Lighthouse of the Mediterranean"*, Italy), **Kilauea & Mauna Loa** (Hawaii), **Mt. Merapi** (Indonesia), **Ojos del Salado** (Chile/Argentina, ~6,893 m — highest active volcano), **Cotopaxi** (Ecuador), **Mt. Erebus** (Antarctica), **Barren Island** (Andaman & Nicobar — **India's ONLY active volcano**).
  - **Dormant (Sleeping):** Quiet for long, may erupt again — **Mt. Vesuvius** (Italy), **Mt. Fuji** (Japan), **Krakatoa** (Indonesia), **Narcondam Island** (Andaman & Nicobar — India).
  - **Extinct (Dead):** No historic eruption, magma cut off — **Mt. Kilimanjaro** (Tanzania), **Mt. Kenya**, **Mt. Popa** (Myanmar), **Aconcagua** (Argentina), **Chimborazo** (Ecuador), **Koh-i-Sultan** (Pakistan).
- **By Structure:**
  - **Shield Volcano:** Broad, gentle slopes, fluid basaltic lava (e.g., **Mauna Loa**, Hawaii).
  - **Composite / Stratovolcano:** Tall, steep, alternating lava & ash, highly explosive (e.g., **Mt. Fuji, Vesuvius, Mt. St. Helens**).
  - **Cinder Cone:** Small, steep, pyroclastic fragments (e.g., **Paricutin**, Mexico).
  - **Flood Basalt Province:** Fissure eruptions over vast plains (e.g., **Deccan Traps**, India).
- **Pacific Ring of Fire (Circum-Pacific Belt):** Horseshoe belt (~40,000 km) around the Pacific Ocean caused by **subduction at convergent plate boundaries**. Contains **~75% of world's active volcanoes** and **~90% of world's earthquakes**. *(Australia is the only continent with NO active mainland volcanoes)*.
- **Major Volcanic Eruptions:**
  - **Mt. Vesuvius (79 AD, Italy):** Destroyed Pompeii & Herculaneum.
  - **Mt. Tambora (1815, Indonesia):** Largest recorded eruption (VEI 7); caused *"Year Without a Summer"* (1816).
  - **Krakatoa (1883, Indonesia):** Loudest sound in recorded history; deadly tsunami.
  - **Mt. Pelée (1902, Martinique):** Pyroclastic flow wiped out Saint-Pierre.
  - **Mt. St. Helens (1980, USA):** Major lateral blast.
  - **Mt. Pinatubo (1991, Philippines):** Lowered global temp by ~0.5°C.
  - **Hunga Tonga–Hunga Ha'apai (2022, Tonga):** Massive submarine eruption; plume reached ~57 km (Mesosphere).

---

### 6. Weathering, Erosion and Landforms

- **Weathering:** **In-situ** (on-site) breakdown of rocks with no significant transport.
- **Erosion:** Wearing down **plus transportation** by rivers, wind, glaciers, waves, or groundwater.

#### Types of Weathering

| Type | Mechanism | Key Processes & Examples |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Physical (Mechanical)** | Disintegration without altering chemical composition. | **Exfoliation (onion-peeling)** by thermal expansion/contraction in deserts; **Frost Action / Freeze-Thaw** (water freezes & expands ~9% in cracks); **Salt weathering**; **Unloading**. |
| **Chemical** | Decomposition altering mineral composition. | **Oxidation** (iron rusting → red soil); **Carbonation** ($\text{CO}_2$ + rainwater dissolves **limestone → Karst topography**); **Hydration**; **Hydrolysis** (feldspar → clay); **Solution**. |
| **Biological** | Disintegration/decay by living organisms. | **Root wedging** by trees; **burrowing animals** (rodents, earthworms); **lichens/mosses** secreting organic acids; human mining. |

#### Erosional & Depositional Landforms by Agent

| Agent | Erosional Landforms | Depositional Landforms | Key Exam Examples |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **River (Fluvial)** | **V-shaped Valley**, **Gorge** (steep narrow valley), **Canyon** (stepped walls), **Waterfall & Plunge Pool**, **Potholes**, **Interlocking Spurs**, **Meanders** (outer cut-bank erosion). | **Alluvial Fans / Cones**, **Floodplain**, **Natural Levees**, **Point Bars**, **Oxbow Lake** (cut-off meander), **Braided Channels**, **Delta** *(Arcuate, Bird's Foot, Cuspate)*. | **Grand Canyon** (Colorado); **Kanwar Lake** (Bihar — Asia's largest freshwater oxbow lake); **Sundarbans** (Arcuate, world's largest delta); **Mississippi** (Bird's foot delta). |
| **Wind (Aeolian)** | **Mushroom Rocks (Gara / Pedestal Rocks)** (undercutting near ground), **Yardangs**, **Zeugen**, **Inselberg**, **Deflation Hollows**, **Ventifacts**. | **Sand Dunes** — **Barchans** (crescent, horns downwind), **Seif / Longitudinal** (parallel), **Transverse**, **Parabolic**, **Star**; **Loess** (fine fertile wind-blown silt). | **Loess Plateau** (China); **Qattara Depression** (Egypt); **Uluru** (Inselberg, Australia); **Barchans** (Thar & Sahara). |
| **Glacier (Glacial)** | **U-shaped Valley**, **Hanging Valley** (causes waterfalls), **Cirque / Corrie** (armchair basin → **Tarn Lake**), **Arête** (knife-edge ridge), **Horn** (pyramidal peak), **Fjord** (submerged glacial valley), **Roche Moutonnée**. | **Moraines** (Lateral, Medial, Terminal, Ground), **Drumlins** (*"basket of eggs"* inverted-spoon hills), **Eskers** (sinuous meltwater ridges), **Kames**, **Outwash Plains**. | **Matterhorn** (Horn, Alps); **Norway Coast** (Fjords); **Karewas** of Kashmir (lacustrine/glacio-fluvial deposits). |
| **Sea Waves (Coastal)** | **Sea Cliff**, **Wave-cut Platform**, **Sea Cave** → **Sea Arch** → **Sea Stack** → **Stump**. | **Beach**, **Offshore Bar**, **Spit** (sand ridge attached at one end), **Tombolo** (bar connecting island to mainland), **Lagoon** (enclosed coastal saltwater body). | **Chilika Lake** (Odisha), **Pulicat Lake** (AP/TN), **Vembanad** (Kerala) — lagoons; **Chesil Beach** (UK tombolo). |
| **Groundwater (Karst)** | **Sinkholes / Dolines**, **Swallow Holes**, **Lapies**, **Polje**, **Caverns** (in limestone). | **Stalactites** (*hang from Ceiling*), **Stalagmites** (*grow from Ground*), **Columns / Pillars** (both meet). | **Borra Caves** (AP), **Kutumsar Caves** (Chhattisgarh), **Mawsmai Caves** (Meghalaya). |

## Indian Geography - Basics

### 7. India: Position, Area and Boundaries

#### Geographical Profile of India

| Attribute | Exact Fact / Value |
| :--- | :--- |
| **Hemisphere** | **Northern & Eastern Hemisphere** (South-Central Asia) |
| **Latitudinal Extent** | **8°4′ N to 37°6′ N** *(Mainland)* \| **6°45′ N to 37°6′ N** *(incl. A&N Islands)* |
| **Longitudinal Extent** | **68°7′ E to 97°25′ E** |
| **North–South Extent** | **3,214 km** |
| **East–West Extent** | **2,933 km** |
| **Total Area** | **3,287,263 sq km** (**3.28 million sq km**; **2.4%** of world land area) |
| **World Area Rank** | **7th Largest** *(Russia > Canada > USA > China > Brazil > Australia > **India**)* |
| **Land Boundary** | **15,200 km** *(NCERT approx.)* \| **15,106.7 km** *(MHA precise figure)* |
| **Coastline Length** | **6,100 km** *(Mainland)* \| **7,516.6 km** *(Total incl. A&N and Lakshadweep)* |
| **Coastal States & UTs** | **9 States:** Gujarat (*Longest*, ~1,214.7 km), Andhra Pradesh, Tamil Nadu, Maharashtra, Kerala, Odisha, Karnataka, West Bengal, Goa (*Shortest*). **4 UTs:** A&N Islands (*longest overall* ~1,962 km), Lakshadweep, Puducherry, DNH & DD. |

#### Neighboring Countries & Shared Borders (Mnemonic: *BaChPaN MBA*)

| Country | Border Length (km) | Border Line / Name | Bordering Indian States & UTs | Key Exam Notes |
| :--- | :---: | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **1. Bangladesh** | **4,096.7 km** *(Longest)* | **Radcliffe Line** (1947) | **5 States:** West Bengal (*longest*), Assam, Meghalaya, Tripura, Mizoram. | **Tripura** is surrounded on 3 sides by Bangladesh (*Zero Line*). |
| **2. China** | **3,488 km** | **McMahon Line** (East, 1914); **LAC** | **4 States + 1 UT:** **Ladakh (UT)**, Himachal Pradesh, Uttarakhand, Sikkim, Arunachal Pradesh. | Divided into Western, Middle, and Eastern sectors. |
| **3. Pakistan** | **3,323 km** | **Radcliffe Line** (1947); **LoC** | **3 States + 2 UTs:** **Jammu & Kashmir (UT)**, **Ladakh (UT)**, Punjab, Rajasthan (*longest*), Gujarat. | **24th Parallel** disputed in Sir Creek (Rann of Kutch). |
| **4. Nepal** | **1,751 km** | Open Border | **5 States:** Uttarakhand, Uttar Pradesh, Bihar (*longest*), West Bengal, Sikkim. | **Sugauli Treaty (1816)**; Kali River boundary dispute. |
| **5. Myanmar** | **1,643 km** | **Irrawaddy–Chindwin** divide | **4 States:** Arunachal Pradesh, Nagaland, Manipur, Mizoram. | Separated by Patkai, Naga & Lushai hills. |
| **6. Bhutan** | **699 km** | Open Border | **4 States:** Sikkim, West Bengal, Assam (*longest*), Arunachal Pradesh. | *Land of Thunder Dragon*; Doklam tri-junction nearby. |
| **7. Afghanistan** | **106 km** *(Shortest)* | **Durand Line** (1893) | **1 UT:** **Ladakh** *(Wakhan Corridor, PoK region)*. | Also separates Pakistan & Afghanistan. |
| **Sri Lanka** *(Maritime)* | Maritime | **Palk Strait** & **Gulf of Mannar** | Nearest state: **Tamil Nadu** | Connected via **Adam's Bridge (Rama Setu)** & Pamban Island. |
| **Maldives** *(Maritime)* | Maritime | **8° Channel** | Nearest UT: **Lakshadweep (Minicoy)** | — |

> **Important Maritime Channels:** **8° Channel:** Minicoy & Maldives \| **9° Channel:** Minicoy & Lakshadweep \| **10° Channel:** Little Andaman & Car Nicobar \| **Duncan Passage:** South Andaman & Little Andaman \| **Great Channel:** Great Nicobar & Sumatra (Indonesia) \| **Coco Channel:** North Andaman & Coco Islands (Myanmar).

#### Indian Standard Time (IST) & Tropic of Cancer

| Feature | Details | States Passed Through |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Indian Standard Time (IST)** | **82°30′ E (82.5° E)**; **GMT + 5 hours 30 minutes**; passes near **Mirzapur / Naini (Prayagraj, UP)**. | **5 States (N → S):** **Uttar Pradesh, Madhya Pradesh, Chhattisgarh, Odisha, Andhra Pradesh**. |
| **Tropic of Cancer** | **23°30′ N (23.5° N)**; divides India roughly into tropical (south) & subtropical (north) halves. | **8 States (W → E):** **Gujarat, Rajasthan, Madhya Pradesh, Chhattisgarh, Jharkhand, West Bengal, Tripura, Mizoram**. |

- **SSC Special Traps:**
  - **IST (82.5°E) and Tropic of Cancer (23.5°N) intersect** in **Chhattisgarh** (Koriya / Surajpur district region).
  - **Mahi River** crosses the **Tropic of Cancer TWICE**.
  - **Time Lag:** ~30° longitudinal span causes **~2 hours** time difference between Arunachal Pradesh and Gujarat.
  - **State with Maximum Neighbors:** **Uttar Pradesh** borders **8 States + 1 UT (Delhi)**; **Sonbhadra** district (UP) touches **4 states** (MP, Chhattisgarh, Jharkhand, Bihar).

#### Extreme Points of India

| Direction | Extreme Point | Location / State or UT | Coordinates / Notes |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Northernmost** | **Indira Col** | Siachen Glacier, Karakoram (**Ladakh UT**) | **37°6′ N** |
| **Southernmost (Indian Union)** | **Indira Point** *(formerly Pygmalion / Parsons Point)* | **Great Nicobar Island** (**A&N Islands**) | **6°45′ N** *(partially submerged in 2004 Tsunami)* |
| **Southernmost (Mainland)** | **Kanyakumari (Cape Comorin)** | **Tamil Nadu** | **8°4′ N** *(meeting point of Arabian Sea, Indian Ocean, Bay of Bengal)* |
| **Easternmost** | **Kibithu** | Anjaw district (**Arunachal Pradesh**) | **97°25′ E** |
| **Westernmost** | **Guhar Moti** *(near Sir Creek)* | Kutch district (**Gujarat**) | **68°7′ E** |

### 8. Physiographic Divisions of India

#### 1. The Himalayan Mountains
Young fold mountains formed by collision of **Indian Plate** & **Eurasian Plate** (uplift of **Tethys Sea**); length ~**2,400 km** (Indus gorge to Brahmaputra gorge), width **400 km (Kashmir) → 150 km (Arunachal)**; western syntaxial bend at **Nanga Parbat**, eastern at **Namcha Barwa**.

- **Trans-Himalayas (Tibetan Himalayas):** North of Great Himalayas — **Karakoram** (*K2*, *Siachen Glacier* ~76 km — longest in India), **Ladakh Range**, **Zanskar Range**, **Kailash Range** (Tibet).

| Himalayan Division | Other Names | Avg. Elevation | Rock / Composition | Key Features & Ranges |
| :--- | :--- | :---: | :--- | :--- |
| **Greater Himalayas** | **Himadri / Inner Himalayas** | **~6,000 m** | **Granite** core | Continuous snowline; world's highest peaks (**Everest, Kanchenjunga, Nanda Devi**); **Gangotri & Yamunotri** glaciers. |
| **Lesser Himalayas** | **Himachal / Middle Himalayas** | **3,700 – 4,500 m** | Compressed altered rocks | **Pir Panjal** *(longest range)*, **Dhauladhar**, **Nag Tibba**, **Mahabharat**. Valleys: **Kashmir** (*Karewas* for **Saffron**), Kangra, Kullu. Hill stations: Shimla, Mussoorie, Nainital, Darjeeling. |
| **Outer Himalayas** | **Shiwaliks** | **900 – 1,100 m** | Unconsolidated river sediments | Youngest & southernmost range. Longitudinal valleys between Himachal & Shiwaliks = **Duns** (west: *Dehradun, Kotli Dun*) & **Duars** (east: *tea gardens*). |

- **Longitudinal Division by Rivers (Sir Sidney Burrard — West to East):**
  1. **Punjab / Kashmir Himalayas** (~560 km): **Indus to Sutlej**
  2. **Kumaon Himalayas** (~320 km): **Sutlej to Kali** *(Nanda Devi, Kamet)*
  3. **Nepal Himalayas** (~800 km — *longest & highest*): **Kali to Teesta** *(Everest, Kanchenjunga, Makalu, Dhaulagiri, Annapurna)*
  4. **Assam / Eastern Himalayas** (~720 km): **Teesta to Dihang (Brahmaputra)** *(Namcha Barwa)*
- **Purvanchal (Eastern Hills):** Southward bend across NE states — **Patkai Bum**, **Naga Hills** (*Saramati*), **Manipur Hills** (*Loktak Lake*), **Mizo / Lushai Hills** (*Blue Mountain / Phawngpui*). *(Note: Garo, Khasi, Jaintia Hills belong to Peninsular Plateau, NOT Himalayas!).*

#### Major Mountain Peaks of India & Himalayas

| Peak | Height (m) | Range / Region | Location | Exam Significance |
| :--- | :---: | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Mount Everest** | **8,848.86 m** | Great Himalayas | Nepal–Tibet border | **World's highest peak** (*Sagarmatha* in Nepal, *Chomolungma* in Tibet); Bachendri Pal (1st Indian woman, 1984). |
| **K2 (Godwin-Austen)** | **8,611 m** | **Karakoram** | Ladakh (PoK) | **2nd highest in World**; highest peak claimed by India. |
| **Kanchenjunga** | **8,586 m** | Great Himalayas | **Sikkim**–Nepal border | **3rd highest in World**; **Highest peak in administered India**. |
| **Nanga Parbat** | **8,126 m** | Great Himalayas | Gilgit-Baltistan (PoK) | Western anchor of the Himalayas. |
| **Nanda Devi** | **7,816 m** | Kumaon Himalayas | **Uttarakhand** | **Highest peak lying ENTIRELY within India**. |
| **Namcha Barwa** | **7,782 m** | Eastern Himalayas | Tibet / near Arunachal | Eastern anchor; Brahmaputra takes U-turn here. |
| **Kamet** | **7,756 m** | Garhwal Himalayas | Uttarakhand | — |
| **Saramati** | **3,826 m** | Naga Hills (Purvanchal) | Nagaland | Highest peak of Nagaland / Purvanchal. |
| **Anamudi** | **2,695 m** | **Anaimalai Hills** (Western Ghats) | **Kerala** | **Highest peak of Peninsular / South India**. |
| **Doda Betta** | **2,637 m** | **Nilgiri Hills** | **Tamil Nadu** | 2nd highest peak in South India; Nilgiris join W & E Ghats. |
| **Guru Shikhar** | **1,722 m** | **Aravalli Range** (Mt. Abu) | **Rajasthan** | **Highest peak of Aravalli** (oldest fold mountains). |
| **Jindhagada / Arma Konda** | **1,690 m / 1,680 m** | Eastern Ghats | **Andhra Pradesh** | Highest peak of Eastern Ghats *(SSC often asks **Mahendragiri**, 1,501 m, Odisha — remember both!)*. |
| **Mahendragiri** | **1,501 m** | Eastern Ghats | **Odisha** | Historically cited as highest peak of Eastern Ghats in NCERT/older SSC keys. |
| **Dhupgarh** | **1,350 m** | **Satpura Range** (Mahadeo Hills) | **Madhya Pradesh** | Highest peak of Satpura (near Pachmarhi). |
| **Parasnath** | **1,365 m** | Chota Nagpur Plateau | **Jharkhand** | Highest peak of Jharkhand; sacred Jain site. |
| **Saddle Peak** | **732 m** | North Andaman | **A&N Islands** | **Highest peak of Andaman & Nicobar Islands**. |

#### 2. The Northern Plains (Indo-Gangetic-Brahmaputra Plain)
Alluvial plains (~2,400 km long, 150–300 km wide) divided North-to-South into **4 relief belts**:

| Zone | Width | Composition & Drainage | Fertility & Land Use |
| :--- | :---: | :--- | :--- |
| **1. Bhabar** | 8–16 km *(Shiwalik foothills)* | **Coarse pebbles, boulders & gravel**; **streams disappear underground** due to porosity. | **Unfit for agriculture**; large trees with deep roots. |
| **2. Terai** | 15–30 km *(South of Bhabar)* | **Streams re-emerge** creating **marshy, swampy** conditions. | Rich wildlife (*Dudhwa, Kaziranga, Corbett*); cleared for rice, wheat, sugarcane. |
| **3. Bhangar** | Extensive terraces | **Older alluvium** above flood level; contains calcareous nodules (**Kankar**). | Moderately fertile. |
| **4. Khadar** | Along river floodplains | **Newer alluvium** replenished annually by floods; fine silt/clay (*called **Bet** in Punjab*). | **Most fertile**; ideal for intensive farming. |

- **Punjab Plains (5 Doabs — North to South):** **Sindh Sagar** (Indus–Jhelum), **Chaj** (Jhelum–Chenab), **Rachna** (Chenab–Ravi), **Bari** (Ravi–Beas), **Bist** (Beas–Sutlej).
- **Thar Desert (Marusthali):** West of Aravallis (Rajasthan); **Luni** is the only major river (inland drainage); **Sambhar Lake** (largest inland saline lake); **Indira Gandhi Canal** (from Harike Barrage) irrigates NW Rajasthan.

#### 3. The Peninsular Plateau
Oldest landmass (**Gondwanaland** fragment), triangular, avg height **600–900 m**, composed of ancient **crystalline igneous & metamorphic rocks**; slopes **West → East**. Divided by **Narmada River** into **Central Highlands** and **Deccan Plateau**:

- **Malwa Plateau:** Between Aravallis & Vindhyas; drained by **Chambal, Betwa, Ken, Mahi**; covered by **Black Soil (Regur)**; famous for **Chambal Ravines (badlands)**.
- **Central Ranges:** **Aravalli** (*oldest fold / residual mountains*, Gujarat–Delhi Ridge); **Vindhya** (*North–South water divide*); **Satpura** (*Block mountain/Horst* between Narmada & Tapti rift valleys).
- **Chota Nagpur Plateau:** Jharkhand & adjoining states; **"Ruhr / Mineral Heartland of India"** (coal, iron, mica, bauxite); drained by **Damodar, Subarnarekha**; **Hundru Falls** (Subarnarekha); **Parasnath Hill**.
- **Deccan Plateau:** South of Narmada; **Deccan Trap** (NW Maharashtra/MP/Gujarat) made of Cretaceous fissure **basalt lava** yielding **Black Cotton Soil (Regur)**.
- **Meghalaya / Karbi-Anglong Plateau:** NE extension (**Garo, Khasi, Jaintia Hills**), separated from Chota Nagpur by **Malda Gap / Fault**.

**Western Ghats vs Eastern Ghats:**

| Feature | Western Ghats (Sahyadri) | Eastern Ghats |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Continuity** | **Continuous** wall; crossed only via passes (*Thal, Bhor, Pal, Shencottah*). | **Discontinuous**, eroded by Mahanadi, Godavari, Krishna, Kaveri. |
| **Avg. Elevation** | **900 – 1,600 m** (increases North → South). | **~600 m** (lower). |
| **Highest Peak** | **Anamudi (2,695 m)** *(Kerala)*; **Doda Betta (2,637 m)** *(Nilgiris)*. | **Jindhagada (1,690 m)** / **Arma Konda (1,680 m)** *(AP)*; **Mahendragiri (1,501 m)** *(Odisha)*. |
| **Meeting Point** | **Nilgiri Hills** (Tri-junction of TN, Kerala, Karnataka). | Meet Western Ghats at **Nilgiris**. |
| **Local Names** | Sahyadri (MH/KA), Nilgiris (TN), Anaimalai & Cardamom Hills (KL/TN). | Nallamala, Palkonda, Velikonda (AP); Javadi, Shevaroy (TN). |

#### 4. The Coastal Plains

| Coast | Location / Divisions | Key Characteristics & Exam Facts |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Western Coastal Plain** | Narrow (~50 km), **submerged** coast; **Estuaries** (no deltas); ideal for **natural ports** (*Mumbai, Kandla, Mormugao, Kochi*). | 1. **Kutch & Kathiawar** (Gujarat)<br>2. **Konkan** (Daman/Maharashtra–Goa)<br>3. **Kanara** (Karnataka)<br>4. **Malabar** (Kerala — famous for **Kayals / Backwaters**, e.g., *Vembanad, Ashtamudi*). |
| **Eastern Coastal Plain** | Broad (~100–120 km), **emergent** coast; huge fertile **Deltas**; fewer natural harbors. | 1. **Utkal** (Odisha — *Chilika Lake*, Mahanadi delta)<br>2. **Northern Circars** (Mahanadi to Krishna, Odisha/AP)<br>3. **Coromandel** (Krishna delta to Kanyakumari, AP/TN — gets **NE Monsoon winter rain**). |

### 9. Indian Rivers - Complete

#### Himalayan vs Peninsular Rivers — Quick Contrast
- **Himalayan Rivers:** Perennial (rain + snowmelt), antecedent, deep V-shaped valleys/gorges, shifting meanders in plains, huge deltas.
- **Peninsular Rivers:** Seasonal (monsoon-fed, *except Kaveri*), fixed shallow valleys, graded profile, east-flowing form deltas while west-flowing form estuaries.

#### Master Table: Major Indian Rivers (Himalayan & Peninsular)

| River *(Vedic Name)* | Source | Mouth | Length (km) | Major Tributaries *(L = Left, R = Right)* | States / UTs in India | Major Dams / Projects |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :---: | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Indus** *(Sindhu)* | Bokhar Chu Glacier, near Mansarovar (Tibet) | Arabian Sea (near Karachi) | **2,880** *(1,114 in India)* | **L:** Jhelum, Chenab, Ravi, Beas, Sutlej (*Panjnad*)<br>**R:** Shyok, Gilgit, Kabul | **Ladakh (UT)** | Tarbela, Mangla (Pak); Nimoo Bazgo (Ladakh) |
| **Jhelum** *(Vitasta)* | **Verinag Spring** (Pir Panjal, J&K) | Chenab (Trimmu, Pak) | **725** | Kishanganga (Neelum), Lidder, Pohru, Sind | **J&K (UT)** | **Uri, Tulbul, Kishanganga** |
| **Chenab** *(Askini)* | **Baralacha La** (Chandra + Bhaga at Tandi, HP) | Indus (Mithankot, Pak via Panjnad) | **960 – 1,180** | **Largest Indus tributary**; Marusudar, Miyar, Tawi | **HP, J&K (UT)** | **Baglihar, Salal, Dulhasti, Ratle** |

| **Beas** *(Vipasa)* | **Beas Kund** near Rohtang Pass (HP) | **Sutlej at Harike** (Punjab) | **470** | Parbati, Sainj, Tirthan, Uhl | **HP, Punjab** *(100% in India)* | **Pong (Maharana Pratap Sagar), Pandoh, Harike Barrage** |
| **Sutlej** *(Shutudri)* | **Rakas Lake (Rakshastal)** (Tibet); enters India at **Shipki La** | Chenab / Panjnad (Pak) | **1,450** *(1,050 in India)* | **Beas**, Spiti, Baspa | **HP, Punjab** | **Bhakra-Nangal** *(Govind Sagar — highest gravity dam, 226 m)*, **Nathpa Jhakri, Koldam** |
| **Ganga** | **Devprayag** (Bhagirathi from **Gaumukh/Gangotri** + Alaknanda from Satopanth) | **Bay of Bengal** (via Padma/Meghna; **Sundarbans Delta**) | **2,525** *(India's longest)* | **L:** Ramganga, Gomti, Ghaghara, Gandak, Kosi, Mahananda<br>**R:** Yamuna, Tons, Son, Punpun *(Hooghly gets Damodar)* | **Uttarakhand, UP** *(longest 1,450 km)*, **Bihar, Jharkhand, WB** | **Tehri Dam** *(on Bhagirathi, India's tallest dam — 260.5 m)*, **Farakka Barrage** |
| **Yamuna** | **Yamunotri Glacier** (Bandarpunch, UK) | **Ganga at Prayagraj** (Triveni Sangam) | **1,376** *(longest Ganga trib.)* | **L:** Hindon<br>**R:** Tons, **Chambal**, Sindh, **Betwa**, **Ken** | **UK, HP, Haryana, Delhi, UP** | **Lakhwar, Hathnikund Barrage** *(Chambal: Gandhi Sagar, Rana Pratap Sagar, Jawahar Sagar)* |

| **Ghaghara** *(Karnali)* | **Mapchachungo Glacier** (Tibet) | Ganga (Chhapra, Bihar) | **1,080** | **Sarda (Kali)**, Rapti, Sarju *(largest Ganga trib. by volume)* | **UP, Bihar** | Karnali / Girijapuri Barrage |
| **Gandak** *(Narayani)* | Nepal Himalayas (Dhaulagiri–Everest) | Ganga (Sonpur/Hajipur, Bihar) | **630** | Kali Gandaki, Trisuli, Budhi Gandak | **Bihar** *(UP border)* | Valmikinagar (Gandak) Barrage |
| **Kosi** | Tibet / Nepal (Saptakoshi: Sun Kosi, Arun, Tamur) | Ganga (Kursela, Bihar) | **720** | Arun, Sun Kosi, Tamur, Kamla | **Bihar** | **Kosi Barrage** (Bhimnagar) |

| **Narmada** | **Amarkantak Plateau** (Anuppur, MP) | **Gulf of Khambhat (Arabian Sea)** *(Estuary)* | **1,312** *(longest West-flowing)* | **Tawa** *(largest)*, Hiran, Barna, Kolar, Shakkar *(flows in **Rift Valley** between Vindhya & Satpura)* | **MP, MH (border), Gujarat** | **Sardar Sarovar** (GJ), **Indira Sagar** (MP), Omkareshwar, Bargi |
| **Tapti (Tapi)** | **Multai** (Betul, Satpura Range, MP) | **Gulf of Khambhat (Arabian Sea)** near **Surat** *(Estuary)* | **724** | **Purna** *(largest)*, Girna, Panjhra, Aner *(flows in **Rift Valley** south of Satpura)* | **MP, MH, Gujarat** | **Ukai Dam** (Vallabh Sagar), **Kakrapar Dam** |

#### Crucial River System Facts (SSC Goldmine)
- **Panch Prayag (North → South along Alaknanda):**
  1. **Vishnuprayag:** Alaknanda + **Dhauliganga**
  2. **Nandaprayag:** Alaknanda + **Nandakini**
  3. **Karnaprayag:** Alaknanda + **Pindar**
  4. **Rudraprayag:** Alaknanda + **Mandakini**
  5. **Devprayag:** Alaknanda + **Bhagirathi** = **Ganga**
- **Indus Waters Treaty (1960, brokered by World Bank):** **Western Rivers (Indus, Jhelum, Chenab)** → **Pakistan** (~80% total water); **Eastern Rivers (Ravi, Beas, Sutlej)** → **India** (~20%).
- **Brahmaputra Names:** **Yarlung Tsangpo** (Tibet) → **Siang / Dihang** (Arunachal) → **Brahmaputra** (Assam) → **Jamuna** (Bangladesh) → **Meghna** (joins Padma before sea). Forms **Majuli (Assam)** — **world's largest river island**.
- **Other Key West-Flowing / Peninsular Rivers:** **Mahi** (crosses Tropic of Cancer twice), **Sabarmati** (Ahmedabad), **Luni** (inland drainage, Rann of Kutch), **Mandovi & Zuari** (Goa — *Dudhsagar Falls*), **Sharavati** (*Jog / Gersoppa Falls*, Karnataka), **Periyar** (Kerala — *Idukki Dam*), **Damodar** (joins Hooghly; *DVC dams: Tilaiya, Konar, Maithon, Panchet*).

#### Interstate River Water Disputes
- **Constitutional Provision:** **Article 262** empowers Parliament to adjudicate interstate river water disputes (barring Supreme Court jurisdiction) → **Inter-State River Water Disputes Act, 1956** & **River Boards Act, 1956**. Water = *State List (Entry 17)*; Interstate rivers = *Union List (Entry 56)*.

| River / Dispute | Conflicting States / UTs | Key Tribunal / Issue |

| **Krishna** | **Maharashtra, Karnataka, Telangana, Andhra Pradesh** | KWDT-I (Bachawat) & KWDT-II (Brijesh Kumar) |
| **Godavari** | **Maharashtra, Telangana, AP, Odisha, MP (Chhattisgarh)** | GWDT (1969); Polavaram Project submergence |
| **Ravi–Beas–Sutlej** | **Punjab, Haryana, Rajasthan** | **Sutlej–Yamuna Link (SYL) Canal**; Eradi Tribunal (1986) |
| **Narmada** | **Madhya Pradesh, Gujarat, Maharashtra, Rajasthan** | NWDT (1969); Sardar Sarovar dam height |
| **Mahadayi (Mandovi)** | **Goa, Karnataka, Maharashtra** | Kalasa–Banduri water diversion project |
| **Mahanadi** | **Odisha, Chhattisgarh** | Upstream barrages by Chhattisgarh |
| **Vansadhara** | **Odisha, Andhra Pradesh** | Neradi Barrage |
| **Mullaperiyar Dam** | **Tamil Nadu & Kerala** *(on Periyar River)* | Dam located in Kerala, operated/leased by Tamil Nadu |

### 10. Climate of India

India has a **Tropical Monsoon Climate** (*Monsoon* derived from Arabic word ***Mausim*** = season).

#### Monsoon Mechanism (SW & NE Monsoons)

| Aspect | South-West (SW) Monsoon *(Advancing)* | North-East (NE) Monsoon *(Retreating)* |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Duration** | **June to September** | **October to December** |
| **Pressure & Wind** | Intense summer heating over NW India & Tibetan Plateau creates deep **Low Pressure**; **ITCZ** shifts north (~20°–25°N). **SE Trade Winds** from **Mascarene High** (S. Indian Ocean) cross Equator and deflect **right** (Coriolis Force / Ferrel's Law) → **SW Monsoon**. | High pressure builds over cooled North India landmass; ITCZ shifts south. Winds blow **land → sea (NE to SW)**. |
| **Share of Rainfall** | **~75% – 80%** of India's total annual rainfall. | Brings winter rain to **Coromandel Coast (Tamil Nadu, coastal AP)** after picking up moisture over Bay of Bengal (~50–60% of TN's annual rain). |
| **Onset & Branches** | Strikes **Kerala (~1 June — Burst of Monsoon)**; covers India by mid-July.<br>1. **Arabian Sea Branch** *(stronger)*: Western Ghats orographic rain; misses Rajasthan (parallel to Aravallis).<br>2. **Bay of Bengal Branch**: Hits Myanmar/NE India (*Mawsynram*); moves East → West up Ganga Plain (rain decreases East → West). | Marked by **October Heat** (clear skies, high day temp & oppressive humidity); frequent **tropical cyclones** in Bay of Bengal hitting Odisha, AP & TN coasts. |

#### Seasons of India (IMD Classification)

| Season | Months | Key Characteristics & Exam Facts |
| :--- | :---: | :--- |
| **1. Cold Weather (Winter)** | **Mid-Dec – Feb** *(Jan coldest)* | Clear skies, low humidity, weak NE trade winds. **Western Disturbances** (from **Mediterranean Sea**) bring rain in NW plains (*Mahawat* in Rajasthan) & snow in Himalayas — **crucial for Rabi crops (Wheat)**. |
| **2. Hot Weather (Summer)** | **March – May** | Temp rises 38°C–48°C in NW plains. Local pre-monsoon storms:<br>• **Loo:** Hot, dry dusty winds in North India.<br>• **Kalbaisakhi / Nor'westers:** Violent evening thunderstorms in WB & Assam (*Bardoli Chheerha* in Assam — helps tea, jute, rice).<br>• **Mango Showers:** Pre-monsoon rain in Kerala & coastal Karnataka (ripens mangoes).<br>• **Blossom Showers (Cherry Blossom):** Kerala & Karnataka (aids **coffee** flowering). |
| **3. Advancing Monsoon (Rainy)** | **June – September** | **SW Monsoon**; max rainfall in July–August; *"Breaks in Monsoon"* occur when monsoon trough shifts to Himalayan foothills. |
| **4. Retreating Monsoon (Transition)** | **October – November/Dec** | Monsoon withdraws North → South; **October Heat**; Bay of Bengal cyclones; **NE Monsoon** rains in Tamil Nadu. |

#### Rainfall Distribution & Rain Shadow Areas
- **India's Average Annual Rainfall:** **~118 – 125 cm**.
- **Heavy Rainfall (>200 cm):** Windward slopes of **Western Ghats**, Meghalaya, Assam, Arunachal Pradesh, Sikkim, Sub-Himalayan WB.
- **Moderate Rainfall (100–200 cm):** Middle/Lower Ganga valley, Bihar, Jharkhand, Odisha, Chhattisgarh, E. MP, coastal AP & TN.
- **Low Rainfall (50–100 cm):** Upper Ganga plain, Punjab, Haryana, E. Rajasthan, interior Deccan.
- **Scanty Rainfall (<50 cm):** Western Rajasthan (Thar), Kutch, **Leh–Ladakh**, & **Rain Shadow Regions**.
- **Major Rain Shadow Areas (Leeward Side of Mountains):**
  1. **Interior Deccan Plateau (Marathwada, Vidarbha, Rayalaseema, Interior Karnataka):** Leeward side of **Western Ghats** *(Mahabaleshwar gets >600 cm, Pune just ~65 km east gets ~70 cm)*.
  2. **Tamil Nadu Coast (during SW Monsoon):** Lies parallel/leeward to Western Ghats.
  3. **Shillong (Meghalaya):** Leeward side of **Khasi Hills** (~200 cm vs Mawsynram's ~1,187 cm just ~56 km away).
  4. **Leh / Ladakh:** Rain shadow of the **Greater Himalayas** (cold desert).

#### Climate Records of India

| Record | Place | Value / Exam Note |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Wettest Place (India & World)** | **Mawsynram** *(East Khasi Hills, Meghalaya)* | Avg ~**11,872 mm** (~1,187 cm)/yr; funnel-shaped Khasi Hills trap Bay of Bengal moisture; followed by **Cherrapunji (Sohra)**. |
| **Driest Place** | **Leh (Ladakh)** & **Jaisalmer / Ruyli (Rajasthan)** | **Leh** (~10 cm/yr — cold desert) is overall driest station; **Jaisalmer / Ruyli** (~10–16 cm/yr) in hot Thar Desert. |
| **Hottest Place** | **Phalodi / Churu / Sri Ganganagar (Rajasthan)** | **Phalodi** recorded India's highest official IMD temp (**51.0°C**, May 2016); Churu & Sri Ganganagar regularly exceed 48–50°C. |
| **Coldest Inhabited Place** | **Dras** *(Kargil district, Ladakh)* | Drops below **-40°C to -45°C**; *"Gateway to Ladakh"*; 2nd coldest inhabited place globally after Siberia *(Siachen Glacier drops below -50°C)*. |

#### El Niño, La Niña & Indian Ocean Dipole (IOD)

| Phenomenon | Ocean–Atmosphere Condition | Effect on Indian SW Monsoon |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **El Niño** *("Little Boy")* | Abnormal **warming** of Eastern & Central Equatorial **Pacific Ocean** (off Peru coast); Trade Winds & Walker Circulation weaken. | High pressure over Western Pacific/Indian Ocean → **WEAKENS SW Monsoon → Drought / Deficient Rain** in India. |
| **La Niña** *("Little Girl")* | Abnormal **cooling** of Eastern & Central Equatorial **Pacific Ocean**; strong Trade Winds. | Strong Low Pressure over Western Pacific/Indian Ocean → **STRENGTHENS SW Monsoon → Excess Rain / Floods** in India. |
| **Indian Ocean Dipole (IOD)** | Sea-surface temperature difference between Western Indian Ocean (Arabian Sea) & Eastern Indian Ocean (Indonesia). | **Positive IOD** (warmer Arabian Sea) **boosts SW Monsoon** (can offset El Niño); **Negative IOD** weakens monsoon. |

#### Jet Streams & Their Role in Indian Climate
Jet streams are fast-moving (~150–300+ km/h), narrow bands of upper-tropospheric winds (~9–14 km altitude):
1. **Subtropical Westerly Jet Stream (STWJ):**
   - **Winter:** Blows **south of the Himalayas** (~27°–30° N) over North India → steers **Western Disturbances** from the Mediterranean Sea into NW India (causing winter rains & Himalayan snow) and maintains winter high pressure.
   - **Summer (May–June):** Shifts **north of the Himalayas / Tibetan Plateau** → its abrupt northward withdrawal triggers the **burst of the SW Monsoon**.
2. **Tropical Easterly Jet Stream (TEJ):**
   - Forms in **summer only** (~14°–15° N over Peninsular India) due to intense heating of the **Tibetan Plateau** (creating the Tibetan Anticyclone). Steers tropical depressions from the Bay of Bengal across India and strengthens the SW Monsoon.
3. **Somali Jet (Findlater Jet):** Low-level cross-equatorial summer jet stream off East Africa/Somalia that accelerates moisture flow in the Arabian Sea branch toward India's west coast.

> | **Elephant Reserves** | **33** (across 14 states) | **Singhbhum ER** (Jharkhand, **2001**) | **Singhbhum ER** (Jharkhand) | **Singphan ER** (Nagaland, **2018**) | **Assam & Tamil Nadu (5 each)** |
> | **Biosphere Reserves** | **18** (**13** in UNESCO MAB; 12 until 2024 + Cold Desert in 2025) | **Nilgiri BR** (**1986**; 1st UNESCO MAB in **2000**) | **Rann of Kutch BR** (Gujarat, ~12,454 sq km) | **Dibru-Saikhowa BR** (Assam, ~765 sq km) | **Madhya Pradesh (3)** |
> | **Ramsar Wetland Sites** | **103** *(as of Oct 2026)* | **Chilika Lake** (Odisha) & **Keoladeo NP** (Rajasthan), **Oct 1, 1981** | **Sundarban Wetland** (West Bengal, 4,230 sq km) | **Renuka Wetland** (HP) & **Vembannur Wetland Complex** (TN) — tied at **20 ha** | **Tamil Nadu (20)**, followed by **Uttar Pradesh (14)** |

---

## National Parks of India (Complete List)

### Key Facts for SSC Exams
* **Legal Basis:** Notified by State Governments under **Section 35** of the **Wildlife (Protection) Act, 1972**. Grazing of livestock and private land rights are **strictly prohibited** inside a National Park (unlike a Wildlife Sanctuary).
* **Total Count Nuance (106 vs. 107–108):**
  * The **Wildlife Institute of India (WII)** National Wildlife Database maintains the central baseline at **106 National Parks** (covering ~44,402.95 sq km, or ~1.35% of India's geographical area).
  * **Sikhna Jwhwlao National Park** (Kokrajhar & Chirang, **Assam**) was notified by the Assam Government in **March 2025** as Assam's **8th National Park**.
  * **Similipal** (**Odisha**) had its core area (845.70 sq km) formally notified under Section 35 by Odisha in **April 2025** (often cited in 2025–26 current affairs as India's **107th** final notification).
* **State with NO National Park:** **Punjab** is the **only state in India without any National Park** (a high-frequency SSC PYQ). Among Union Territories, **Chandigarh, Delhi, Lakshadweep, Puducherry, and Dadra & Nagar Haveli and Daman & Diu** have zero National Parks.
* **First Marine National Park in India:** **Marine National Park, Gulf of Kachchh** (Gujarat, **1982**).
* **Only Floating National Park in the World:** **Keibul Lamjao National Park** (Manipur), floating on biomass mats called ***phumdis*** in **Loktak Lake**.
* **UNESCO World Heritage Natural/Mixed Sites (National Parks):**
  * **Kaziranga NP** (1985), **Manas NP** (1985), **Keoladeo NP** (1985), **Sundarbans NP** (1987), **Nanda Devi & Valley of Flowers NPs** (1988 / extended 2005), **Great Himalayan NP** (2014), and **Khangchendzonga NP** (2016 — **India's only Mixed World Heritage Site**, both Natural & Cultural).
* **Name Changes to Remember:**
  * **Hailey National Park** (1936) → **Ramganga NP** (1954–55) → **Jim Corbett NP** (1957).
  * **Mount Harriet National Park** (A&N Islands) → renamed **Mount Manipur National Park** (**2021**, honoring Manipuri freedom fighters exiled there after the 1891 Anglo-Manipur War).
  * **Orang NP** (Assam) → officially **Orang National Park** (renamed from Rajiv Gandhi Orang NP in 2021).

### High-Yield Exam Spotlight: Top SSC National Park Facts
* **Highest Tiger Density:** Bandhavgarh NP (MP). **Highest Tiger Population (Total Count):** Jim Corbett NP (UK — 260 tigers in 2022).
* **Madhya Pradesh (11 NPs)** is called the **"Tiger State"** and also holds the maximum National Parks in India.
* **Two Pench NPs:** **Pench (Indira Priyadarshini)** is in **MP**, and **Pench (Jawaharlal Nehru)** is in **Maharashtra** — both share the same forest landscape across the state border.
* **Fossil Parks:** **Mandla Plant Fossils NP** and **Dinosaur Fossils NP** are both in **Madhya Pradesh**; **Akal Wood Fossil Park** is inside **Desert NP (Rajasthan)**.
* **Rhino Strongholds:** Kaziranga (Assam — #1), Jaldapara (WB — #2), Orang (Assam), Pobitora WLS (Assam — highest rhino density), Dudhwa (UP — reintroduced).

---

## Wildlife Sanctuaries (Major Ones)

* **Legal Basis:** Notified under **Section 18 / 26A** of the **Wildlife (Protection) Act, 1972**. Unlike National Parks, limited human activities (such as regulated grazing, firewood collection, or traditional community rights) may be permitted by the Chief Wildlife Warden.
* **Total Count:** **573+** Wildlife Sanctuaries across India.
* **Largest Wildlife Sanctuary:** **Kutch Desert Wildlife Sanctuary** (Gujarat, ~7,506.22 sq km — famous for *"Flamingo City"*).
* **Smallest Wildlife Sanctuary:** **Pitti Island (Pakshipitti)** (Lakshadweep, ~0.01 sq km — pelagic seabird breeding site).

## Tiger Reserves of India

### Project Tiger & NTCA — Key Exam Facts
* **Project Tiger Launch:** **April 1, 1973**, from **Jim Corbett National Park** (Uttarakhand) by Prime Minister **Indira Gandhi**.
* **First Director of Project Tiger:** **Kailash Sankhala**, famously known as the **"Tiger Man of India"**.
* **Initial 9 Tiger Reserves (1973–74):** **Bandipur, Corbett, Kanha, Manas, Melghat, Palamau, Ranthambore, Similipal, and Sundarban**.
* **Statutory Body:** **National Tiger Conservation Authority (NTCA)** — constituted in **2006** (under **Section 38L** of the Wildlife Protection Amendment Act, 2006). Chaired by the **Union Minister of Environment, Forest and Climate Change**.
* **Statutory Notification of Reserves:** Reserves are notified by State Governments on NTCA recommendation under **Section 38V** of the Wildlife Protection Act, 1972 (dividing each reserve into **Core / Critical Tiger Habitat** and **Buffer Zone**).
* **Total Tiger Reserves:** **58 Tiger Reserves** across **18 states** (latest additions: 55th Dholpur-Karauli 2023, 56th Guru Ghasidas–Tamor Pingla 2024, 57th Ratapani 2024, 58th Madhav 2025).
* **Key Extremes:**
  * **Largest TR by Area:** **Nagarjunasagar-Srisailam** (Andhra Pradesh; total ~3,296 sq km).
  * **Smallest TR:** **Orang** (Assam) is smallest by total area (~492.46 sq km) and CTH/core area (~79.28 sq km); **Bor** (Maharashtra) has the smallest standalone wildlife-sanctuary core (~138.12 sq km; total ~816 sq km).
  * **State with Most Tiger Reserves:** **Madhya Pradesh (9)**, followed by **Maharashtra (6)** and **Karnataka / Rajasthan / Tamil Nadu (5 each)**.
  * **Highest-Altitude Tiger Reserve:** **Namdapha / Kamlang** (Arunachal Pradesh).
  * **Southernmost Tiger Reserve:** **Kalakad-Mundanthurai** (Tamil Nadu).
  * **Northernmost Tiger Reserve:** **Rajaji** (Uttarakhand).
  * **International Tiger Day:** **July 29** (established at the **2010 St. Petersburg Tiger Summit**, which launched the **TX2** goal to double wild tigers by 2022 — **Pilibhit TR** and **Sathyamangalam TR** won global TX2 awards).

### All India Tiger Estimation 2022 (5th Cycle Census Data)

* **Global Context:** India hosts roughly **75% of the world's wild tiger population**.
* **Total Tiger Population (2022):**
  * **Minimum Unique Camera-Trapped Estimate** (released April 9, 2023 by PM Modi in Mysuru): **3,167**.
  * **Final Average Population Estimate** (released July 29, 2023 by NTCA & WII): **3,682** (upper limit **3,925**; annual growth rate **~6.1%**).
* **Census Trend (Every 4 Years):** **2006:** 1,411 → **2010:** 1,706 → **2014:** 2,226 → **2018:** 2,967 → **2022:** **3,682**.

## Elephant Reserves of India

* **Project Elephant Launch:** **February 1992** (Centrally Sponsored Scheme by MoEFCC).
* **National Heritage Animal:** The **Indian Elephant** was declared India's National Heritage Animal in **2010** (based on the *Gajah* Task Force report).
* **World Elephant Day:** **August 12**.
* **Total Elephant Reserves:** **33 officially notified Elephant Reserves** across **14 elephant-range states**.
  * **First & Largest ER:** **Singhbhum ER** (Jharkhand, **2001**).
  * **Smallest ER:** **Singphan ER** (Nagaland, **2018**).
  * **Latest Additions:** **Lemru ER** (31st, Chhattisgarh, 2021), **Agasthiyamalai ER** (32nd, Tamil Nadu, 2022), **Terai ER** (33rd, Uttar Pradesh, 2022).
* **MIKE Programme (Monitoring the Illegal Killing of Elephants — CITES):** India has **10 MIKE sites** — Chirang-Ripu (AS), Dihing Patkai (AS), Eastern Dooars (WB), Deomali (AR), Garo Hills (ML), Mayurbhanj (OD), Shivalik (UK), Mysuru (KA), Nilgiri (TN), and Wayanad (KL).
* **Elephant Census Data:**
  * **2017 Census (Traditional):** ~27,312–29,964 elephants (Karnataka #1 with 6,049; Assam #2 with 5,719; Kerala #3 with 3,054).
  * **SAIEE 2021–25 (India's 1st DNA-Based Synchronous Census):** **22,446 wild elephants** (range: 18,255–26,645; not directly comparable to 2017 due to genetic SECR methodology). Top states: **Karnataka (6,013)** > **Assam (4,159)** > **Tamil Nadu (3,136)** > **Kerala (2,785)** > **Uttarakhand (1,792)** > **Odisha (912)**.

## Bird Sanctuaries

* **"Birdman of India":** **Dr. Salim Ali** (autobiography: *The Fall of a Sparrow*).
* **Classic SSC Trap (Salim Ali Sites):**
  * **Salim Ali Bird Sanctuary** → **Goa** (Chorao Island, Mandovi River).
  * **Salim Ali National Park (City Forest)** → **Jammu & Kashmir** (Srinagar).
  * **Thattekad (Salim Ali) Bird Sanctuary** → **Kerala** (Ernakulam, Periyar River).

| Sanctuary | State / UT | Famous For |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Keoladeo Ghana (Bharatpur)** | Rajasthan | **Largest/most famous bird sanctuary**; UNESCO WHS, Ramsar, Montreux Record; cranes, painted storks, pelicans |
| **Vedanthangal Bird Sanctuary** | Tamil Nadu | **Oldest waterbird sanctuary in India** (protected since 1798 / notified 1936); Ramsar site; pintails, garganey |

| **Koonthankulam Bird Sanctuary** | Tamil Nadu | Largest reserve for breeding resident & migratory waterbirds in South India; Ramsar site |

## Biosphere Reserves of India (Complete List - all 18)

* **Concept & Zoning:** Initiated under UNESCO's **Man and the Biosphere (MAB) Programme (1971)**. Each Biosphere Reserve has **3 zones**:
  1. **Core Zone:** Strictly protected (National Park/Sanctuary; zero human interference).
  2. **Buffer Zone:** Limited research, education, and tourism.
  3. **Transition Zone:** Sustainable human habitation, farming, and livelihoods.
* **Total in India:** **18 Biosphere Reserves** (all notified nationally by MoEFCC).
* **UNESCO MAB Recognition:**
  * **12 out of 18** were recognized up to 2024 (with **Panna** joining in **2020** as the 12th).
  * In **September 2025**, **Cold Desert Biosphere Reserve** (Himachal Pradesh) became **India's 13th UNESCO MAB Biosphere Reserve**.
  * **5 Non-UNESCO (National-Only) Biosphere Reserves:** **Manas**, **Dibru-Saikhowa**, **Dihang-Dibang**, **Rann of Kutch**, and **Seshachalam Hills**.

| 2 | **Nanda Devi** | Uttarakhand | **1988** | **Yes (2004)** | Snow leopard, Himalayan musk deer, bharal; includes Nanda Devi & Valley of Flowers NPs |

| 17 | **Seshachalam Hills** | Andhra Pradesh | **2010** | **No** *(National only)* | **Red Sanders** (*Pterocarpus santalinus*), **Slender Loris**, Tirupati Hills |
| 18 | **Panna** *(Latest BR)* | Madhya Pradesh | **2011** | **Yes (2020 — 12th)** | **Ken River**, Bengal tiger, gharial, dry deciduous teak forests |

## Ramsar Wetland Sites of India

### Essential Ramsar Facts for SSC
* **Ramsar Convention:** Signed on **February 2, 1971** in **Ramsar, Iran** (entered into force **1975**).
* **World Wetlands Day:** Celebrated every year on **February 2**.
* **India's Accession:** India joined the convention on **February 1, 1982** (first 2 sites designated on **October 1, 1981**: **Chilika Lake** and **Keoladeo National Park**).
* **Growth Milestones:** **75 sites** (Aug 2022, *Azadi Ka Amrit Mahotsav*) → **80 / 85 sites** (2024) → **96 sites** (end of 2025) → **100th site** (**Surha Tal / Jai Prakash Narayan BS**, UP, June 2026) → **101st** (**Glaw Lake**, Arunachal Pradesh, Aug 2026) → **102nd & 103rd** (**Sauj Jheel**, UP & **Sukhna Wetland**, Chandigarh, Oct 15, 2026).
* **Montreux Record** *(Register of Ramsar sites facing adverse ecological changes due to pollution, human interference, or siltation)*:
  * Currently **2 sites in India**: **Keoladeo National Park** (Rajasthan, added 1990) and **Loktak Lake** (Manipur, added 1993).
  * **Chilika Lake** (Odisha) was placed on the Montreux Record in **1993** due to siltation and **removed in 2002** after successful restoration (winning the Ramsar Wetland Conservation Award).
* **State Highlights:** **Tamil Nadu (20)** has the highest number, followed by **Uttar Pradesh (14)**. **Meghalaya, Nagaland, and Telangana** have **0** Ramsar sites.

## Important Conservation Facts

### 1. IUCN Red List Categories
* **IUCN** (International Union for Conservation of Nature): Founded in **1948**; Headquarters in **Gland, Switzerland**; publishes the **Red Data Book / Red List** (pink pages historically for critically endangered species, green pages for formerly endangered recovered species).
* **Total Categories:** **9 Categories** (3 of which — **CR, EN, VU** — constitute **"Threatened"** species):

| Category Code | Category Name | Definition | Indian Examples (Exam Focus) |
| :---: | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **EX** | **Extinct** | No reasonable doubt that the last individual has died | Indian / Asiatic Cheetah *(declared extinct in India in 1952; CR globally in Iran)*, Pink-headed Duck *(possibly EX)* |
| **EW** | **Extinct in the Wild** | Survives only in captivity, cultivation, or naturalized outside historic range | Hawaiian Crow, Scimitar-horned Oryx *(historically)* |
| **CR** | **Critically Endangered** *(Threatened)* | Extremely high risk of extinction in the wild (>80–90% decline in 10 yrs / 3 gen) | **Great Indian Bustard, Kashmir Stag (Hangul), Gharial, Pygmy Hog, Malabar Civet, White-rumped / Red-headed / Indian Vultures, Jerdon's Courser, Forest Owlet** |
| **EN** | **Endangered** *(Threatened)* | Very high risk of extinction in the wild | **Royal Bengal Tiger, Asian Elephant, Asiatic Lion, Lion-tailed Macaque, Nilgiri Tahr, Sangai, Red Panda, Gangetic River Dolphin, Golden Langur, Wild Water Buffalo** |
| **VU** | **Vulnerable** *(Threatened)* | High risk of extinction in the wild in medium-term future | **One-horned Rhinoceros, Snow Leopard, Clouded Leopard, Barasingha (Swamp Deer), Dugong, Olive Ridley Turtle, Sloth Bear, Indian Gaur, Nilgiri Langur** |
| **NT** | **Near Threatened** | Close to qualifying for a threatened category in near future | **Markhor, Black-necked Crane, Indian Wild Ass (*Khur*), Smooth-coated Otter** |
| **LC** | **Least Concern** | Widespread and abundant; low extinction risk | **Blackbuck, Chinkara, Nilgai, Indian Peafowl, Rhesus Macaque** |
| **DD** | **Data Deficient** | Inadequate information to assess extinction risk | Nicobar Tree Shrew *(historically)*, certain deep-sea rays & frogs |
| **NE** | **Not Evaluated** | Not yet assessed against IUCN criteria | Newly discovered insects, fungi, amphibians |

---

| **Asiatic Lion** *(India-restricted wild range)* | Dry deciduous forest of **Saurashtra, Gujarat** | Gir NP | **EN** |
| **Great Indian Bustard** | Arid & semi-arid grasslands (**Rajasthan, Gujarat, MH**) | Desert NP, Nannaj, Kutch Bustard | **CR** |
| **Purple Frog (*Nasikabatrachus sahyadrensis*)** | Burrowing amphibian of **Western Ghats** (KL, TN) | Idukki / Anamalai region | **NT / EN** |
| **Nicobar Megapode** | Coastal forests of **Nicobar Islands** | Campbell Bay & Galathea Bay NPs | **VU** |

| **Jerdon's Courser** | Scrub jungle of **Eastern Ghats (Andhra Pradesh)** | Sri Lankamalleswara WLS | **CR** |
| **Golden Langur** *(endemic to Bhutan–Assam belt)* | Foothills between **Manas & Sankosh rivers** (Assam) | Manas NP, Raimona NP, Chakrashila WLS | **EN** |
