### 3. Biodiversity Hotspots in India (4 out of 36 Globally)

* **Concept Coined By:** **Norman Myers (1988)**; recognized globally by **Conservation International**.
* **Two Strict Criteria to Qualify:**
  1. Must contain at least **1,500 species of vascular plants (>0.5% of world's total) as endemics**.
  2. Must have lost at least **70% of its original primary natural vegetation** (high threat).

| Biodiversity Hotspot | Indian States / Regions Covered | Key Endemic / Flagship Species |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **1. Himalaya** *(includes Eastern Himalayas)* | J&K, Ladakh, HP, Uttarakhand, Sikkim, Arunachal Pradesh, Darjeeling (WB), northern Assam | Snow Leopard, Red Panda, Hangul, Western Tragopan, Blue Poppy |
| **2. Western Ghats (and Sri Lanka)** | Gujarat, Maharashtra, Goa, Karnataka, Kerala, Tamil Nadu *(UNESCO WHS 2012)* | Lion-tailed Macaque, Nilgiri Tahr, Malabar Civet, Purple Frog, Neelakurinji |
| **3. Indo-Burma** | North-Eastern India south of Brahmaputra (Manipur, Mizoram, Tripura, Nagaland, Meghalaya, south Assam) + **Andaman Islands** | Sangai, Hoolock Gibbon, White-winged Wood Duck, freshwater turtles |
| **4. Sundaland** | **Nicobar Group of Islands** *(Note: Andaman = Indo-Burma; Nicobar = Sundaland!)* | Nicobar Megapode, Crab-eating Macaque, Leatherback Turtle |

---

### 4. Wildlife (Protection) Act, 1972 & National Board for Wildlife (NBWL)

* **Wildlife (Protection) Act, 1972 (WPA 1972):**
  * Enacted on **September 9, 1972** (provides legal framework for National Parks, Wildlife Sanctuaries, Conservation Reserves, and Community Reserves).
  * **Key Amendments:**
    * **2002 Amendment:** Created **National Board for Wildlife (NBWL)**, **Conservation Reserves**, and **Community Reserves**.
    * **2006 Amendment:** Created **NTCA** and **Wildlife Crime Control Bureau (WCCB — HQ: New Delhi)**.
    * **2022 Amendment (in force April 1, 2023):** Rationalized the **6 Schedules down to 4 Schedules** and incorporated **CITES**:
      * **Schedule I:** Animal species with the **highest level of protection** (maximum penalties).
      * **Schedule II:** Animal species with a **lesser degree of protection**.
      * **Schedule III:** **Protected Plant species** (previously Schedule VI).
      * **Schedule IV:** **Specimens listed in the Appendices under CITES** *(New addition; old Schedule V "Vermin" was **omitted**)*.
* **National Board for Wildlife (NBWL):**
  * **Statutory Body** under **Section 5A** of WPA, 1972 (replacing the non-statutory Indian Board for Wildlife set up in 1952).
  * **Composition:** **47 members**.
  * **Chairperson:** **Prime Minister of India** *(High-frequency SSC question!)*.
  * **Vice-Chairperson:** **Union Minister of Environment, Forest and Climate Change**.
  * **Role:** Apex advisory body; approval of the Standing Committee of NBWL is mandatory for altering boundaries of National Parks/Sanctuaries or executing projects inside Protected Areas and Eco-Sensitive Zones (ESZs).
* **Other Key Environmental Bodies & Acts (Quick Revision):**
  * **Central Zoo Authority (CZA):** Statutory body under WPA 1972 (1991 amendment); HQ: New Delhi.
  * **National Biodiversity Authority (NBA):** Statutory body under **Biological Diversity Act, 2002**; HQ: **Chennai**.
  * **Forest (Conservation) Act, 1980** | **Environment (Protection) Act, 1986** *(enacted after Bhopal Gas Tragedy 1984)* | **National Green Tribunal (NGT) Act, 2010** *(HQ: New Delhi)*.

---

### 5. CITES (Convention on International Trade in Endangered Species of Wild Fauna and Flora)

* Also known as the **Washington Convention** (signed **March 3, 1973** in Washington, D.C.).
* **Entered into Force:** **July 1, 1975** (Legally binding international agreement; **World Wildlife Day** is observed on **March 3** to mark its signing).
* **India Joined CITES:** **October 18, 1976** (18th/25th party).
* **CITES Secretariat:** Administered by **UNEP**; located in **Geneva, Switzerland**.
* **Three Appendices:**
  * **Appendix I:** Species threatened with **extinction** — **commercial international trade is completely prohibited** (e.g., Tiger, Asiatic Lion, One-horned Rhino, Snow Leopard).
  * **Appendix II:** Species not necessarily threatened now, but **trade must be strictly regulated** via export permits to prevent overexploitation (e.g., Indian Star Tortoise moved to App I in 2019; many corals, orchids, Red Sanders).
  * **Appendix III:** Species protected in **at least one country**, which asks other CITES Parties for assistance in controlling trade.

---

## 1. Dams and Multipurpose River Valley Projects

### 1.1 Complete Dam Table (70+ Major Dams of India)

| Dam / Project | River | State / UT | Purpose | Notable Facts |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Tehri Dam** | Bhagirathi | Uttarakhand | Hydroelectricity (1,000 MW), Irrigation, Drinking Water | **Highest dam in India** (260.5 m); earth and rock-fill embankment dam; Swami Ram Tirtha Sagar reservoir. |
| **Bhakra Dam** | Sutlej | Himachal Pradesh (Bilaspur) / Punjab border | Irrigation, Hydroelectricity (1,325 MW), Flood Control | **Highest concrete gravity dam in India** (226 m; 2nd tallest dam overall); reservoir is **Gobind Sagar**. |
| **Nangal Dam** | Sutlej | Punjab (Rupnagar) | Irrigation, Power Canal Diversion | Located ~13 km downstream of Bhakra; forms part of the Bhakra-Nangal Project. |
| **Hirakud Dam** | Mahanadi | Odisha (Sambalpur) | Flood Control, Irrigation, Hydroelectricity (~347.5 MW) | **Longest dam in India / world** (total length **25.79 km** incl. dykes; main dam **4.8 km**); built in 1957. |
| **Nagarjuna Sagar Dam** | Krishna | Telangana / Andhra Pradesh border | Irrigation, Hydroelectricity (815.6 MW) | **World's largest masonry dam** (height 124 m); stone masonry gravity structure; foundation laid in 1955. |
| **Sardar Sarovar Dam** | Narmada | Gujarat (Narmada district) | Irrigation, Hydroelectricity (1,450 MW), Drinking Water | Terminal dam of Narmada Valley Project; benefits Gujarat, MP, Maharashtra, Rajasthan; Statue of Unity is nearby. |
| **Indira Sagar Dam** | Narmada | Madhya Pradesh (Khandwa) | Hydroelectricity (1,000 MW), Irrigation | Holds **India's largest reservoir by water storage capacity** (12.22 billion m³); also called Punasa Dam. |
| **Mettur Dam** | Kaveri (Cauvery) | Tamil Nadu (Salem) | Irrigation, Hydroelectricity | Forms **Stanley Reservoir**; built in 1934; one of India's oldest and largest masonry dams. |
| **Kallanai (Grand Anicut)** | Kaveri (Cauvery) | Tamil Nadu (Thanjavur / Tiruchirappalli) | Irrigation Diversion | **Oldest water-diversion structure in the world still in use**; originally built by Chola king Karikala (~2nd century CE). |
| **Tungabhadra Dam** | Tungabhadra (tributary of Krishna) | Karnataka (Vijayanagara / Ballari) | Irrigation, Hydroelectricity, Flood Control | Joint project of **Karnataka & Andhra Pradesh**; reservoir is **Pampa Sagar**. |
| **Koyna Dam** | Koyna (tributary of Krishna) | Maharashtra (Satara) | Hydroelectricity (1,960 MW), Irrigation | Largest completed hydroelectric project in Maharashtra; rubble-concrete dam; linked to 1967 reservoir-induced earthquake. |
| **Idukki Dam** | Periyar | Kerala (Idukki) | Hydroelectricity (780 MW) | **India's first and highest double-curvature parabolic arch dam** (168.9 m); located between Kuravan and Kurathi hills. |
| **Banasura Sagar Dam** | Karamanathodu (tributary of Kabini) | Kerala (Wayanad) | Irrigation, Drinking Water, Hydel Support | **Largest earthen dam in India** (2nd largest in Asia); hosts India's first large-scale floating solar plant. |
| **Mullaperiyar Dam** | Periyar | Kerala (Idukki) | Irrigation, Power (for Tamil Nadu) | Masonry gravity dam (1895); situated in Kerala but **operated and leased by Tamil Nadu** (999-year lease). |
| **Farakka Barrage** | Ganga | West Bengal (Murshidabad) | Navigation, Flushing Silt | Diverts Ganga water via feeder canal into Bhagirathi-Hooghly to keep **Kolkata Port** navigable (commissioned 1975). |
| **Tilaiya Dam** | Barakar (Damodar basin) | Jharkhand (Koderma) | Flood Control, Irrigation, Hydel | **First dam completed by DVC** (1953); concrete gravity dam. |
| **Konar Dam** | Konar (Damodar basin) | Jharkhand (Hazaribagh) | Flood Control, Cooling Water, Irrigation | Second DVC dam (1955); composite earth and concrete dam; supplies cooling water to Bokaro Thermal Power Plant. |
| **Maithon Dam** | Barakar (Damodar basin) | Jharkhand (Dhanbad) / West Bengal border | Flood Control, Hydroelectricity | DVC dam (1957); features **India's first underground hydroelectric power station**. |
| **Panchet Dam** | Damodar | Jharkhand (Dhanbad) / West Bengal (Purulia) | Flood Control, Hydroelectricity | Last of the four original DVC dams (1959); earthen/concrete composite dam. |
| **Durgapur Barrage** | Damodar | West Bengal (Paschim Bardhaman) | Irrigation, Industrial Water Supply | Part of DVC network (1955); supplies water to Durgapur industrial belt and Damodar canals. |
| **Rihand Dam** | Rihand (tributary of Son) | Uttar Pradesh (Sonbhadra) | Hydroelectricity (300 MW), Cooling, Irrigation | Forms **Govind Ballabh Pant Sagar** (India's largest artificial lake by volume; UP–MP border). |
| **Almatti Dam** | Krishna | Karnataka (Vijayapura) | Irrigation, Hydroelectricity | Officially **Lal Bahadur Shastri Dam**; main reservoir of the **Upper Krishna Project**. |
| **Narayanpur Dam** | Krishna | Karnataka (Yadgir) | Irrigation | Officially **Basava Sagar Dam**; located downstream of Almatti under Upper Krishna Project. |
| **Krishna Raja Sagara (KRS)** | Kaveri | Karnataka (Mandya) | Irrigation, Drinking Water (Bengaluru/Mysuru) | Engineered by **Sir M. Visvesvaraya** (1932); houses the famous **Brindavan Gardens**. |
| **Srisailam Dam** | Krishna | Andhra Pradesh / Telangana border | Hydroelectricity (1,670 MW), Irrigation | **2nd largest capacity working hydroelectric station in India**; situated in Nallamala Hills. |
| **Upper Siang Project** | Siang (Brahmaputra) | Arunachal Pradesh | Hydroelectricity (~11,000–11,200 MW), Flood Moderation | Proposed mega multipurpose project; designed to counter upstream Brahmaputra diversions and regulate floods. |
| **Subansiri Lower HEP** | Subansiri | Arunachal Pradesh / Assam border | Hydroelectricity (2,000 MW) | **India's largest hydroelectric project by NHPC** (8 × 250 MW); concrete gravity dam. |
| **Dibang Multipurpose Project** | Dibang | Arunachal Pradesh (Lower Dibang Valley) | Hydroelectricity (2,880 MW), Flood Control | Planned **278 m concrete gravity dam**—set to become **India's tallest dam** upon completion. |
| **Ranganadi Dam** | Ranganadi (Panyor) | Arunachal Pradesh (Lower Subansiri) | Hydroelectricity (405 MW) | Operated by NEEPCO; run-of-the-river scheme with diversion dam. |
| **Kameng HEP** | Bichom & Tenga (Kameng basin) | Arunachal Pradesh (West Kameng) | Hydroelectricity (600 MW) | Implemented by NEEPCO; uses two concrete gravity dams (Bichom & Tenga). |
| **Polavaram Project** | Godavari | Andhra Pradesh (Eluru / East Godavari) | Irrigation, Hydroelectricity (960 MW), Water Supply | Officially **Indira Sagar Polavaram Project**; declared a **National Project** under AP Reorganisation Act 2014. |
| **Prakasam Barrage** | Krishna | Andhra Pradesh (Vijayawada) | Irrigation, Road Bridge | Connects Krishna and Guntur districts; irrigates Krishna delta. |
| **Somasila Dam** | Pennar (Penna) | Andhra Pradesh (SPSR Nellore) | Irrigation, Drinking Water | Largest storage dam on the Pennar River; feeds Telugu Ganga Project. |
| **Kaleshwaram (Medigadda)** | Godavari | Telangana (Jayashankar Bhupalpally) | Lift Irrigation, Drinking Water | **World's largest multi-stage lift irrigation project**; comprises Medigadda, Annaram, and Sundilla barrages. |
| **Sriram Sagar Dam** | Godavari | Telangana (Nizamabad) | Irrigation, Hydroelectricity | Also called **Pochampad Project**; lifeline for northern Telangana (Kakatiya Canal). |
| **Nizam Sagar Dam** | Manjira (tributary of Godavari) | Telangana (Kamareddy) | Irrigation, Drinking Water | Built in 1923–1931 under Nizam rule; oldest major dam in Telangana. |
| **Singur Dam** | Manjira | Telangana (Sangareddy) | Drinking Water (Hyderabad), Irrigation | Major drinking water source for Greater Hyderabad and power generation. |
| **Salal Dam** | Chenab | Jammu & Kashmir (Reasi) | Hydroelectricity (690 MW) | First hydropower project built by India in J&K under the Indus Waters Treaty (run-of-the-river). |
| **Baglihar Dam** | Chenab | Jammu & Kashmir (Ramban) | Hydroelectricity (900 MW) | Run-of-the-river project; cleared by World Bank neutral expert under Indus Waters Treaty. |
| **Dul Hasti Dam** | Chenab | Jammu & Kashmir (Kishtwar) | Hydroelectricity (390 MW) | Concrete gravity dam operated by NHPC in Doda/Kishtwar region. |
| **Pakal Dul Dam** | Marusudar (tributary of Chenab) | Jammu & Kashmir (Kishtwar) | Hydroelectricity (1,000 MW) | Concrete-face rock-fill dam (167 m high); joint venture of NHPC & JKSPDC (CVPPPL). |
| **Ratle HEP** | Chenab | Jammu & Kashmir (Kishtwar) | Hydroelectricity (850 MW) | Run-of-the-river concrete gravity dam under CVPPPL. |
| **Uri-I & Uri-II Dams** | Jhelum | Jammu & Kashmir (Baramulla) | Hydroelectricity (480 MW + 240 MW) | Run-of-the-river barrage projects near the Line of Control (LoC). |
| **Kishanganga Dam** | Kishanganga (Neelum, trib. of Jhelum) | Jammu & Kashmir (Bandipora) | Hydroelectricity (330 MW) | Concrete-faced rock-fill dam; diverts water via underground tunnel to Bonar Nallah (Wular basin). |
| **Tulbul Navigation Project** | Jhelum | Jammu & Kashmir (Baramulla) | Navigation, Flow Regulation | Located at the mouth of **Wular Lake**; also known as Wular Barrage. |
| **Nimoo Bazgo Dam** | Indus | Ladakh (Leh) | Hydroelectricity (45 MW) | Concrete gravity dam on main Indus River in cold-desert Ladakh (Alchi village). |
| **Chutak Dam** | Suru (tributary of Indus) | Ladakh (Kargil) | Hydroelectricity (44 MW) | Run-of-the-river barrage project operated by NHPC. |
| **Pong Dam** | Beas | Himachal Pradesh (Kangra) | Irrigation, Hydroelectricity (396 MW) | Reservoir is **Maharana Pratap Sagar** (a **Ramsar Wetland**); earth-fill dam. |
| **Pandoh Dam** | Beas | Himachal Pradesh (Mandi) | Water Diversion, Hydroelectricity | Diverts Beas water through tunnels into Sutlej River (Dehar Powerhouse) to augment Bhakra. |
| **Nathpa Jhakri Dam** | Sutlej | Himachal Pradesh (Kinnaur / Shimla) | Hydroelectricity (1,500 MW) | **India's largest underground hydroelectric project**; operated by SJVN. |
| **Koldam** | Sutlej | Himachal Pradesh (Bilaspur) | Hydroelectricity (800 MW) | Built by **NTPC** (NTPC's first hydropower project); rock-and-gravel fill dam. |
| **Chamera Dams (I, II, III)** | Ravi | Himachal Pradesh (Chamba) | Hydroelectricity (540 + 300 + 231 MW) | Arch/concrete gravity dams operated by NHPC on the Ravi River. |
| **Ranjit Sagar (Thein) Dam** | Ravi | Punjab (Pathankot) / J&K border | Irrigation, Hydroelectricity (600 MW) | **Highest earth-fill dam in India** (160 m); prevents Ravi water from flowing unused into Pakistan. |
| **Shahpurkandi Dam** | Ravi | Punjab (Pathankot) | Irrigation, Hydroelectricity (206 MW) | Downstream balancing reservoir of Ranjit Sagar; stops Ravi surplus flow to Pakistan. |
| **Harike Barrage** | Sutlej & Beas confluence | Punjab (Tarn Taran / Ferozepur) | Irrigation Diversion | Origin point of the **Indira Gandhi Canal** (Rajasthan Canal); Harike Wetland is a Ramsar site. |
| **Koteshwar Dam** | Bhagirathi | Uttarakhand (Tehri Garhwal) | Hydroelectricity (400 MW), Balancing | Located 22 km downstream of Tehri Dam; acts as lower balancing reservoir for Tehri PSP. |
| **Ramganga (Kalagarh) Dam** | Ramganga | Uttarakhand (Pauri Garhwal) | Irrigation, Hydroelectricity (198 MW) | Embankment dam located inside/adjacent to **Jim Corbett National Park**. |
| **Lakhwar-Vyasi Project** | Yamuna | Uttarakhand (Dehradun) | Irrigation, Drinking Water, Hydel (420 MW) | National Project benefiting 6 Yamuna basin states (UK, HP, HR, UP, RJ, Delhi). |
| **Hathnikund Barrage** | Yamuna | Haryana (Yamunanagar) | Irrigation, Drinking Water Diversion | Replaced old Tajewala Barrage (1999); feeds Western & Eastern Yamuna Canals. |
| **Gandhi Sagar Dam** | Chambal | Madhya Pradesh (Mandsaur / Neemuch) | Irrigation, Hydroelectricity (115 MW) | First of the four Chambal Valley dams (1960); masonry gravity dam. |
| **Rana Pratap Sagar Dam** | Chambal | Rajasthan (Chittorgarh / Rawatbhata) | Irrigation, Hydroelectricity (172 MW) | Second Chambal dam; supplies cooling water to **Rawatbhata Nuclear Power Plant**. |
| **Jawahar Sagar Dam** | Chambal | Rajasthan (Kota / Bundi) | Hydroelectricity (99 MW) | Third Chambal dam; pick-up dam located upstream of Kota. |
| **Kota Barrage** | Chambal | Rajasthan (Kota) | Irrigation Diversion | Fourth and terminal structure of Chambal Project; distributes water to MP and Rajasthan canals. |
| **Mahi Bajaj Sagar Dam** | Mahi | Rajasthan (Banswara) | Irrigation, Hydroelectricity | Longest dam in Rajasthan (~3.1 km); named after freedom fighter Jamnalal Bajaj. |
| **Bisalpur Dam** | Banas | Rajasthan (Tonk) | Drinking Water, Irrigation | Major drinking water source for **Jaipur, Ajmer, and Tonk**. |
| **Jakham Dam** | Jakham (tributary of Mahi) | Rajasthan (Pratapgarh) | Irrigation | **Highest dam in Rajasthan** (81 m). |
| **Matatila Dam** | Betwa | Uttar Pradesh (Lalitpur) / MP border | Irrigation, Hydroelectricity | Earthen/masonry dam (1958); also known as Laxmibai Sagar earlier; joint UP–MP project. |
| **Rajghat Dam** | Betwa | Uttar Pradesh / Madhya Pradesh border | Irrigation, Hydroelectricity | Officially **Rani Laxmibai Dam**; joint venture of UP and MP. |
| **Bansagar Dam** | Son | Madhya Pradesh (Shahdol) | Irrigation, Hydroelectricity | Joint project of **MP, UP, and Bihar** (water share ratio 2:1:1). |
| **Omkareshwar Dam** | Narmada | Madhya Pradesh (Khandwa) | Hydroelectricity (520 MW), Irrigation | Houses one of **India's largest floating solar power plants** (600 MW phased). |
| **Bargi Dam** | Narmada | Madhya Pradesh (Jabalpur) | Irrigation, Hydroelectricity, Water Supply | Officially **Rani Avanti Bai Lodhi Sagar**; one of the first completed dams on the Narmada. |
| **Tawa Dam** | Tawa (tributary of Narmada) | Madhya Pradesh (Narmadapuram / Hoshangabad) | Irrigation | Located adjacent to Satpura Tiger Reserve; triggered the historic *Tawa Matsya Sangh* movement. |
| **Ukai Dam** | Tapi (Tapti) | Gujarat (Tapi district) | Flood Control, Irrigation, Hydel (300 MW) | Forms **Vallabh Sagar** reservoir; 2nd largest reservoir in Gujarat after Sardar Sarovar. |
| **Kakrapar Weir / Dam** | Tapi | Gujarat (Tapi / Surat) | Irrigation | Located near Kakrapar Atomic Power Station. |
| **Kadana Dam** | Mahi | Gujarat (Mahisagar) | Irrigation, Hydroelectricity (240 MW) | Earthen and masonry dam; receives regulated discharge from Mahi Bajaj Sagar. |
| **Dantiwada Dam** | West Banas | Gujarat (Banaskantha) | Irrigation, Flood Control | Major earthen dam in northern Gujarat. |
| **Jayakwadi Dam** | Godavari | Maharashtra (Chhatrapati Sambhajinagar) | Irrigation, Industrial Water | Forms **Nath Sagar** reservoir (bird sanctuary); one of the largest earthen dams in Maharashtra. |
| **Ujjani (Bhima) Dam** | Bhima (tributary of Krishna) | Maharashtra (Solapur) | Irrigation, Hydroelectricity | Forms **Yashwant Sagar**; critical for sugarcane belt of Solapur & drought-prone Marathwada border. |
| **Mulshi Dam** | Mula (Bhima basin) | Maharashtra (Pune) | Hydroelectricity | Built by Tata Power; site of the famous **Mulshi Satyagraha** (1921, led by Senapati Bapat). |
| **Gangrel Dam** | Mahanadi | Chhattisgarh (Dhamtari) | Irrigation, Industrial Water (Bhilai Steel Plant) | Officially **Ravishankar Sagar Dam**; longest and largest dam in Chhattisgarh. |
| **Hasdeo Bango Dam** | Hasdeo (tributary of Mahanadi) | Chhattisgarh (Korba) | Irrigation, Industrial Water, Hydel (120 MW) | Officially **Minimata Hasdeo Bango Dam** (tallest dam in Chhattisgarh, 87 m). |
| **Rengali Dam** | Brahmani | Odisha (Angul) | Flood Control, Irrigation, Hydel (250 MW) | Controls floods in the Brahmani–Baitarani delta. |
| **Upper Indravati Dam** | Indravati (Godavari basin) | Odisha (Kalahandi / Nabarangpur) | Hydroelectricity (600 MW), Irrigation | Transfers Indravati water across basin divide to Hati/Mahanadi basin for irrigation in Kalahandi. |
| **Balimela Dam** | Sileru (Sabari–Godavari basin) | Odisha (Malkangiri) | Hydroelectricity (510 MW), Irrigation | Joint Odisha–Andhra Pradesh agreement project. |
| **Machkund (Jalaput) Dam** | Machkund (Sileru) | Odisha / Andhra Pradesh border | Hydroelectricity (120 MW) | Joint Odisha–AP project (1955); reservoir is Jalaput. |
| **Linganamakki Dam** | Sharavathi | Karnataka (Shivamogga) | Hydroelectricity | Feeds the **Sharavathi / Mahatma Gandhi HEP** near **Jog Falls**. |
| **Supa Dam** | Kali (Kalinadi) | Karnataka (Uttara Kannada) | Hydroelectricity | Highest dam in Karnataka (101 m); concrete gravity dam. |
| **Bhavanisagar Dam** | Bhavani (tributary of Kaveri) | Tamil Nadu (Erode) | Irrigation | One of the largest earthen dams in India (~8 km total length). |
| **Vaigai Dam** | Vaigai | Tamil Nadu (Theni) | Irrigation, Drinking Water (Madurai) | Supplies drinking water to Madurai and Dindigul districts. |
| **Massanjore Dam** | Mayurakshi | Jharkhand (Dumka) | Irrigation, Hydroelectricity | Built in 1955 with **Canadian assistance**, hence popularly called **"Canada Dam"**. |
| **Chandil Dam** | Subarnarekha | Jharkhand (Seraikela Kharsawan) | Irrigation, Industrial Water (Jamshedpur) | Part of the Subarnarekha Multipurpose Project (JH, WB, OD). |
| **Teesta Low Dam (III & IV)** | Teesta | West Bengal (Kalimpong / Darjeeling) | Hydroelectricity (132 MW + 160 MW) | Run-of-the-river projects operated by NHPC. |
| **Rangit Dam** | Rangit (tributary of Teesta) | Sikkim (Gyalshing / South Sikkim) | Hydroelectricity (60 MW) | First major NHPC hydropower project in Sikkim. |
| **Ithai Barrage** | Manipur (Imphal) | Manipur (Bishnupur) | Hydroelectricity (105 MW), Irrigation | Associated with **Loktak HEP**; maintains water level of Loktak Lake. |
| **Umiam (Barapani) Dam** | Umiam | Meghalaya (Ri-Bhoi) | Hydroelectricity, Tourism | Created **Umiam Lake**; Northeast India's first major hydel reservoir (1965). |
| **Doyang Dam** | Doyang | Nagaland (Wokha) | Hydroelectricity (75 MW) | Operated by NEEPCO; Doyang reservoir is famous as the roosting stopover of **Amur Falcons**. |
| **Gumti Dam** | Gumti | Tripura (Gomati) | Hydroelectricity, Irrigation | Created **Dumboor Lake**; only major hydro project in Tripura. |

### 1.2 Multipurpose River Valley Projects

| Project | Rivers | States | Dams Included | Key Purpose |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Damodar Valley Corporation (DVC)** | Damodar, Barakar, Konar | Jharkhand, West Bengal | Tilaiya, Konar, Maithon, Panchet, Durgapur Barrage | **India's 1st Multipurpose Project (1948)**; modeled on **Tennessee Valley Authority (TVA, USA)**; flood control ("Sorrow of Bengal"), power, irrigation. |
| **Bhakra Nangal Project** | Sutlej | Punjab, Haryana, Rajasthan, Himachal Pradesh | Bhakra Dam, Nangal Dam | Irrigation (Sutlej–Sirhind canals), hydroelectricity (1,325 MW), flood control; India's largest multipurpose scheme at launch. |
| **Hirakud Project** | Mahanadi | Odisha (benefits Chhattisgarh basin upstream) | Hirakud Dam | Flood control in Mahanadi delta, irrigation (~2.5 lakh ha), hydroelectricity. |
| **Chambal Valley Project** | Chambal | Madhya Pradesh, Rajasthan | Gandhi Sagar, Rana Pratap Sagar, Jawahar Sagar, Kota Barrage | Soil/ravine conservation, irrigation, hydroelectricity (50:50 joint venture of MP & Rajasthan). |
| **Narmada Valley Project** | Narmada & tributaries | Gujarat, Madhya Pradesh, Maharashtra, Rajasthan | Sardar Sarovar, Indira Sagar, Omkareshwar, Maheshwar, Bargi, Tawa | Mega irrigation across drought-prone Saurashtra/Kutch & MP, drinking water, ~3,000+ MW hydroelectricity. |
| **Tungabhadra Project** | Tungabhadra | Karnataka, Andhra Pradesh (Telangana shares via canals) | Tungabhadra (Pampa Sagar) Dam | Irrigation in Raichur, Ballari, Kurnool, Anantapur; power generation. |
| **Nagarjuna Sagar Project** | Krishna | Telangana, Andhra Pradesh | Nagarjuna Sagar Dam | Irrigation of Krishna delta and Nalgonda/Guntur uplands; 815.6 MW hydro power. |
| **Upper Krishna Project** | Krishna | Karnataka | Almatti (Lal Bahadur Shastri), Narayanpur (Basava Sagar) | Drought mitigation and irrigation in northern Karnataka (Vijayapura, Bagalkot, Yadgir, Raichur). |
| **Rihand Valley Project** | Rihand (Son tributary) | Uttar Pradesh, Madhya Pradesh | Rihand Dam (Govind Ballabh Pant Sagar) | Power supply to Singrauli coal belt, Renukoot Aluminium (HINDALCO), and irrigation via Son canal. |
| **Mayurakshi Project** | Mayurakshi | Jharkhand, West Bengal | Massanjore (Canada) Dam, Tilpara Barrage | Irrigation in Birbhum/Murshidabad (WB) and power generation. |
| **Indira Gandhi Canal Project (Beas–Sutlej Link)** | Beas, Sutlej, Ravi | Punjab, Haryana, Rajasthan | Harike Barrage, Pong Dam, Pandoh Dam | Greening Thar Desert (Ganganagar, Bikaner, Jaisalmer, Barmer); world's longest irrigation canal system (~649 km main). |
| **Kosi Project** | Kosi ("Sorrow of Bihar") | Bihar, **Nepal** (International) | Hanumannagar (Kosi) Barrage (Nepal) | Flood control in North Bihar, irrigation, power (India–Nepal Agreement 1954). |
| **Gandak Project** | Gandak (Narayani) | Bihar, Uttar Pradesh, **Nepal** | Valmikinagar (Triveni) Barrage | Irrigation in Champaran/Saran/Gorakhpur and hydro power (India–Nepal Agreement 1959). |
| **Mahakali / Tanakpur / Pancheshwar Project** | Mahakali (Sharda) | Uttarakhand, Uttar Pradesh, **Nepal** | Tanakpur Barrage, Sharda Barrage, Pancheshwar (proposed) | Irrigation, flood moderation, power under the **1996 Mahakali Treaty**. |
| **Subarnarekha Multipurpose Project** | Subarnarekha, Kharkai | Jharkhand, West Bengal, Odisha | Chandil Dam, Icha Dam (planned), Galudih Barrage | Industrial water (Jamshedpur), irrigation, flood control in Odisha/WB. |
| **Mahi Project** | Mahi | Rajasthan, Gujarat | Mahi Bajaj Sagar Dam, Kadana Dam | Irrigation in tribal Banswara/Dungarpur (RJ) and Panchmahal/Kheda (GJ); power. |
| **Ukai–Kakrapar Project** | Tapi | Gujarat | Ukai Dam, Kakrapar Weir | Flood protection for Surat city, irrigation in South Gujarat, power. |
| **Polavaram National Project** | Godavari | Andhra Pradesh (inter-state Godavari–Krishna link) | Polavaram Dam | Diverting surplus Godavari water to Krishna basin; 960 MW power; irrigation & Visakhapatnam industrial water. |
| **Kaleshwaram Lift Irrigation Project** | Godavari, Pranahita | Telangana | Medigadda, Annaram, Sundilla Barrages | Multi-stage pumping to irrigate upland Telangana districts and supply Hyderabad/industries. |
| **Ken–Betwa Link Project (KBLP)** | Ken & Betwa | Madhya Pradesh, Uttar Pradesh | Daudhan Dam (Ken) | **First river-interlinking project** under National Perspective Plan; irrigates drought-prone Bundelkhand (passes near Panna TR). |

> **SSC Exam Rapid Bites:**
> - **Jawaharlal Nehru** famously called dams **"Temples of Modern India"** (spoken at the opening of Nangal Canal / Bhakra Nangal).
> - **Indus Waters Treaty (1960):** Brokered by World Bank; **Eastern Rivers** (Sutlej, Beas, Ravi) allocated to **India**; **Western Rivers** (Indus, Jhelum, Chenab) allocated to **Pakistan** (India permitted limited run-of-the-river hydro & storage).

---

## 2. Lakes of India

### 2.1 Natural Lakes

| Lake | State / UT | Type | Key Facts |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Wular Lake** | Jammu & Kashmir (Bandipora) | Freshwater (Tectonic) | **Largest freshwater lake in India** (and one of Asia's largest); fed by **Jhelum River**; **Ramsar site** (1990). |
| **Dal Lake** | Jammu & Kashmir (Srinagar) | Freshwater | Known as **"Jewel in the crown of Kashmir"** / Srinagar's Jewel; famous for houseboats (*Shikaras*) and floating gardens (*Rad*). |
| **Manasbal Lake** | Jammu & Kashmir (Ganderbal) | Freshwater | Considered the **deepest lake in Kashmir Valley**; famous for lotus blooms. |
| **Pangong Tso** | Ladakh / Tibet (China) | Brackish / Saltwater (Endorheic) | High-altitude (~4,225 m) transboundary lake (~134 km long; ~1/3 in India, ~2/3 in China); changes colors; freezes completely in winter. |
| **Tso Moriri** | Ladakh (Changthang) | Brackish (High-altitude) | High-altitude (~4,522 m) **Ramsar site**; breeding ground for **Black-necked Crane** and Bar-headed Goose. |
| **Tso Kar** | Ladakh | Saltwater / Brackish | Twin lake basin (Startsapuk Tso freshwater + Tso Kar hypersaline); **"White Lake"** due to salt efflorescence; **Ramsar site** (2020). |
| **Chilika Lake** | Odisha (Puri, Khordha, Ganjam) | Brackish Water Lagoon | **Largest brackish water lagoon in India** (Asia's largest lagoon); separated from Bay of Bengal by sandbar; **India's 1st Ramsar site** (Oct 1981, along with Keoladeo); Nalabana Bird Sanctuary; Irrawaddy Dolphins; removed from Montreux Record in 2002. |
| **Anshupa Lake** | Odisha (Cuttack) | Freshwater (Oxbow) | Largest freshwater lake in Odisha; horseshoe-shaped oxbow lake of Mahanadi; **Ramsar site**. |
| **Pulicat Lake** | Andhra Pradesh / Tamil Nadu border | Brackish Water Lagoon | **2nd largest brackish water lagoon in India**; **Sriharikota** barrier island (ISRO Satish Dhawan Space Centre) separates it from Bay of Bengal. |
| **Kolleru Lake** | Andhra Pradesh (Eluru / Krishna) | Freshwater | **Largest shallow freshwater lake in India** located between **Godavari and Krishna deltas**; **Ramsar site** (2002); Atapaka Bird Sanctuary. |
| **Sambhar Lake** | Rajasthan (Jaipur / Didwana-Kuchaman / Ajmer) | Saltwater (Playa / Endorheic) | **Largest inland saltwater lake in India** (~9% of India's salt output); **Ramsar site** (1990); pink flamingos. |
| **Didwana, Pachpadra & Lunkaransar** | Rajasthan | Saltwater (Playas) | Desert saline lakes in Thar region used for commercial salt extraction (high NaCl/Na₂SO₄). |
| **Loktak Lake** | Manipur (Bishnupur) | Freshwater | **Largest freshwater lake in Northeast India**; famous for floating vegetation mats (**Phumdis**); hosts **Keibul Lamjao National Park** (**world's only floating National Park**, home to **Sangai / Brow-antlered Deer**); **Ramsar site** & on **Montreux Record**. |
| **Vembanad Lake** | Kerala | Brackish Water Lagoon (Kayal) | **Longest lake in India** (~96.5 km) and largest lake in Kerala; **Ramsar site**; venue of **Nehru Trophy Boat Race** (Vallam Kali); Willingdon Island & Cochin Port sit here. |
| **Ashtamudi Lake** | Kerala (Kollam) | Brackish Water Lagoon | "Eight-armed" backwater lake; gateway to Kerala backwaters; **Ramsar site** (2002). |
| **Sasthamkotta Lake** | Kerala (Kollam) | Freshwater | **Largest freshwater lake in Kerala**; **Ramsar site**; natural water purifier due to *Cavaborus* larvae. |
| **Naini, Bhimtal, Sattal, Naukuchiatal** | Uttarakhand (Nainital) | Freshwater (Tectonic / Glacial) | Famous Kumaon lakes; **Bhimtal** is the largest in Nainital district; **Naukuchiatal** (nine-cornered) is the deepest in Kumaon. |
| **Roopkund Lake** | Uttarakhand (Chamoli) | Freshwater (Glacial) | High-altitude (~5,029 m) **"Mystery Lake" / "Skeleton Lake"** near Trishul peak. |
| **Chandra Tal & Suraj Tal** | Himachal Pradesh (Lahaul–Spiti) | Freshwater (Glacial) | Chandra Tal (4,300 m, **Ramsar site**, source-near Chandra river); Suraj Tal (4,883 m, near Baralacha La, source of Bhaga river). |
| **Renuka Lake** | Himachal Pradesh (Sirmaur) | Freshwater | Shaped like a sleeping woman; **smallest Ramsar site in India** (tied with **Vembannur Wetland Complex, TN** at **20 hectares**). |
| **Cholamu (Tso Lhamo) Lake** | Sikkim (North Sikkim) | Freshwater (Glacial) | **Highest lake in India** (~5,330 m / 17,487 ft) near Dongkha La; source of **Teesta River**. |
| **Gurudongmar Lake** | Sikkim (North Sikkim) | Freshwater (Glacial) | ~5,183 m high; sacred to Buddhists, Sikhs, and Hindus; one of the highest lakes in the world. |
| **Tsongmo (Changu) Lake** | Sikkim (East Sikkim) | Freshwater (Glacial) | Glacial lake (~3,753 m) near Nathu La; remains frozen in winter. |
| **Kabartal (Kanwar Taal)** | Bihar (Begusarai) | Freshwater (Oxbow) | **Asia's largest freshwater oxbow lake** (formed by Burhi Gandak); **Bihar's 1st Ramsar site** (2020). |
| **Deepor Beel** | Assam (Kamrup) | Freshwater | Permanent freshwater riverine wetland south of Brahmaputra; **Assam's only Ramsar site**. |
| **Rudrasagar Lake** | Tripura (Sepahijala) | Freshwater | **Ramsar site**; hosts the royal water palace **Neermahal**. |
| **Nal Sarovar** | Gujarat (Ahmedabad / Surendranagar) | Brackish / Freshwater | Largest wetland bird sanctuary in Gujarat; **Ramsar site**. |
| **Shilloi Lake** | Nagaland (Phek) | Freshwater | Natural footprint-shaped lake in the Patkai hills. |

> **Ramsar Wetlands Snapshot (SSC High-Yield):**
> - **Total Ramsar Sites in India:** **103** (as of 2026).
> - **State with Most Sites:** **Tamil Nadu (20)**, followed by **Uttar Pradesh (14)**.
> - **Largest Ramsar Site:** **Sundarban Wetland** (West Bengal, ~4,230 km²).
> - **Smallest Ramsar Sites (Tied):** **Renuka Wetland** (HP) & **Vembannur Wetland Complex** (TN) — **20 ha** each.
> - **Montreux Record (India):** **Keoladeo National Park** (RJ) & **Loktak Lake** (MN) *(Chilika Lake was removed in 2002)*.

---

### 2.2 Artificial Lakes / Reservoirs

| Artificial Lake / Reservoir | River / Feed | State / UT | Key Facts |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Govind Ballabh Pant Sagar** | Rihand (Son tributary) | Uttar Pradesh / MP border | **Largest artificial lake in India by volume**; created by Rihand Dam (Sonbhadra). |
| **Gobind Sagar** | Sutlej | Himachal Pradesh (Bilaspur) | Created by **Bhakra Dam**; named after Guru Gobind Singh; major fishery & water sports hub. |
| **Dhebar (Jaisamand) Lake** | Gomati | Rajasthan (Salumbar / Udaipur) | **2nd largest artificial lake in Asia** / India's oldest large man-made marble dam lake (built 1685 by Maharana Jai Singh). |
| **Pichola, Fateh Sagar & Rajsamand** | Berach / Gomati / Ahar | Rajasthan (Udaipur / Rajsamand) | Historic Mewar artificial lakes; Pichola hosts **Jag Niwas (Lake Palace)** and Jag Mandir. |
| **Pushkar & Ana Sagar Lakes** | Luni basin / Rain-fed | Rajasthan (Ajmer) | Pushkar (52 ghats, sacred to Lord Brahma); Ana Sagar (built 1135–1150 CE by Arnoraja Chauhan). |
| **Nakki Lake** | Rain-fed (Aravalli) | Rajasthan (Mount Abu) | Highest man-made lake in Rajasthan (~1,200 m altitude). |
| **Hussain Sagar** | Musi tributary | Telangana (Hyderabad) | Heart-shaped lake built in **1563** by Ibrahim Quli Qutb Shah; separates **Hyderabad & Secunderabad**; monolithic **Buddha statue** on Gibraltar Rock. |
| **Osman Sagar & Himayat Sagar** | Musi / Esi | Telangana (Hyderabad) | Twin reservoirs built in 1920s by Nizam Osman Ali Khan to protect Hyderabad from floods and supply drinking water. |
| **Nagarjuna Sagar Reservoir** | Krishna | Telangana / Andhra Pradesh | Created by Nagarjuna Sagar Dam; **Nagarjunakonda** Buddhist site lies on an island within it. |
| **Indira Sagar Reservoir** | Narmada | Madhya Pradesh (Khandwa) | **Largest reservoir in India by water storage capacity** (12.22 billion m³). |
| **Bhojtal (Upper Lake)** | Kolans | Madhya Pradesh (Bhopal) | Built in **11th century by Paramara Raja Bhoj**; India's oldest surviving large man-made urban lake; part of **Bhoj Wetland** (Ramsar). |
| **Gandhi Sagar, Rana Pratap Sagar, Jawahar Sagar** | Chambal | Madhya Pradesh / Rajasthan | Cascade of three artificial reservoirs under Chambal Valley Project. |
| **Stanley Reservoir** | Kaveri | Tamil Nadu (Salem) | Created by **Mettur Dam** (1934); largest fishing reservoir in South India. |
| **Krishnarajasagara (KRS) Reservoir** | Kaveri | Karnataka (Mandya) | Created by KRS Dam; supplies water to Bengaluru/Mysuru. |
| **Periyar Lake** | Periyar | Kerala (Idukki) | Created by **Mullaperiyar Dam (1895)**; forms core of **Periyar Tiger Reserve**. |
| **Sukhna Lake** | Sukhna Choe (Shivalik streams) | Chandigarh | Created in **1958** by Le Corbusier & P.L. Verma at the foothills of Shivalik range. |
| **Powai & Vihar Lakes** | Mithi River | Maharashtra (Mumbai) | Built in 19th century by British; located near Sanjay Gandhi National Park & IIT Bombay. |
| **Kankaria Lake** | Rain-fed | Gujarat (Ahmedabad) | Polygonal (34-sided) lake built in **1451** by Sultan Qutb-ud-din Ahmad Shah II (formerly Hauz-e-Qutb). |
| **Umiam (Barapani) Lake** | Umiam | Meghalaya (near Shillong) | Reservoir created in 1965 for Assam/Meghalaya's first major hydroelectric project. |
| **Kodaikanal & Ooty Lakes** | Hill streams | Tamil Nadu (Dindigul / Nilgiris) | Star-shaped Kodaikanal Lake (1863) & Ooty Lake (1824 by John Sullivan). |
| **Sur Sarovar (Keetham Lake)** | Yamuna canal feed | Uttar Pradesh (Agra) | Artificial lake & bird sanctuary; **Ramsar site** (2020); home to Bear Rescue Centre. |

### 2.3 Crater Lakes & Impact Structures

| Crater / Lake | Location | Origin & Type | Key Facts |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Lonar Lake** | Buldhana, Maharashtra | **Hypervelocity Meteorite Impact Crater** in Deccan Basalt (~50,000 years old) | **India's most famous crater lake** (also the world's only known hypervelocity impact crater in basaltic rock alongside Earth analogies); **saline & highly alkaline (pH ~10.5)**; notified **National Geo-heritage Monument**; **Ramsar site** (2020); turned pink in 2020 due to *Haloarchaea* microbes. |
| **Ramgarh Crater** | Baran, Rajasthan | Meteorite Impact Structure (~165 Ma) | ~3.5 km diameter ring structure in Vindhyan sandstone with Pushkar Talab inside; recognized as a Geo-heritage site. |
| **Dhala Crater** | Shivpuri, Madhya Pradesh | Ancient Eroded Impact Crater (~1.7–2.5 Ga) | Largest impact structure in India (~11 km diameter) in Bundelkhand craton (largely eroded/no perennial lake). |

## 3. Soils of India

*(Classified into **8 major groups** by the Indian Council of Agricultural Research — **ICAR**)*

| Soil Type | Also Called | Found In (States) | Suitable Crops | Key Properties | Color |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Alluvial Soil** | **Khadar** (new alluvium, fertile) & **Bangar** (old alluvium, calcareous *kankar* nodules); **Bhabar** & **Terai** | Northern Plains (Punjab, Haryana, UP, Bihar, WB, Assam), Coastal Plains & River Deltas (~**43%** of India's area — largest group) | Rice, Wheat, Sugarcane, Cotton, Jute, Maize, Pulses, Oilseeds | Transported/depositional soil; **rich in Potash & Lime**, **deficient in Nitrogen, Phosphorus & Humus**; sandy-loam to clayey; intensely cultivated. | Light Grey to Ash Grey |
| **Black Soil** | **Regur Soil**, **Black Cotton Soil**, **Tropical Chernozem** | Deccan Trap: Maharashtra (largest share), MP, Gujarat, Telangana, Andhra Pradesh, Karnataka, Tamil Nadu (~**15%**) | **Cotton** (ideal), Sugarcane, Jowar, Tobacco, Wheat, Linseed, Soybean, Millets | Formed from **basaltic lava weathering**; high clay content; **"self-ploughing"** (swells when wet, develops deep cracks when dry); high moisture retention; **rich in Iron, Lime, Calcium, Magnesium, Alumina, Potash**; **deficient in Nitrogen, Phosphorus & Organic Matter**. | Deep Black to Greyish Black |
| **Red & Yellow Soil** | **Omnibus Group** | Tamil Nadu, Karnataka, Andhra Pradesh, Telangana, Odisha, Chhattisgarh, Jharkhand, NE States (~**18.5%**) | Millets (Ragi), Groundnut, Tobacco, Pulses, Cotton, Potato, Rice (with irrigation) | Formed from **crystalline & metamorphic rocks** (granite, gneiss) in low-rainfall areas; **rich in Iron & Potash**; **deficient in Nitrogen, Phosphorus, Lime & Humus**; porous and friable. | **Red** (ferric oxide / iron diffusion) → **Yellow** when **hydrated** |
| **Laterite Soil** | **Brick Soil** (*Latin: "Later" = brick*) | Western Ghats (Kerala, Karnataka, Maharashtra), Eastern Ghats, Odisha, Meghalaya Plateau, Assam hills (~**3.7%**) | **Cashew nut** (best), Tea, Coffee, Rubber, Coconut, Tapioca, Cinchona | Formed by intense **leaching** under high temperature & heavy rainfall (monsoon climate); **acidic**; **rich in Iron Oxide & Aluminium**; **poor in Nitrogen, Potash, Lime & Organic Matter**; hardens like brick on drying. | Brick Red / Rusty Brown |
| **Desert / Arid Soil** | **Sandy / Sierozem Soil** | Western Rajasthan (Thar), Southern Haryana, Southern Punjab, Northern Gujarat (~**4.4%**) | Bajra (Pearl Millet), Jowar, Guar, Pulses (Moong/Moth), Barley & Wheat (with Indira Gandhi Canal irrigation) | Coarse sandy texture, saline/alkaline; **high Calcium Carbonate (*kankar* layer)** restricting water infiltration; **rich in soluble salts & Phosphate**; **deficient in Nitrogen, Moisture & Humus**. | Red to Brown / Pale Yellow |
| **Mountain / Forest Soil** | **Podzolic / Hill Soil** | Himalayan region (J&K, Ladakh, HP, Uttarakhand, Sikkim, Arunachal Pradesh), Western/Eastern Ghats (~**5.5%**) | Tea, Coffee, Spices, Temperate Fruits (Apple, Pear, Saffron on Karewas), Maize, Barley | Heterogeneous (varies with altitude); loamy/silty in valleys, coarse on upper slopes; **acidic & rich in Humus** (forests) but **deficient in Potash, Phosphorus & Lime**; thin & immature. | Dark Brown to Greyish Brown |
| **Peaty & Marshy Soil** | **Kari Soil** (in Kerala), **Organic Soil** | Kottayam & Alappuzha (Kerala), Sundarbans (WB), coastal Odisha, Tamil Nadu, North Bihar | Paddy (Rice), Jute | Formed in **waterlogged, humid areas** by accumulation of dead organic matter; **very high Humus / Organic matter (10–40%)**; **heavy, black & acidic**; contains **ferrous iron & aluminium** (sometimes toxic). | Deep Black |
| **Saline & Alkaline Soil** | **Usar, Reh, Kallar, Thur, Rakhar, Karl, Chopan** | Arid/semi-arid & canal-irrigated belts of UP, Haryana, Punjab, Rajasthan, Gujarat (Rann of Kutch), Coastal deltas | Salt-tolerant crops (Barley, Sugarbeet, Dhaincha, Rice after reclamation) | Infertile due to capillary rise of salts in poorly drained/over-irrigated areas; high **Sodium, Potassium & Magnesium**; **deficient in Nitrogen & Calcium**. | White efflorescence / Greyish White |

> **Soil Science & Conservation Exam Nuggets:**
> - **All Indian soils** are generally **deficient in Nitrogen (N)** and **Humus** (except Peaty/Forest soils).
> - **Soil Reclamation:** **Gypsum ($CaSO_4 \cdot 2H_2O$)** reclaims **Alkaline/Sodic soils**; **Lime ($CaCO_3$)** reclaims **Acidic soils**; **Leaching/Drainage** removes excess salts.
> - **Soil Erosion Types (Order of severity):** **Splash → Sheet → Rill → Gully Erosion** (creates **Badlands / Ravines** in **Chambal Basin**).
> - **Karewas:** Lacustrine glacial-clay deposits in Kashmir Valley ideal for **Saffron (Zafran)**, Almonds, and Apples.

---

## 4. Agriculture in India

### 4.1 Cropping Seasons of India

| Season | Sowing Period | Harvesting Period | Climate Requirement | Major Crops |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Kharif** (Monsoon) | June – July (onset of SW Monsoon) | September – October | High temperature & abundant water/humidity | Rice, Maize, Jowar, Bajra, Ragi, Tur (Arhar), Moong, Urad, Cotton, Jute, Groundnut, Soybean |
| **Rabi** (Winter) | October – December | March – April | Cool growing season; bright sunshine at ripening | Wheat, Barley, Peas, Gram (Chana), Mustard/Rapeseed, Masoor (Lentil), Linseed |
| **Zaid** (Summer) | March – May (between Rabi & Kharif) | May – June | Warm, dry weather & longer day length | Watermelon, Muskmelon, Cucumber, Bitter Gourd, Fodder crops, Summer Moong |

*(Note: In West Bengal, Odisha, and Assam, **3 rice crops** are grown annually: **Aus** [autumn], **Aman** [winter — main crop], and **Boro** [summer].)*

---

### 4.2 Major Crops of India

| Crop | Type (Rabi / Kharif / Zaid) | Season | Top Producing States | Conditions (Temp / Rainfall / Soil) | India's Rank in World |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Rice (Paddy)** | **Kharif** *(also Rabi/Summer in South & East: Aus, Aman, Boro)* | Jun–Oct (main) | **Uttar Pradesh, Telangana, West Bengal, Punjab** *(WB leads in traditional area/output; UP/Telangana lead in recent 2024–25 production estimates; Punjab highest yield/ha)* | **Temp:** 22–32°C<br>**Rainfall:** >100–150 cm (needs standing water)<br>**Soil:** Clayey / Alluvial loam | **2nd** (after China; **1st** exporter globally) |
| **Wheat** | **Rabi** | Oct–Apr | **Uttar Pradesh** (1st), **Madhya Pradesh** (2nd), **Punjab** (3rd) *(Punjab/Haryana highest productivity)* | **Temp:** 10–15°C (sowing), 21–26°C (ripening)<br>**Rainfall:** 50–75 cm<br>**Soil:** Well-drained fertile Alluvial / Loam | **2nd** (after China) |
| **Maize (Corn)** | **Kharif** *(also Rabi in Bihar/South India)* | Jun–Oct | **Madhya Pradesh, Karnataka, Bihar, Maharashtra** | **Temp:** 21–27°C<br>**Rainfall:** 50–100 cm<br>**Soil:** Old alluvial / well-drained sandy loam | **4th–5th** (US & China lead) |
| **Millets (Overall — *Shree Anna*)** | **Kharif** *(Jowar also Rabi)* | Jun–Oct | **Rajasthan** (1st), **Karnataka**, **Madhya Pradesh**, **Maharashtra** | **Temp:** 25–32°C; drought-hardy<br>**Rainfall:** 35–75 cm<br>**Soil:** Sandy, shallow black, red soils | **1st** globally |
| **— Bajra (Pearl Millet)** | **Kharif** | Jun–Oct | **Rajasthan** (1st by wide margin), **Uttar Pradesh, Haryana** | **Rainfall:** 40–50 cm; **Soil:** Sandy / shallow black | **1st** |
| **— Jowar (Sorghum)** | **Kharif & Rabi** | Jun–Oct / Oct–Mar | **Maharashtra** (1st), **Karnataka, Tamil Nadu** | **Rainfall:** 40–75 cm; rainfed crop | Top 3–5 globally |
| **— Ragi (Finger Millet)** | **Kharif** | Jun–Oct | **Karnataka** (1st, >50% share), **Tamil Nadu, Uttarakhand** | **Soil:** Red, black, sandy loam; rich in Calcium & Iron | **1st** |
| **Pulses (Overall)** | **Kharif** (Tur, Moong, Urad) & **Rabi** (Gram/Chana, Masoor) | Both | **Madhya Pradesh** (1st), **Maharashtra, Rajasthan** | **Temp:** 20–30°C<br>**Rainfall:** 40–75 cm<br>Leguminous (**Nitrogen-fixing** via *Rhizobium*) | **1st** (largest producer, consumer & importer) |
| **Sugarcane** | **Annual / Kharif-planted** (10–18 months) | Jan–Mar (Eksali) / Jul–Aug (Adsali) | **Uttar Pradesh** (1st), **Maharashtra** (2nd), **Karnataka** (3rd) *(TN highest yield/ha)* | **Temp:** 21–27°C (hot & humid)<br>**Rainfall:** 75–100 cm<br>**Soil:** Deep fertile Alluvial & Black soil | **2nd** (after Brazil) |
| **Cotton** | **Kharif** | Apr–Nov (needs **210 frost-free days**) | **Maharashtra, Gujarat, Telangana** *(Maharashtra & Gujarat alternate 1st/2nd)* | **Temp:** 21–30°C<br>**Rainfall:** 50–100 cm (bright sunshine at boll bursting)<br>**Soil:** **Black (Regur) Soil** | **2nd** (competes closely with China for 1st/2nd) |
| **Jute ("Golden Fibre")** | **Kharif** | Mar–Aug | **West Bengal** (1st, ~75%+ share), **Bihar, Assam** | **Temp:** 25–35°C (hot & humid)<br>**Rainfall:** >150 cm<br>**Soil:** Well-drained fertile flood-plain **Alluvial soil** | **1st** (Bangladesh leads raw export; India leads production) |
| **Tea** | **Perennial Beverage Crop** | Year-round (flushes Mar–Nov) | **Assam** (1st, >50%), **West Bengal** (Darjeeling/Dooars), **Tamil Nadu** (Nilgiris) | **Temp:** 20–30°C<br>**Rainfall:** 150–300 cm (well-distributed, no stagnant water)<br>**Soil:** Deep, acidic, humus-rich hill slopes (**Laterite/Forest**) | **2nd** (after China; Kenya leads export) |
| **Coffee** | **Perennial Beverage Crop** | Harvested Nov–Feb | **Karnataka** (1st, ~70%+ — Kodagu, Chikmagalur), **Kerala** (Wayanad), **Tamil Nadu** | **Temp:** 15–28°C (under shade trees)<br>**Rainfall:** 150–250 cm<br>**Soil:** Well-drained rich forest/laterite loams (Western Ghats) | **6th–8th** (Brazil 1st; India famous for shade-grown **Arabica** & **Robusta**) |
| **Rubber (Natural)** | **Perennial Plantation** | Year-round tapping | **Kerala** (1st, ~75–80%), **Tripura** (2nd), **Karnataka** | **Temp:** >25°C (equatorial/humid)<br>**Rainfall:** >200 cm<br>**Soil:** Deep well-drained **Laterite** soil | **5th–6th** (Thailand & Indonesia lead; India 2nd largest consumer) |
| **Spices (Overall)** | **Kharif / Rabi / Perennial** | Various | **Madhya Pradesh** (overall), **Rajasthan** (cumin/coriander), **Gujarat**, **Andhra Pradesh** (chillies/turmeric), **Kerala** (pepper/cardamom) | Tropical to subtropical humid climates; well-drained loamy/lateritic soils (**Kerala** = "Spice Garden of India") | **1st** (largest producer, consumer & exporter) |
| **Oilseeds (Overall)** | **Kharif** (Groundnut, Soybean, Sesame) & **Rabi** (Mustard, Linseed) | Both | **Rajasthan** (1st), **Madhya Pradesh, Maharashtra, Gujarat** | **Temp:** 15–30°C; **Rainfall:** 50–100 cm; dry harvest period | **2nd–4th** overall (2nd in Groundnut & Rapeseed-Mustard) |
| **— Groundnut** | **Kharif** (mostly; also Rabi/Summer) | Jun–Oct | **Gujarat** (1st, >40%), **Rajasthan, Madhya Pradesh** | **Rainfall:** 50–75 cm; **Soil:** Sandy loam / red & black | **2nd** (after China) |
| **— Soybean** | **Kharif** | Jun–Oct | **Maharashtra & Madhya Pradesh** (alternate 1st/2nd; >80% combined), **Rajasthan** | **Temp:** 20–30°C; **Rainfall:** 60–90 cm; well-drained loam | **5th** (Brazil & US lead) |
| **— Rapeseed & Mustard** | **Rabi** | Oct–Mar | **Rajasthan** (1st, ~40–45% — "Mustard Bowl"), **Uttar Pradesh, Haryana** | Cool dry season; **Soil:** Alluvial / sandy loam | **2nd–3rd** (competes with Canada & China) |

### 4.3 Agricultural Revolutions in India

| Revolution | Related To | Pioneer / Father | Year / Period |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Green Revolution** | Food Grains (especially **Wheat & Rice** HYV seeds) | **M.S. Swaminathan** (India) / **Norman Borlaug** (World) | **1966–67** (3rd FYP / Plan Holiday) |
| **White Revolution** (*Operation Flood*) | **Milk & Dairy** Production | **Dr. Verghese Kurien** ("Milkman of India") | **1970–1996** (3 Phases) |
| **Blue Revolution** | **Fish & Aquaculture** | **Dr. Hiralal Chaudhuri** & **Dr. Arun Krishnan** | **1973–2002** (7th FYP push) |
| **Yellow Revolution** | **Oilseeds** (Mustard, Sunflower, Groundnut) | **Sam Pitroda** | **1986–1990** |
| **Pink Revolution** | **Prawns, Onion, Pharmaceuticals & Meat** | **Durgesh Patel** | **1970s** |
| **Red Revolution** | **Meat & Tomato** Production | **Vishal Tewari** | **1980s** |
| **Golden Revolution** | **Horticulture, Fruits & Honey** | **Nirpakh Tutej (Tuteja)** | **1991–2003** |
| **Golden Fibre Revolution** | **Jute** Production | — | **1990s** |
| **Silver Revolution** | **Eggs & Poultry** Production | **Indira Gandhi** | **1969–1978 / 2000s** |
| **Silver Fibre Revolution** | **Cotton** Production | — | **2000** (Technology Mission on Cotton) |
| **Brown Revolution** | **Leather, Cocoa & Non-conventional Energy** | **Hiralal Chaudhuri** | — |
| **Black Revolution** | **Petroleum, Crude Oil & Biodiesel** | — | — |
| **Grey Revolution** | **Fertilizers** Production | — | **1960s–1970s** |
| **Round Revolution** | **Potato** Production | — | **1965–2005** |
| **Evergreen Revolution** | **Sustainable / Holistic Agriculture** (ecology-safe productivity) | **M.S. Swaminathan** | **2000s–Present** |
| **Protein Revolution** | **Higher agricultural output via technology** (2nd Green Revolution) | **Narendra Modi & Arun Jaitley** (coined term) | **2014** |
| **Sweet Revolution** | **Beekeeping & Honey** Production | — | **2016** |
| **Parbhani Revolution** | **Okra (Bhindi)** Production | — | — |
| **Rainbow Revolution** | **Integration of all agricultural revolutions** (crop, dairy, fishery, horticulture) | Nitish Kumar / National Policy | **2000** |
| **Saffron Revolution** | **Solar Energy / Saffron** (J&K Mission) | — | **2014–2020** |

---

### 4.4 Agricultural Research Institutions of India

| Institution | Location | Focus Area |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **ICAR (Indian Council of Agricultural Research)** | **New Delhi** (Krishi Bhavan) | Apex body coordinating agricultural education and research across India (est. 1929). |
| **IARI (Indian Agricultural Research Institute — "Pusa Institute")** | **New Delhi** | Premier institute for crop improvement, Green Revolution wheat varieties, genetics & agronomy. |
| **NDRI (National Dairy Research Institute)** | **Karnal**, Haryana | Dairy science, cattle breeding, milk processing & White Revolution R&D. |
| **IVRI (Indian Veterinary Research Institute)** | **Izatnagar (Bareilly)**, Uttar Pradesh | Animal health, veterinary vaccines, and livestock research. |
| **NRRI / CRRI (National Rice Research Institute)** | **Cuttack**, Odisha | Rice breeding, high-yielding paddy varieties & rice agronomy. |
| **IIWBR (Indian Institute of Wheat and Barley Research)** | **Karnal**, Haryana | Wheat & barley genetics, rust resistance, and productivity. |
| **CPRI (Central Potato Research Institute)** | **Shimla**, Himachal Pradesh | Potato breeding (*Kufri* varieties), seed pathology, and cold storage. |
| **IIPR (Indian Institute of Pulses Research)** | **Kanpur**, Uttar Pradesh | Research on chickpea, pigeon pea, lentil, mungbean, and urdbean. |
| **IISR (Indian Institute of Sugarcane Research)** | **Lucknow**, Uttar Pradesh | Sugarcane agronomy, mechanization, and subtropical cane varieties. |
| **SBI (Sugarcane Breeding Institute)** | **Coimbatore**, Tamil Nadu | Breeding of tropical/subtropical sugarcane varieties (*Co* canes). |
| **CTRI (now ICAR–NIRCA)** | **Rajamahendravaram (Rajahmundry)**, Andhra Pradesh | Tobacco and commercial agriculture research. |
| **CICR (Central Institute for Cotton Research)** | **Nagpur**, Maharashtra | Cotton genetics, Bt/non-Bt cotton & pest management. |
| **CRIJAF (Central Research Institute for Jute and Allied Fibres)** | **Barrackpore (Kolkata)**, West Bengal | Jute, mesta, sunnhemp, sisal & allied natural fibres. |
| **CAZRI (Central Arid Zone Research Institute)** | **Jodhpur**, Rajasthan | Arid/desert agriculture, sand-dune stabilization, drought crops. |
| **IGFRI (Indian Grassland and Fodder Research Institute)** | **Jhansi**, Uttar Pradesh | Fodder crops, pasture management, and silvipasture. |
| **IIHR (Indian Institute of Horticultural Research)** | **Bengaluru**, Karnataka | Fruits, vegetables, ornamental & medicinal horticulture. |
| **IISR (Indian Institute of Spices Research)** | **Kozhikode (Calicut)**, Kerala | Black pepper, cardamom, ginger, turmeric, nutmeg & cinnamon. |
| **CCRI (Central Coffee Research Institute)** | **Chikmagalur (Balehonnur)**, Karnataka | Coffee cultivation, rust resistance & shade-grown varieties. |
| **Tocklai Tea Research Institute** | **Jorhat**, Assam | World's oldest & largest tea research institute (est. 1911). |
| **Rubber Research Institute of India (RRII)** | **Kottayam**, Kerala | Natural rubber clones, tapping & latex technology (under Rubber Board). |
| **IIMR (Indian Institute of Millets Research)** | **Hyderabad**, Telangana | Nutri-cereals (Jowar, Bajra, Ragi, small millets); Global Centre of Excellence. |
| **DRMR (Directorate of Rapeseed–Mustard Research)** | **Bharatpur**, Rajasthan | Mustard and rapeseed crop improvement. |
| **Indian Institute of Soybean Research (IISR)** | **Indore**, Madhya Pradesh | Soybean genetics, yield, and oil/protein quality. |
| **Directorate of Groundnut Research (DGR)** | **Junagadh**, Gujarat | Groundnut varieties and aflatoxin management. |
| **CTCRI (Central Tuber Crops Research Institute)** | **Thiruvananthapuram**, Kerala | Cassava (tapioca), sweet potato, yam, and taro. |
| **NRC for Banana / Grapes / Litchi** | **Tiruchirappalli** (TN) / **Pune** (MH) / **Muzaffarpur** (BH) | Commodity-specific National Research Centres for fruit crops. |
| **NRC on Camel** | **Bikaner**, Rajasthan | Camel breeding, desert livestock & camel dairy research. |
| **ICRISAT (Int'l Crops Research Institute for Semi-Arid Tropics)** | **Patancheru (Hyderabad)**, Telangana | International CGIAR institute for sorghum, pearl millet, chickpea, pigeon pea, groundnut. |
| **Central Soil Salinity Research Institute (CSSRI)** | **Karnal**, Haryana | Reclamation of saline/alkaline soils and salt-tolerant crops. |
| **ICAR–Central Marine Fisheries Research Institute (CMFRI)** | **Kochi**, Kerala | Marine fisheries, mariculture, and coastal resource sustainability. |

## 5. Minerals and Mining

> **Three Major Mineral Belts of India (NCERT):**
> 1. **North-Eastern Plateau Belt** (*"Mineral Heartland / Ruhr of India"*): Chotanagpur (Jharkhand), Odisha Plateau, WB, parts of Chhattisgarh — Iron ore, Coal, Manganese, Bauxite, Mica, Copper.
> 2. **South-Western Plateau Belt:** Karnataka, Goa, Tamil Nadu uplands, Kerala — High-grade Iron ore, Manganese, Bauxite, Gold, Limestone, Monazite.
> 3. **North-Western Belt:** Rajasthan (Aravallis) & Gujarat — Copper, Lead, Zinc, Silver, Gypsum, Marble, Limestone, Petroleum, Salt.

### 5.1 Metallic Minerals

| Mineral | Principal Ore(s) | Major Producing States (Top Regions / Mines) | Key Uses | India's World Rank |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Iron Ore** | **Haematite** ($Fe_2O_3$, best in India ~60–70% Fe), **Magnetite** ($Fe_3O_4$, 72% Fe), Limonite, Siderite | **Odisha** (1st, >50%: Mayurbhanj, Keonjhar, Sundargarh), **Chhattisgarh** (Bailadila, Dalli-Rajhara), **Karnataka** (Bellary–Hospet, Kudremukh, Kemmangundi), **Jharkhand** (Singhbhum: Noamundi, Gua) | Steel manufacturing, pig iron, heavy machinery, infrastructure | **4th** (~11% of global output; largest metallic mineral in India by value) |
| **Bauxite** | **Bauxite** ($Al_2O_3 \cdot 2H_2O$ — hydrated aluminium oxide) | **Odisha** (1st, >50%: Kalahandi, Koraput/Panchpatmali, Bolangir), **Gujarat** (Jamnagar, Kutch), **Jharkhand** (Lohardaga), **Maharashtra**, **Chhattisgarh** (Amarkantak/Maikala) | Aluminium smelting, aircraft, electrical cables, refractories | **5th** globally (~5.8% share) |
| **Manganese** | **Pyrolusite**, Psilomelane, Braunite | **Madhya Pradesh** (Balaghat, Chhindwara) & **Odisha** (Keonjhar, Sundargarh) alternate 1st/2nd; **Maharashtra** (Nagpur, Bhandara), **Karnataka** | Steel alloys (ferromanganese — ~10 kg needed per tonne of steel), dry-cell batteries, glass, chemicals | **5th** globally (~3.9% share) |
| **Copper** | **Chalcopyrite** ($CuFeS_2$), Chalcocite, Bornite, Cuprite, Malachite | **Madhya Pradesh** (1st: **Malanjkhand** in Balaghat), **Rajasthan** (2nd: **Khetri–Singhana** belt in Jhunjhunu), **Jharkhand** (Singhbhum: Mosabani, Ghatshila) | Electrical wiring, electronics, brass/bronze alloys, motors | Low in ore mining; **~10th globally in refined copper** |
| **Gold** | Native gold (quartz reefs / placer) | **Karnataka** (1st: **Hutti Gold Mines** in Raichur — only major active primary producer; historic Kolar Gold Fields closed), Jharkhand, Rajasthan *(Note: **Bihar's Jamui** holds huge estimated gold ore reserves)* | Jewellery, bullion reserves, electronics, dentistry | Very small primary mine output (outside top 10; major importer) |
| **Silver** | **Argentite**, by-product of **Galena** (Lead) & **Sphalerite** (Zinc) | **Rajasthan** (1st — virtual monopoly via Hindustan Zinc at **Zawar, Rampura Agucha, Dariba**), Karnataka (by-product) | Jewellery, coinage, photography, electronics, solar cells | Outside top global miners (by-product recovery) |
| **Lead** | **Galena** ($PbS$) | **Rajasthan** (1st, ~88–90% share: Zawar in Udaipur, Rampura Agucha in Bhilwara, Rajpura-Dariba), Andhra Pradesh | Lead-acid batteries, radiation shielding, cables, solder | **7th** globally (~4.9% share) |
| **Zinc** | **Sphalerite / Zinc Blende** ($ZnS$), Calamine ($ZnCO_3$) | **Rajasthan** (1st, ~90%+ share: Rampura Agucha — world's largest zinc mine, Zawar, Sindesar Khurd) | Galvanizing steel (anti-rust), brass alloys, pharmaceuticals | **4th** globally (~7.3% share) |
| **Chromite** | **Chromite** ($FeCr_2O_4$) | **Odisha** (1st, >95–99% monopoly: **Sukinda Valley** in Jajpur, Boula-Nuasahi in Keonjhar) | Stainless steel, chrome plating, ferrochrome, refractories | **3rd** globally (~6.8% share) |
| **Mica** *(geologically non-metallic silicate; asked in metallic tables)* | **Muscovite** (Ruby Mica), Biotite, Phlogopite | **Andhra Pradesh** (1st in output: **Nellore**), **Rajasthan** (Ajmer–Bhilwara), **Jharkhand** (**Koderma** — *"Mica Capital of India"*, Giridih), Bihar (Nawada) | Electrical & electronic insulators, capacitors, cosmetics, paints *(Reclassified as a Major Mineral in India in 2025)* | **6th** globally in current production (historically 1st in sheet mica exports) |
| **Uranium & Thorium** *(Nuclear)* | **Uraninite / Pitchblende** (U); **Monazite** (Th) | **Uranium:** Jharkhand (**Jaduguda**, Turamdih), Andhra Pradesh (**Tummalapalle** — massive reserve), Meghalaya (Domiasiat)<br>**Thorium:** Kerala, Tamil Nadu, Odisha **Monazite coastal sands** | Nuclear power generation (PHWRs & 3rd-stage Thorium breeder reactors) | Holds **~25% of world's Thorium** reserves (1st/Top globally) |

---

### 5.2 Non-Metallic Minerals

| Mineral | Major Producing States | Famous Mines / Belts | Key Uses | Notes / Global Rank |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Limestone** | **Rajasthan** (1st), **Madhya Pradesh**, **Andhra Pradesh**, Chhattisgarh | Chittorgarh, Nagaur (RJ); Katni, Satna (MP); Cuddapah (AP) | **Cement** (~75% use), iron & steel flux, lime, glass | India is **2nd largest cement producer** globally |
| **Gypsum** | **Rajasthan** (1st, >90% share), Jammu & Kashmir, Gujarat | Bikaner, Barmer, Jaisalmer, Sri Ganganagar (RJ) | Plaster of Paris, cement retarder, **alkaline soil reclamation** | Domestic self-sufficiency |
| **Dolomite** | **Chhattisgarh** (1st in output), Odisha, Andhra Pradesh, MP | Bilaspur, Bastar (CG); Sundargarh (OD) | Flux in blast furnaces (iron & steel), refractories, glass | Over 90% used in metallurgical & refractory sectors |
| **Diamond** | **Madhya Pradesh** (sole commercial primary producer), Andhra Pradesh (historical) | **Panna** (Majhgawan mine, MP); **Golconda** / Kollur (historical, AP/TS) | Gemstones, industrial cutting/drilling abrasives | **Surat (GJ)** is the world's largest diamond cutting & polishing hub |
| **Graphite** | **Odisha**, **Tamil Nadu**, **Jharkhand** | Bolangir, Kalahandi (OD); Sivaganga (TN); Palamu (JH) | Pencils, lubricants, refractories, **EV lithium-ion battery anodes**, nuclear moderators | High strategic importance (Critical Mineral) |
| **Magnesite** | **Tamil Nadu** (1st), **Uttarakhand**, **Karnataka** | Salem (**Chalk Hills**, TN), Almora/Pithoragarh (UK) | Refractory bricks for steel furnaces, magnesium compounds | Salem holds largest deposits |
| **Barytes** | **Andhra Pradesh** (1st, >95% share) | **Mangampet** (Annamayya / Kadapa district, AP) | Oil & gas well drilling mud, paints, barium chemicals | Mangampet is **world's largest single barytes deposit** |
| **Rock Phosphate** | **Rajasthan** (1st), **Madhya Pradesh** | Jhamarkotra (Udaipur, RJ); Jhabua, Chhatarpur (MP) | Phosphatic fertilizers (DAP, SSP) | Vital for reducing fertilizer import dependence |
| **Asbestos** | **Rajasthan**, **Andhra Pradesh** | Udaipur, Ajmer (RJ); Cuddapah (AP) | Heat/fire-resistant sheets, brake linings (health-regulated) | Amphibole mining restricted; chrysotile regulated |
| **Salt (Common)** | **Gujarat** (1st, >75% share), **Tamil Nadu**, **Rajasthan**, **HP** | Rann of Kutch/Kharaghoda (GJ); Thoothukudi (TN); Sambhar (RJ); **Mandi** (HP — only **rock salt** mine in India) | Food, soda ash, caustic soda, chlorine chemicals | **3rd** largest salt producer globally |
| **Kyanite & Sillimanite** | **Jharkhand**, **Maharashtra** (Kyanite); **Odisha**, **Kerala** (Sillimanite) | Lapsa Buru (Singhbhum, JH — world-famous kyanite); Chhatrapur beach sands (OD) | High-alumina refractory linings, spark plugs, ceramics | Critical refractory minerals |
| **Talc / Soapstone / Steatite** | **Rajasthan** (1st, >80%), **Uttarakhand** | Udaipur, Bhilwara, Dungarpur (RJ); Bageshwar (UK) | Paper, talcum powder, ceramics, paints, insecticides | Softest mineral (Mohs hardness = 1) |

### 5.3 Energy Resources

#### A. Coal Types & Classification

| Type of Coal | Carbon Content | Moisture / Volatile Matter | Properties & Quality | Major Occurrence in India |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Anthracite** | **80% – 95%** | Very low (<5%) | Hardest, shiny, highest calorific value, burns with blue smokeless flame | **Kalakot (Jammu & Kashmir)** — very small reserves |
| **Bituminous** | **60% – 80%** | Moderate (15–40%) | Most widely used commercial & **coking coal** (steel making + thermal power) | **Gondwana Coalfields (~98% of India's reserves)**: Damodar, Mahanadi, Son, Godavari valleys |
| **Lignite** (*Brown Coal*) | **40% – 55%** | High (>30%) | Soft, brownish, low heating value; used in mine-mouth thermal power | **Neyveli (Tamil Nadu — largest)**, Palana/Barsingsar (Rajasthan), Panandhro (Gujarat), Mannargudi |
| **Peat** | **< 40%** | Very high | First stage of wood-to-coal transformation; low heat, high smoke | Swamps/marshy areas (Nilgiris, Sundarbans, Kashmir) |

*(Note: **Gondwana Coal** [~250 million years old] accounts for **~98% of India's reserves and ~99% of production**; **Tertiary Coal** [~15–60 million years old] occurs in Assam, Meghalaya, Arunachal, Nagaland, and Rajasthan/TN lignite. **India ranks 2nd globally in coal production** after China; **Odisha** & **Chhattisgarh** lead production, while **Jharkhand** holds the largest reserves.)*

#### B. Major Coalfields of India

| Coalfield | State | River Basin | Key Exam Facts |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Jharia** | Jharkhand (Dhanbad) | Damodar Valley | **Largest coal-producing field in Jharkhand**; India's primary source of **prime coking coal**; famous for century-old underground coal fires. |
| **Raniganj** | West Bengal (Paschim Bardhaman) | Damodar Valley | **Oldest coalfield in India** (mining began **1774** under East India Company); superior non-coking coal. |
| **Bokaro & Karanpura** | Jharkhand | Damodar Valley | Major coking and thermal coal supplier to Bokaro Steel Plant & thermal stations. |
| **Talcher** | Odisha (Angul) | Brahmani Valley | **Largest coal reserve field in Odisha** (MCL); supplies thermal coal across eastern & southern India. |
| **Ib Valley** | Odisha (Jharsuguda / Sundargarh) | Mahanadi Basin | Major open-cast thermal coal belt. |
| **Korba** | Chhattisgarh | Hasdeo Valley | Major power-grade coalfield (SECL — **Gevra, Dipka, Kusmunda**, among Asia's largest open-cast mines). |
| **Singrauli** | Madhya Pradesh / UP border | Son Valley | Major thermal power hub ("Energy Capital of India" — supplies Vindhyachal, Rihand, Anpara STPPs). |
| **Singareni** | Telangana (Khammam / Peddapalli / Mancherial) | Godavari Valley | **Only major coal producer in South India** (operated by SCCL, est. 1889/1920). |
| **Neyveli** | Tamil Nadu (Cuddalore) | Cauvery Basin | **Largest Lignite coalfield in India** (operated by NLC India Ltd.). |
| **Makum / Namchik–Namphuk** | Assam (Tinsukia) / Arunachal Pradesh | Upper Brahmaputra | Major **Tertiary coalfields** (high sulphur content). |

#### C. Petroleum & Natural Gas

| Oil / Gas Field | State / Region | Operator & Key Facts |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Mumbai High (Bombay High)** | **Western Offshore** (176 km off Mumbai coast) | Discovered **1974**, production started **1976** (ONGC); **India's largest oilfield** (~60%+ of India's crude via offshore fields; *Sagar Samrat* rig). |
| **Bassein (Vasai)** | **Western Offshore** (South of Mumbai High) | **India's largest offshore natural gas field** (ONGC). |
| **Digboi** | Assam (Tinsukia) | **Oldest oilfield (1889)** and **oldest operating refinery in Asia/India (1901)**. |
| **Naharkatiya & Moran–Hugrijan** | Assam (Dibrugarh / Sivasagar) | Major onshore fields of Upper Assam valley supplying Guwahati & Barauni refineries. |
| **Ankleshwar, Kalol, Cambay (Lunej), Mehsana** | Gujarat (Cambay Basin) | **Ankleshwar** (1958, "Fountain of Prosperity") is Gujarat's premier onshore field; Cambay had India's 1st oil show post-independence. |
| **Barmer–Sanchore Basin (Mangala, Bhagyam, Aishwariya)** | Rajasthan (Barmer) | **Largest onshore oil-producing state** in India today; operated by Vedanta/Cairn & ONGC. |
| **Krishna–Godavari (KG-D6) Basin** | Andhra Pradesh Offshore (Bay of Bengal) | Major deepwater **natural gas** basin (Reliance/BP & ONGC KG-DWN-98/2). |
| **Jamnagar Refinery** | Gujarat | **World's largest oil refining complex** (Reliance Industries, private sector). |

*(Petroleum Production Lead: **Offshore Mumbai High** overall; **Rajasthan** leads among onshore states, followed by Gujarat & Assam. Natural Gas: **Offshore** ~67% share; **Assam** leads onshore states. **HBJ Pipeline** — Hazira–Vijaipur–Jagdishpur — was India's first cross-country gas pipeline, 1,750 km.)*

#### D. Nuclear Power Plants in India

*(NPCIL operates **24 commercial reactors = 8,780 MW** as of 2025–2026, excluding old RAPS-1 100 MW; India's 3-Stage Nuclear Programme was formulated by **Dr. Homi J. Bhabha**.)*

| Plant Name | Location & State | Operator | Reactor Type | Installed / Active Capacity | Key Exam Facts |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Tarapur Atomic Power Station (TAPS)** | Tarapur (Palghar), Maharashtra | NPCIL | 2 × BWR (160 MW) + 2 × PHWR (540 MW) | **1,400 MW** (4 units) | **India's oldest nuclear power plant** (commissioned Oct **1969** with **USA** assistance); only site with BWRs. |
| **Rajasthan Atomic Power Station (RAPS)** | Rawatbhata (Chittorgarh), Rajasthan | NPCIL | PHWR (1 × 200 + 4 × 220 + 1 × 700 MW active) | **1,180 MW** *(Fleet Units 2–6)* + **RAPS-7 (700 MW)** | Built initially with **Canada**; **RAPS-7 (700 MW)** entered commercial operation on **April 15, 2025**; **RAPS-8 (700 MW)** in advanced commissioning. *(RAPS-1 100 MW DAE-owned, shut down).* |
| **Madras Atomic Power Station (MAPS) & PFBR** | Kalpakkam (Chengalpattu), Tamil Nadu | NPCIL / **BHAVINI** (PFBR) | 2 × 220 MW PHWR + **500 MW PFBR** | **440 MW** (MAPS) + **500 MW PFBR** | **1st completely indigenous nuclear plant** (1984); **PFBR (Prototype Fast Breeder Reactor, Stage-2)** achieved **first criticality on April 6, 2026**. |
| **Narora Atomic Power Station (NAPS)** | Narora (Bulandshahr), Uttar Pradesh | NPCIL | 2 × 220 MW PHWR | **440 MW** (2 units) | Situated on the banks of Ganga River; seismic zone-IV design. |
| **Kakrapar Atomic Power Station (KAPS)** | Kakrapar (Tapi), Gujarat | NPCIL | 2 × 220 MW + **2 × 700 MW Indigenous PHWR** | **1,840 MW** (4 units) | **KAPS-3 & KAPS-4** are India's flagship **first indigenous 700 MW PHWRs** (KAPS-4 commercial operation in 2024). |
| **Kaiga Generating Station (KGS)** | Kaiga (Uttara Kannada), Karnataka | NPCIL | 4 × 220 MW PHWR | **880 MW** (4 units) | Located on Kali River; holds world record for longest uninterrupted operation of a PHWR (Unit-1: 962 days). |
| **Kudankulam Nuclear Power Plant (KKNPP)** | Kudankulam (Tirunelveli), Tamil Nadu | NPCIL | **PWR (VVER-1000)** | **2,000 MW** operational (2 × 1,000 MW) | **India's largest operational nuclear power station**; built with **Russian (Rosatom)** collaboration; Units 3–6 (4 × 1,000 MW) under construction. |
| **Gorakhpur Haryana Anu Vidyut Pariyojana** | Fatehabad, Haryana | NPCIL | 700 MW PHWR | 2,800 MW planned (Under construction) | First nuclear power project in Haryana (Phase-I: 2 × 700 MW). |
| **Upcoming / Approved Sites** | **Mahi Banswara** (RJ), **Chutka** (MP), **Jaitapur** (MH), **Kovvada** (AP) | NPCIL (Jaitapur: France/EDF; Kovvada: USA) | PHWR / EPR / AP1000 | Planned multi-GW | **Jaitapur** (Ratnagiri, MH) planned as world's largest nuclear site (9,900 MW with France); **Chutka** near Kanha/Narmada MP. |

#### E. Renewable Energy (Solar & Wind)

- **Cumulative Non-Fossil Capacity (2025–2026):** Surpassed **299–308 GW**; **Solar ~175 GW** (added record 44.61 GW in FY 2025–26); **Wind >59 GW** (added record 6.05 GW in FY 2025–26).
- **Top Solar States:** **Rajasthan** (1st), **Gujarat**, **Karnataka**, **Maharashtra**, **Tamil Nadu**.
- **Top Wind States:** **Gujarat** & **Tamil Nadu** (top two; Gujarat leads recent installed capacity), followed by **Karnataka, Maharashtra, Rajasthan**.
- **Major Solar Parks:**
  - **Khavda Renewable Energy Park (Kutch, Gujarat):** World's largest planned hybrid (solar + wind) park (**30 GW** capacity across 72,600 ha).
  - **Bhadla Solar Park (Phalodi/Jodhpur, Rajasthan):** **2,245 MW** commissioned; one of the world's largest operational solar parks.
  - **Pavagada Solar Park / Shakti Sthala (Tumakuru, Karnataka):** **2,050 MW**.
  - **Kurnool Ultra Mega Solar Park (Andhra Pradesh):** **1,000 MW**; **Rewa Solar Park (MP):** **750 MW** (supplies Delhi Metro); **Omkareshwar (MP):** major floating solar hub.
- **Major Wind Hubs:** **Muppandal Wind Farm** (Kanyakumari, Tamil Nadu — largest wind cluster in India, 1,500 MW), **Jaisalmer Wind Park** (Rajasthan), **Brahmanvel & Dhalgaon** (Maharashtra), **Kutch** (Gujarat).
- **International Solar Alliance (ISA):** Launched by India & France at COP21 (2015); **HQ in Gurugram, Haryana** (1st international treaty-based organisation headquartered in India).
