### World Climate Zones

#### 1. The Three Broad Latitudinal Heat Zones

| Zone | Latitudinal Extent | Sun Angle & Temperature | Key Characteristics & Sub-Zones |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Tropical (Torrid) Zone** | **0° to 23.5° N & S** (Between Tropic of Cancer & Tropic of Capricorn) | Overhead sun twice a year; **high temperatures year-round** (no true winter; mean monthly temp >18°C) | Highest annual insolation; dominated by ITCZ & Trade Winds; hosts **Equatorial Rainforests**, **Tropical Monsoon**, **Savanna**, and **Hot Trade-Wind Deserts**. |
| **Temperate Zone** | **23.5° to 66.5° N & S** (Tropics to Arctic/Antarctic Circles) | Slanting sunrays (never directly overhead); **4 distinct seasons** (spring, summer, autumn, winter) | Dominated by **Westerlies** & frontal cyclones; hosts **Mediterranean**, **China-type**, **Marine West Coast (British-type)**, **Steppe**, **Continental**, and **Taiga** climates. |
| **Polar (Frigid) Zone** | **66.5° to 90° N & S** (Above Arctic & Antarctic Circles) | Extremely oblique sunrays; **6 months daylight / 6 months darkness** near poles; **extreme cold** | Dominated by Polar Easterlies; low precipitation ("polar desert"); hosts **Tundra** (permafrost, mosses/lichens) and **Ice Cap** (Antarctica, inner Greenland). |

#### 2. Major Climatic Sub-Types (Köppen-Based Exam Reference)

| Climate Type | Köppen Code | Latitude / Distribution | Rainfall & Temperature | Natural Vegetation & Economy |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Equatorial (Tropical Wet)** | **Af** | 0°–10° N/S (Amazon, Congo, Malaysia, Indonesia) | Hot (~27°C year-round, tiny annual range); heavy daily **convectional "4 o'clock" rainfall** (>200 cm). | **Selvas** (broadleaf evergreen rainforest); mahogany, ebony, rubber; high biodiversity. |
| **Tropical Monsoon** | **Am** | 10°–25° N/S (India, SE Asia, N Australia) | Seasonal wind reversal; intense summer monsoon rain + distinct dry season. | Tropical deciduous (**monsoon**) forests (teak, sal, bamboo); rice, sugarcane, jute. |
| **Tropical Savanna (Sudan)** | **Aw** | 5°–20° N/S (Sudan, East Africa, Llanos, Campos) | Warm year-round; wet summers, dry winters (~75–150 cm). | Tall grass + scattered deciduous trees (acacia, baobab); wildlife safaris, pastoralism. |
| **Hot Desert (Sahara-type)** | **BWh** | 15°–30° N/S western coasts/interiors (Sahara, Thar, Atacama, Arabian) | Highest diurnal temperature range; rainfall <25 cm. | Xerophytes (cactus, date palm, thorny bushes); oasis agriculture. |
| **Mid-Latitude Steppe** | **BSk** | 35°–55° interior continents (Eurasian Steppe, Prairies) | Hot summers, cold winters; light summer rain (~25–50 cm). | Short nutritious grass; commercial grain farming & livestock ranching. |
| **Mediterranean** | **Cs** | 30°–45° **western margins** (Mediterranean basin, California, Central Chile, Cape Town, SW Australia) | **Hot dry summers, mild wet winters** (winter rain from shifting Westerlies!). | **Sclerophyllous** evergreen shrubs (*maquis/chaparral*), olive, cork oak, **citrus fruits & vineyards ("Orchards of the World")**. |
| **Humid Subtropical (China-type)** | **Cfa / Cwa** | 25°–40° **eastern margins** (SE China, SE USA, S Brazil, N India/Ganga plain) | Warm wet summers, cool winters; typhoons/hurricanes. | Mixed deciduous & evergreen forests; rice, tea, cotton, maize. |
| **Marine West Coast (British-type)** | **Cfb** | 40°–60° **western margins** (UK, NW Europe, British Columbia, New Zealand) | Mild year-round (moderated by warm ocean currents); **rain all year** under Westerlies. | Temperate deciduous forests (oak, beech, ash); dairy farming & mixed agriculture. |
| **Subarctic / Taiga (Siberian-type)** | **Dfc** | 50°–70° N only (Siberia, Northern Canada, Scandinavia) | Short cool summers, bitterly cold winters (**largest annual temperature range**; Verkhoyansk/Oymyakon); light summer snow/rain. | **Boreal coniferous forests** (pine, spruce, fir, larch) — world's primary source of **softwood & paper pulp**. |
| **Tundra** | **ET** | 65°–75° N (Arctic coasts of Canada, Alaska, Siberia, Greenland edge) | Mean warmest month 0°–10°C; **permafrost** subsoil; <25 cm precip (mostly snow). | Treeless **mosses, lichens, sedges, dwarf willows**; reindeer/caribou; Inuit/Sami tribes. |
| **Ice Cap** | **EF** | Interior Antarctica & Greenland | All months below **0°C**; permanent ice sheet; katabatic winds. | Practically no vegetation; penguins (Antarctica), polar research stations. |

### Important Geographic Terms (Glossary)

| Term | Meaning / Definition | Notable Examples (World & India) |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Alluvial Fan** | Fan-shaped deposit of gravel/sand dropped where a fast mountain stream exits onto a flat plain. | Kosi Megafan; Bhabar foothills along Himalayas. |
| **Archipelago** | A group or chain of islands clustered together in a sea or ocean. | **Malay Archipelago** (Indonesia — largest), Japan, Philippines, **Andaman & Nicobar**, Lakshadweep. |
| **Atoll** | A ring-shaped coral reef, island, or series of islets surrounding a central lagoon. | **Maldives**, **Lakshadweep** (e.g., Minicoy, Kavaratti), Bikini Atoll. |
| **Badlands** | Heavily eroded, barren terrain of soft sedimentary rocks dissected by deep gullies/ravines. | **Chambal Ravines** (MP/UP/Rajasthan), Badlands of South Dakota (USA). |
| **Basin** | A depression or region drained by a river and its tributaries (river basin) or an enclosed structural dip. | Amazon Basin, Ganga Basin, Tarim Basin (endorheic). |
| **Bay** | A broad inlet of the sea where the land curves inward (generally wider/less enclosed than a gulf). | **Bay of Bengal**, Hudson Bay, Bay of Biscay. |
| **Bight** | A long, gradual inward curve or wide indentation along an open coastline. | **Great Australian Bight**, Bight of Benin, Bight of Biafra. |
| **Butte** | An isolated, steep-sided hill with a small flat top (smaller than a mesa, formed by erosion). | Monument Valley buttes (USA). |
| **Caldera** | A large cauldron-like depression formed when a volcanic magma chamber empties and the summit collapses. | Yellowstone Caldera, **Toba** (Indonesia), Crater Lake (Oregon). |
| **Canyon** | A deep, narrow valley with steep rocky walls carved over millennia by a river (especially in arid plateaus). | **Grand Canyon** (Colorado River, USA), Yarlung Tsangpo Grand Canyon, **Gandikota** ("Grand Canyon of India", Pennar River). |
| **Cape** | A prominent pointed headland or piece of land jutting out into a sea or ocean. | **Cape of Good Hope** (South Africa), **Cape Horn** (Chile), **Cape Comorin / Kanyakumari** (India). |
| **Cataract** | A large waterfall or steep series of white-water rapids over rocky ledges. | Boyoma Falls (Congo), Nile Cataracts 1–6. |
| **Confluence** | The meeting point where two or more rivers or streams join into one channel. | **Prayagraj** (Ganga–Yamuna), **Devprayag** (Bhagirathi–Alaknanda), **Khartoum** (White–Blue Nile). |
| **Continental Shelf** | Gently sloping submerged edge of a continent extending from the shoreline to the continental slope (<200 m deep). | Grand Banks, North Sea Dogger Bank, **Bombay High** (rich in oil & fisheries). |
| **Coral Reef** | Underwater calcium-carbonate structure built by colonial coral polyps in warm, shallow, clear tropical seas. | **Great Barrier Reef** (Australia), Gulf of Mannar, Lakshadweep, Andaman reefs. |
| **Delta** | A triangular/fan-shaped depositional landform created at a river's mouth where sediment settles into calm sea/lake water. | **Sundarbans Delta** (Ganga–Brahmaputra — world's largest), Nile Delta (arcuate), Mississippi (bird's-foot). |
| **Distributary** | A river branch that flows away from a main river channel near its delta and does not return. | **Hooghly** (off Ganga), Damietta & Rosetta (Nile). |
| **Doab** | Fertile alluvial land lying between two converging rivers (Persian: *do* = two, *ab* = water). | **Bist Doab** (Beas–Sutlej), **Bari Doab** (Beas–Ravi), **Ganga–Yamuna Doab**. |
| **Dune** | A hill or ridge of loose sand piled up by wind (aeolian) in deserts or along beaches. | **Barchans** (crescent-shaped), **Seif** (longitudinal); Thar *dhrians*. |
| **Endorheic Basin** | A closed drainage basin that retains water and allows no outflow to external oceans (drains into salt lakes/sinks). | **Caspian Sea**, Aral Sea, Dead Sea, **Sambhar Lake / Luni** (India). |
| **Estuary** | A semi-enclosed tidal mouth of a river where freshwater mixes with salty seawater (no delta formed). | **Narmada & Tapi** mouths (India), **Thames**, **St. Lawrence**, **Zuari & Mandovi** (Goa). |
| **Fjord** | A long, narrow, deep sea inlet bounded by steep cliffs, carved originally by glacial erosion. | **Sognefjord** (Norway), Milford Sound (New Zealand), Chilean & Alaskan fjords. |
| **Floodplain** | Flat low-lying land adjacent to a river channel that is periodically flooded and enriched with alluvium. | Indo-Gangetic Plains (**Khadar** = new alluvium, **Bhangar** = old alluvium). |
| **Geyser** | A hot spring that intermittently ejects jets of boiling water and steam due to geothermal pressure. | **Old Faithful** (Yellowstone, USA), **Strokkur** (Iceland). |
| **Glacier** | A massive, slow-moving river of compacted ice and snow flowing downhill under its own weight. | **Lambert–Fisher** (Antarctica — largest), **Fedchenko** (Pamirs), **Siachen** (India's longest ~76 km). |
| **Gorge** | A very narrow, steep-walled V-shaped valley cut by a swiftly flowing river (narrower than a canyon). | **Indus Gorge** (near Bunji), Dihang Gorge, Tiger Leaping Gorge (Yangtze). |
| **Gulf** | A large, deep inlet of the ocean largely enclosed by land with a narrower mouth than a bay. | **Gulf of Mexico** (largest), Persian Gulf, **Gulf of Khambhat & Kutch** (India). |
| **Horst & Graben** | Raised crustal block (**Horst** = block mountain) and sunken block (**Graben** = rift valley) bounded by parallel faults. | **Vindhya/Satpura** (Horsts) & **Narmada/Tapi valleys** (Grabens); Black Forest & Rhine Graben. |
| **Isthmus** | A narrow strip of land connecting two larger landmasses and separating two bodies of water. | **Isthmus of Panama** (N & S America), **Isthmus of Suez** (Asia & Africa), **Isthmus of Kra** (Thailand). |
| **Karst Topography** | Landscape formed by chemical dissolution of soluble rocks (limestone, dolomite, gypsum) creating sinkholes & caves. | Karst region (Slovenia), **Guilin** (China), **Borra Caves** (AP) & **Mawsmai Caves** (Meghalaya). |
| **Lagoon** | A shallow body of coastal saltwater/brackish water separated from the open ocean by a sandbar, spit, or coral reef. | **Chilika Lake** (Odisha — India's largest), **Pulicat** (AP/TN), **Vembanad** (Kerala *kayals*). |
| **Levee** | A natural or artificial raised embankment running alongside a river bank that prevents overflow during floods. | Natural levees along Lower Mississippi & Lower Ganga. |
| **Loess** | Fine-grained, fertile, wind-blown yellowish silt deposited far from desert/glacial sources. | **Loess Plateau** (China — Yellow River basin), Pampas. |
| **Meander** | A pronounced sweeping loop or bend formed in the middle/lower course of a mature river via lateral erosion & deposition. | Lower Ganga, **Jhelum** (famous meandering near Srinagar), Mississippi. |
| **Mesa** | An elevated, flat-topped hill or tableland with steep cliff sides (Spanish for "table"; larger than a butte). | Grand Mesa (Colorado, USA), **Pats** (Chota Nagpur Plateau). |
| **Moraine** | Accumulations of unsorted glacial rock debris (*till*) deposited at the sides, center, or snout of a glacier. | Terminal & lateral moraines across Kashmir (Karewas), Alps, & Northern Europe. |
| **Oasis** | A fertile green area in a desert where underground water reaches the surface via springs or wells. | **Siwa Oasis** (Egypt), Al-Ahsa (Saudi Arabia). |
| **Oxbow Lake** | A U-shaped or crescent lake formed when a curved river meander is cut off from the main channel. | **Kanwar (Kabar) Lake** (Bihar — Asia's largest freshwater oxbow lake), Wular & Ganga floodplain *mauns/beels*. |
| **Peninsula** | A landmass surrounded by water on three sides and connected to a mainland on one side. | **Arabian Peninsula** (world's largest), **Indian Peninsula**, Iberian, Korean, Scandinavian. |
| **Plateau** | An extensive, elevated flat-topped highland rising sharply above surrounding terrain ("Tableland"). | **Tibetan Plateau** (highest & largest), **Deccan Plateau**, **Chota Nagpur Plateau** ("Ruhr of India"). |
| **Rift Valley** | A linear lowland between highlands created by divergent tectonic plates pulling Earth's crust apart. | **East African (Great) Rift Valley**, Rhine Valley, **Narmada–Tapi–Damodar valleys** (India). |
| **Strait** | A narrow natural waterway connecting two larger bodies of water and separating two landmasses. | **Palk Strait**, Strait of Malacca, Strait of Gibraltar, Bering Strait. |
| **Tombolo** | A sandy depositional bar or spit connecting an island to the mainland or to another island. | Chesil Beach (UK), **Rameswaram / Pamban** sandbar formations, Mount Maunganui (NZ). |
| **Tributary** | A smaller river or stream that flows into and joins a larger main river. | **Yamuna** (longest tributary of Ganga), **Madeira** (largest tributary of Amazon). |
| **Tundra** | Treeless polar or high-alpine biome characterized by permanently frozen subsoil (**permafrost**), mosses, and lichens. | Arctic coast of Russia/Canada/Alaska; Alpine tundra above timberline in Himalayas. |
| **Watershed (Water Divide)** | An elevated ridge or highland separating two adjacent river drainage basins. | **Ambala** (water divide between Indus and Ganga systems); Western Ghats (Arabian Sea vs Bay of Bengal). |

## SSC CHSL / CGL Geography PYQs (Last 10 Years)

### Physical Geography PYQs (12 Questions)

**Q.** Which atmospheric layer contains the ozone layer that absorbs harmful ultraviolet (UV) radiation from the Sun? (SSC CHSL 2021)
**Answer:** Stratosphere
**Key Fact:** Located roughly between 15 km and 50 km altitude, the stratosphere hosts maximum ozone concentration around 20–35 km.

**Q.** Pumice, obsidian, and andesite belong to which category of rocks? (SSC CGL 2024)
**Answer:** Extrusive Igneous Rocks
**Key Fact:** They form when volcanic magma erupts onto Earth's surface as lava and cools rapidly, producing glassy or fine-grained textures.

**Q.** Which soil type is also known as "Regur soil" and is ideal for cotton cultivation? (SSC CGL 2018)
**Answer:** Black Soil
**Key Fact:** Derived from basaltic Deccan Trap lava, black cotton soil is rich in clay, iron, lime, and magnesium, and develops deep cracks when dry (self-ploughing).

**Q.** Which soil covers the largest geographical area in India (~40–43%)? (SSC CHSL 2023)
**Answer:** Alluvial Soil
**Key Fact:** Deposited by the Indus, Ganga, and Brahmaputra river systems, it is classified into newer fertile *Khadar* and older *Bhangar* plains.

**Q.** The narrow belt of porous gravel and pebbles parallel to the Shiwalik foothills where Himalayan streams disappear underground is called: (SSC CGL 2023)
**Answer:** Bhabar
**Key Fact:** South of the 8–16 km wide Bhabar belt lies the marshy *Terai* zone where those underground streams re-emerge on the surface.

**Q.** Which type of rocks are granite and basalt? (SSC CHSL 2019)
**Answer:** Igneous Rocks
**Key Fact:** Granite is an intrusive (plutonic) igneous rock cooled slowly underground, whereas basalt is an extrusive (volcanic) igneous rock cooled rapidly at the surface.

**Q.** Which seismic waves can travel through solids, liquids, and gases and are the first to reach a seismograph? (SSC CGL 2020)
**Answer:** P-waves (Primary waves)
**Key Fact:** P-waves are longitudinal compressional waves, unlike S-waves (Secondary waves) which are transverse and cannot pass through Earth's liquid outer core.

**Q.** Which planet in our solar system is known as "Earth's Twin" due to similar size and mass? (SSC CHSL 2017)
**Answer:** Venus
**Key Fact:** Venus is also the hottest planet (runaway CO₂ greenhouse effect) and rotates clockwise (retrograde), opposite to most planets.

**Q.** What is the boundary line separating Earth's crust from the underlying mantle called? (SSC CGL 2019)
**Answer:** Mohorovičić Discontinuity (Moho)
**Key Fact:** Discovered in 1909, it sits ~30–40 km below continents and ~5–10 km below oceans; Gutenberg Discontinuity separates the mantle from the outer core.

**Q.** Which local wind in Kerala and coastal Karnataka brings pre-monsoon showers beneficial for early ripening of mangoes? (SSC CHSL 2024)
**Answer:** Mango Showers
**Key Fact:** Similar pre-monsoon thunderstorms are called *Kalbaishakhi* in West Bengal/Assam (*Bardoli Chheerha*) and *Blossom Showers* for coffee in Kerala.

**Q.** Hanging valleys, cirques, and moraines are landforms primarily created by which geological agent? (SSC CGL 2023)
**Answer:** Glaciers
**Key Fact:** Cirques and hanging valleys are glacial erosional landforms, whereas moraines and drumlins are glacial depositional features.

**Q.** Which layer of the atmosphere is responsible for reflecting radio waves back to Earth and displaying auroras? (SSC CHSL 2020)
**Answer:** Thermosphere (Ionosphere)
**Key Fact:** Charged ions in the ionosphere reflect shortwave radio signals, while solar wind collisions produce Aurora Borealis and Aurora Australis.

---

### Indian Geography PYQs (16 Questions)

**Q.** The Tropic of Cancer (23.5°N) passes through how many Indian states? (SSC CHSL 2018 / CGL 2023)
**Answer:** 8 states
**Key Fact:** From west to east, they are Gujarat, Rajasthan, Madhya Pradesh, Chhattisgarh, Jharkhand, West Bengal, Tripura, and Mizoram.

**Q.** Which is the southernmost point of the Indian Union? (SSC CGL 2019)
**Answer:** Indira Point (Pygmalion Point)
**Key Fact:** Located at 6°45'N on Great Nicobar Island, whereas Kanyakumari (Cape Comorin, 8°4'N) is the southernmost point of mainland India.

**Q.** Which Indian state has the longest coastline? (SSC CHSL 2019 / 2022)
**Answer:** Gujarat
**Key Fact:** Gujarat has ~1,214.7 km of mainland coastline (over 1,600 km with creeks), followed by Andhra Pradesh and Tamil Nadu among states.

**Q.** Which neighboring country shares the longest international land border with India? (SSC CHSL 2022)
**Answer:** Bangladesh
**Key Fact:** India shares 4,096.7 km of border with Bangladesh across five states (West Bengal, Assam, Meghalaya, Tripura, and Mizoram).

**Q.** Which is the oldest mountain range in India? (SSC CGL 2022 / CHSL 2023)
**Answer:** Aravalli Range
**Key Fact:** Stretching ~800 km from Gujarat through Rajasthan and Haryana to Delhi, its highest peak is Guru Shikhar (1,722 m) in Mount Abu.

**Q.** Which is the highest mountain peak in South India (Peninsular India)? (SSC CHSL 2020)
**Answer:** Anamudi
**Key Fact:** Rising to 2,695 m in the Anaimalai Hills (Idukki district, Kerala), it stands higher than Doddabetta (2,637 m) in the Nilgiris.

**Q.** Which plateau is popularly called the "Mineral Heartland of India" or "Ruhr of India"? (SSC CGL 2024)
**Answer:** Chota Nagpur Plateau
**Key Fact:** Centered in Jharkhand and parts of Odisha, West Bengal, and Chhattisgarh, it holds vast reserves of coal, iron ore, mica, and copper.

**Q.** The Palghat Gap (Palakkad Gap) connects which two South Indian states? (SSC CHSL 2022)
**Answer:** Kerala and Tamil Nadu
**Key Fact:** It is a ~30–40 km wide low pass in the Western Ghats situated between the Nilgiri Hills to the north and the Anaimalai Hills to the south.

**Q.** Zoji La Pass connects which two cities/regions? (SSC CGL 2019)
**Answer:** Srinagar and Leh
**Key Fact:** Located in the Great Himalayan range (Ladakh), it serves as the strategic gateway linking Kashmir Valley with Kargil and Leh.

**Q.** Which mountain pass is situated in Himachal Pradesh and serves as the entry point for the Sutlej River into India? (SSC CHSL 2021)
**Answer:** Shipki La
**Key Fact:** Located in Kinnaur district on the India–Tibet border, Shipki La was historically an important Indo-Tibetan trade route alongside Nathu La and Lipulekh.

**Q.** India's only active volcano is located on which island? (SSC CGL 2023)
**Answer:** Barren Island
**Key Fact:** Situated in the Andaman Sea (~135 km northeast of Sri Vijaya Puram/Port Blair), while Narcondam Island nearby is an extinct/dormant volcano.

**Q.** Which Indian coast receives the bulk of its annual rainfall during winter from the retreating (Northeast) monsoon? (SSC CHSL 2024)
**Answer:** Coromandel Coast (Tamil Nadu & southern Andhra Pradesh)
**Key Fact:** As northeast winds sweep over the Bay of Bengal between October and December, they pick up moisture and strike Tamil Nadu's coast.

**Q.** Nanda Devi peak, the highest mountain lying entirely within India (undisputed territory), is in which state? (SSC CHSL 2018)
**Answer:** Uttarakhand
**Key Fact:** Standing at 7,816 m in Garhwal Himalayas, it is surrounded by Nanda Devi National Park, a UNESCO World Heritage Site.

**Q.** Which glacier in the Karakoram Range is the longest glacier in India and the world's highest battlefield? (SSC CGL 2020)
**Answer:** Siachen Glacier
**Key Fact:** Stretching ~76 km in eastern Karakoram, it feeds the Nubra River (a tributary of the Shyok River in the Indus basin).

**Q.** The Indian Standard Time (IST) meridian of 82°30'E passes through which place near Prayagraj? (SSC CHSL 2021)
**Answer:** Mirzapur / Naini (Uttar Pradesh)
**Key Fact:** IST (UTC +5:30) passes through 5 Indian states: Uttar Pradesh, Madhya Pradesh, Chhattisgarh, Odisha, and Andhra Pradesh.

**Q.** Which hill range among the Nilgiris, Anaimalai, Cardamom, and Satpura is NOT part of the Western Ghats? (SSC CGL 2023)
**Answer:** Satpura Range
**Key Fact:** Satpura is a central Indian block mountain range running east–west between the Narmada and Tapi rift valleys, whereas the others form the Western Ghats.

---

### Rivers and Dams PYQs (12 Questions)

**Q.** Which two major peninsular rivers flow westward into the Arabian Sea through rift valleys? (SSC CGL 2018 / 2023)
**Answer:** Narmada and Tapi
**Key Fact:** Flowing between the Vindhya, Satpura, and Ajanta ranges, both rivers form estuaries rather than deltas at the Gulf of Khambhat.

**Q.** Which river is known as the "Dakshin Ganga" (Ganga of the South) and originates at Trimbakeshwar in Nashik? (SSC CHSL 2019 / CGL 2024)
**Answer:** Godavari River
**Key Fact:** At 1,465 km, the Godavari is India's second-longest river overall and the longest river of Peninsular India (Vriddha Ganga).

**Q.** Hirakud Dam, the longest major earthen dam in the world, is built across which river? (SSC CHSL 2022 / CGL 2023)
**Answer:** Mahanadi River
**Key Fact:** Located near Sambalpur in Odisha, the main dam section is ~4.8 km long (25.8 km including dykes) and was completed in 1957.

**Q.** Tehri Dam, the highest dam in India (260.5 m), is constructed on which river? (SSC CGL 2021)
**Answer:** Bhagirathi River
**Key Fact:** Located in Tehri Garhwal, Uttarakhand, near the confluence of the Bhagirathi and Bhilangna rivers.

**Q.** Which river is known as the "Sorrow of Bihar" due to its frequent shifting of course and devastating floods? (SSC CGL 2022)
**Answer:** Kosi River
**Key Fact:** Originating in Tibet/Nepal (Saptakoshi), it deposits vast alluvial fans across northern Bihar; similarly, Damodar was historically called "Sorrow of Bengal".

**Q.** The Salal Hydroelectric Project is built on which tributary of the Indus River? (SSC CHSL 2022)
**Answer:** Chenab River
**Key Fact:** Located in Reasi district of Jammu & Kashmir; Baglihar and Dul Hasti dams are also situated on the Chenab.

**Q.** Which is the largest tributary by water volume of the Yamuna River? (SSC CGL 2024)
**Answer:** Tons River
**Key Fact:** The Tons carries more water than the Yamuna itself at Kalsi (Uttarakhand), while the Chambal is the Yamuna's longest tributary overall.

**Q.** Which ephemeral river of Western India originates in the Aravalli Range near Pushkar and vanishes into the Rann of Kutch? (SSC CGL 2024)
**Answer:** Luni River
**Key Fact:** Known as *Sagarmati* at its origin and *Lavanavari* ("Salt river") downstream of Balotra, it is India's largest inland-draining saline river.

**Q.** Majuli, the world's largest inhabited river island, is formed by which river? (SSC CGL 2020)
**Answer:** Brahmaputra River
**Key Fact:** Located in Assam, Majuli was officially declared India's first island district in 2016.

**Q.** Nagarjuna Sagar Dam is built across which river on the border of Telangana and Andhra Pradesh? (SSC CHSL 2021)
**Answer:** Krishna River
**Key Fact:** Originating at Mahabaleshwar in Maharashtra, the Krishna also hosts the Almatti Dam (Karnataka) and Srisailam Dam.

**Q.** Jayakwadi Dam, one of Maharashtra's largest earthen irrigation projects, is located on which river? (SSC CHSL 2021)
**Answer:** Godavari River
**Key Fact:** Situated in Chhatrapati Sambhajinagar (Aurangabad) district, its reservoir is known as Nath Sagar.

**Q.** Which is India's largest inland saltwater lake? (SSC CGL 2019 / 2023)
**Answer:** Sambhar Lake
**Key Fact:** Located southwest of Jaipur in Rajasthan, Sambhar produces ~8–9% of India's salt and is a designated Ramsar Wetland.

---

### National Parks and Wildlife PYQs (12 Questions)

**Q.** Which is the only floating national park in the world, located on Loktak Lake? (SSC CGL 2021 / 2023)
**Answer:** Keibul Lamjao National Park
**Key Fact:** Situated in Bishnupur district of Manipur, its floating biomass islands (*phumdis*) are the sole natural habitat of the endangered Sangai (brow-antlered) deer.

**Q.** In which state is Jim Corbett National Park, India's first national park (established 1936 as Hailey National Park), located? (SSC CGL 2022)
**Answer:** Uttarakhand
**Key Fact:** Drained by the Ramganga River, it became the launch site of **Project Tiger** in April 1973.

**Q.** In which year was Project Tiger launched in India? (SSC CGL Tier II 2022)
**Answer:** 1973
**Key Fact:** Launched on 1 April 1973 under PM Indira Gandhi with 9 initial tiger reserves to arrest declining Royal Bengal Tiger populations.

**Q.** Dachigam National Park in Jammu & Kashmir is famous for protecting which critically endangered species? (SSC CGL 2020)
**Answer:** Hangul (Kashmir Stag)
**Key Fact:** Located ~22 km from Srinagar, Dachigam is the last viable stronghold of the Kashmir Red Deer (*Hangul*).

**Q.** Silent Valley National Park, famous for the endangered Lion-tailed Macaque, is located in which state? (SSC CHSL 2021)
**Answer:** Kerala
**Key Fact:** Located in the Nilgiri Hills (Palakkad district), it is drained by the Kunthipuzha River and spared from hydroelectric flooding by a famous 1970s–80s conservation movement.

**Q.** Which national park in Assam is a UNESCO World Heritage Site and famous for hosting two-thirds of the world's Great One-Horned Rhinoceroses? (SSC CGL 2019)
**Answer:** Kaziranga National Park
**Key Fact:** Spanning the Brahmaputra floodplains across Golaghat and Nagaon districts, it also has high tiger and wild water buffalo densities.

**Q.** Campbell Bay National Park, home to the Nicobar Megapode and Giant Robber Crab, is located in which UT? (SSC CGL 2023)
**Answer:** Andaman and Nicobar Islands
**Key Fact:** Situated on Great Nicobar Island, it forms the core of the Great Nicobar Biosphere Reserve alongside Galathea National Park.

**Q.** In which state is Chandra Prabha Wildlife Sanctuary located? (SSC CGL 2023)
**Answer:** Uttar Pradesh
**Key Fact:** Situated in Chandauli district near Varanasi, it is known for Rajdari and Devdari waterfalls and was once an experimental lion reintroduction site.

**Q.** Namdapha National Park, the largest protected area in the Eastern Himalayan biodiversity hotspot, is in which state? (SSC CGL 2020)
**Answer:** Arunachal Pradesh
**Key Fact:** Located in Changlang district, it is the only park in India known to harbor four big cat species: tiger, leopard, snow leopard, and clouded leopard.

**Q.** Which state is home to Pin Valley National Park, located in a cold-desert Himalayan biosphere? (SSC CHSL 2021)
**Answer:** Himachal Pradesh
**Key Fact:** Situated in Lahaul–Spiti district, it protects snow leopards and Siberian ibex within the Cold Desert Biosphere Reserve.

**Q.** Sariska Tiger Reserve is situated in which district of Rajasthan? (SSC CGL 2021)
**Answer:** Alwar
**Key Fact:** Nestling in the Aravalli Hills, Sariska became famous as the first tiger reserve globally to successfully relocate tigers after local extinction.

**Q.** Manas National Park in Assam gets its name from which river? (SSC CHSL 2022)
**Answer:** Manas River
**Key Fact:** A major northern tributary of the Brahmaputra originating in Bhutan, flowing right through the UNESCO-listed Manas Tiger Reserve and Biosphere Reserve.

---

### World Geography PYQs (12 Questions)

**Q.** Which strait separates Russia (Asia) from Alaska/USA (North America) and connects the Pacific and Arctic oceans? (SSC CGL 2023)
**Answer:** Bering Strait
**Key Fact:** About 85 km wide, the International Date Line runs directly through the Bering Strait between Big Diomede (Russia) and Little Diomede (USA).

**Q.** Which of the following is a cold ocean current: Gulf Stream, Kuroshio, North Atlantic Drift, or Labrador Current? (SSC CHSL 2023)
**Answer:** Labrador Current
**Key Fact:** Flowing southward from the Arctic Ocean past Newfoundland, the Labrador Current collides with the warm Gulf Stream to create rich Grand Banks fisheries and dense sea fog.

**Q.** Which is the largest island in the world by land area? (SSC CHSL 2022)
**Answer:** Greenland
**Key Fact:** Covering ~2.16 million km², Greenland is geographically part of North America and an autonomous territory within the Kingdom of Denmark.

**Q.** The "Ring of Fire", an earthquake- and volcano-prone horseshoe belt, is located around the basin of which ocean? (SSC CGL 2021)
**Answer:** Pacific Ocean
**Key Fact:** Stretching ~40,000 km along subduction zones from New Zealand through Japan and Alaska to Chile, it accounts for ~90% of global earthquakes.

**Q.** Which is the largest freshwater lake in the world by surface area? (SSC CGL 2018)
**Answer:** Lake Superior
**Key Fact:** Spanning 82,100 km² on the USA–Canada border, it is the largest of the five North American Great Lakes (while Lake Baikal is largest by volume).

**Q.** What are the temperate grasslands of South America (Argentina and Uruguay) called? (SSC CHSL 2019)
**Answer:** Pampas
**Key Fact:** Fertile plains famed for wheat cultivation and beef cattle ranching; similarly, North America has Prairies, South Africa has Veld, and Australia has Downs.

**Q.** The 49th Parallel serves as the international boundary between which two countries? (SSC CGL 2017)
**Answer:** USA and Canada
**Key Fact:** Running from Lake of the Woods to the Strait of Georgia, it forms the centerpiece of the world's longest undefended land border.

**Q.** Which country is known as the "Land of the Rising Sun"? (SSC CHSL 2016)
**Answer:** Japan
**Key Fact:** Called *Nihon* or *Nippon* ("origin of the sun"), it lies near the eastern edge of Asia where the sun rises earliest across East Asia.

**Q.** Which is the driest non-polar desert in the world, located along the Pacific coast of Chile and Peru? (SSC CGL 2020)
**Answer:** Atacama Desert
**Key Fact:** Its extreme hyper-aridity stems from the rain shadow of the Andes combined with the cold upwelling Humboldt (Peru) Current.

**Q.** Which artificial waterway connects the Mediterranean Sea directly to the Red Sea? (SSC CHSL 2018)
**Answer:** Suez Canal
**Key Fact:** Opened in 1869 across Egypt's Sinai Isthmus, the 193 km lockless canal cuts maritime travel distance between Europe and India by ~7,000 km.

**Q.** Which river flows through 10 European countries—more countries than any other river in the world? (SSC CGL 2022)
**Answer:** Danube River
**Key Fact:** Rising in Germany's Black Forest and draining into the Black Sea, it passes through 4 national capitals: Vienna, Bratislava, Budapest, and Belgrade.

**Q.** India's Antarctic research stations "Dakshin Gangotri", "Maitri", and "Bharati" are operated in which continent? (SSC CGL 2023)
**Answer:** Antarctica
**Key Fact:** Dakshin Gangotri (1983) was India's first Antarctic base (now decommissioned/supply base), while Maitri (1989) and Bharati (2012) remain active; **Himadri** is in the Arctic (Svalbard).

---

## Exam Strategy for Geography

### 1. Weightage & Pattern Across SSC Exams

| Exam | Total GA Questions | Geography Share | Difficulty Level | Top-Priority Areas |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **SSC CGL Tier-1** | 25 | **3–5 questions (12–20%)** | Easy to Moderate | Rivers & Tributaries, National Parks/Ramsar, Passes & Peaks, Soils, World Straits/Currents |
| **SSC CGL Tier-2** | 25 | **3–4 questions** *(often statement-based)* | Moderate | Physical Geography concepts (Plate tectonics, Monsoons, Rocks, Atmospheric layers), Census & Resources |
| **SSC CHSL Tier-1** | 25 | **3–5 questions (12–20%)** | Easy to Moderate | Indian States/Borders, Dams & Waterfalls, Lakes, Solar System, Grasslands & Deserts |
| **SSC GD / MTS** | 20 / 25 | **3–4 questions** | Easy (Direct Static) | National Symbols, Capitals/State Parks, Longest/Highest/Smallest superlatives, Neighboring borders |

### 2. High-ROI Priority Matrix

- **Tier 1 (Must-Master — ~65% of SSC Geography Questions):**
  1. **Indian Drainage System:** Ganga, Indus, Brahmaputra, Godavari, Krishna, Kaveri, Narmada, Tapi (origins, left/right tributaries, Panch Prayag, dams).
  2. **Indian Physiography:** Himalayan ranges & passes, Western vs Eastern Ghats peaks, Tropic of Cancer (8 states) & IST (5 states), coastal plains.
  3. **Protected Areas:** National Parks, Tiger Reserves, Biosphere Reserves (state mapping + flagship animals).
  4. **Soils, Crops & Climate:** Alluvial, Black (Regur), Laterite soils; Kharif/Rabi/Zaid crops; Monsoon & local pre-monsoon showers.
- **Tier 2 (High Scoring Edge — ~25%):**
  1. **World Superlatives:** Straits/Channels, Mountain ranges, Deserts, Lakes, Waterfalls, Ocean currents (warm vs cold).
  2. **Physical Geography:** Rock types, Earth's interior discontinuities, Atmospheric layers, Winds & Grasslands.
- **Tier 3 (Bonus / Static Tie-Breakers — ~10%):**
  1. **Census 2011**, Mineral belts (coal/iron/bauxite mines), Tribes of India, National Symbols & UT changes (Sri Vijaya Puram, Ladakh languages).

### 3. Proven Tips & Mnemonics for Fast Retention

1. **Tropic of Cancer (8 States) — West to East:**
   - *"Garba Raat Mein Chhota Jhaad West Tripura Mizoram"* → **Gu**jarat, **Ra**jasthan, **M**adhya Pradesh, **Chh**attisgarh, **Jh**arkhand, **W**est Bengal, **T**ripura, **M**izoram.
2. **IST Meridian 82°30'E (5 States) — North to South:**
   - **UMCOA** → **U**ttar Pradesh, **M**adhya Pradesh, **C**hhattisgarh, **O**disha, **A**ndhra Pradesh.
3. **India's 7 Land Borders (Descending Order of Length):**
   - **B**achpan **M**ein **B**hool **A**aya (**B**angladesh 4,096 km > **C**hina 3,488 km > **P**akistan 3,323 km > **N**epal 1,751 km > **M**yanmar 1,643 km > **B**hutan 699 km > **A**fghanistan 106 km).
4. **North American Great Lakes (West to East / Size):**
   - **HOMES:** **H**uron, **O**ntario, **M**ichigan, **E**rie, **S**uperior. *(West to East: **S**uper **M**an **H**elps **E**very **O**ne).*
5. **Indus 5 Tributaries (North to South):**
   - **J-C-R-B-S:** **J**helum, **C**henab, **R**avi, **B**eas, **S**utlej. *(Under 1960 Indus Waters Treaty: **West 3** — Indus, Jhelum, Chenab → Pakistan; **East 3** — Ravi, Beas, Sutlej → India).*
6. **Panch Prayag (North to South along Alaknanda):**
   - **V-N-K-R-D** (*"Vishnu Nand Karn Rudra Dev"*): **Vishnu**prayag (Alaknanda + Dhauliganga) → **Nand**prayag (+ Nandakini) → **Karn**prayag (+ Pindar) → **Rudra**prayag (+ Mandakini) → **Dev**prayag (+ Bhagirathi = **Ganga**).
7. **Always Study with Blank Maps & Pairings:** Never memorize a park, dam, or pass in isolation—group by **State** (e.g., all Himachal passes together: *Rohtang, Baralacha La, Shipki La*) and **River** (e.g., Narmada = *Sardar Sarovar, Indira Sagar, Omkareshwar, Dhuandhar Falls*).

## National Symbols of India

| Symbol Category | Official Symbol | Scientific / Native Name | Adopted / Declared & Key Exam Facts |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **National Flag** | **Tiranga (Tricolour)** | Deep Saffron (Kesaria), White, India Green + Navy Blue 24-spoke Chakra | Adopted **22 July 1947** by Constituent Assembly; designed by **Pingali Venkayya**; width-to-length ratio **2:3**; Chakra taken from Sarnath Lion Capital. |
| **National Emblem** | **State Emblem of India** | Adaptation of **Lion Capital of Ashoka** (Sarnath) | Adopted **26 January 1950**; shows 4 Asiatic lions (3 visible), abacus with **bull, horse, elephant & lion**; motto **"Satyameva Jayate"** (*Mundaka Upanishad*, Devanagari script). |
| **National Anthem** | **"Jana Gana Mana"** | Original Bengali song (*Bharoto Bhagyo Bidhata*) | Composed by **Rabindranath Tagore**; adopted **24 January 1950**; first sung **27 Dec 1911** (Calcutta INC); formal playing time **52 seconds** (short version 20 sec). |
| **National Song** | **"Vande Mataram"** | Sanskrit / Bengali (from novel *Anandamath*) | Composed by **Bankim Chandra Chatterjee**; adopted **24 January 1950** (equal status with Anthem); first sung **1896** (Calcutta INC, tuned by Tagore). |
| **National Calendar** | **Saka Calendar** | Indian National Calendar (Saka Era, 78 CE) | Adopted **22 March 1957**; first month **1 Chaitra** falls on **22 March** (normal year) or **21 March** (leap year); 365 days. |
| **National Currency** | **Indian Rupee (₹)** | Symbol blends Devanagari **'र'** and Roman **'R'** | Symbol designed by **D. Udaya Kumar** (IIT Bombay); approved by Union Cabinet on **15 July 2010**; issued by RBI. |
| **National Animal** | **Royal Bengal Tiger** | *Panthera tigris* | Declared National Animal in **1973** (with Project Tiger); replaced Asiatic Lion (pre-1972). |
| **National Bird** | **Indian Peacock** | *Pavo cristatus* | Declared National Bird in **1963**; protected under Wildlife Protection Act, 1972. |
| **National Flower** | **Lotus** | *Nelumbo nucifera* | Sacred symbol of purity, wealth & knowledge in Indian culture and art. |
| **National Tree** | **Indian Banyan** | *Ficus benghalensis* | Known as *Kalpavriksha*; aerial prop roots symbolize immortality and vast unity. |
| **National Fruit** | **Mango** | *Mangifera indica* | Over 1,000 indigenous varieties (Alphonso, Dasheri, Langra); native to Indian subcontinent. |
| **National River** | **Ganga (Ganges)** | Bhagirathi–Ganga | Declared on **4 November 2008**; longest river in India (2,525 km). |
| **National Aquatic Animal** | **Ganges River Dolphin** | *Platanista gangetica* | Declared **5 October 2009** (notified May 2010); locally called **Susu**; functionally blind, hunts via echolocation. |
| **National Heritage Animal** | **Indian Elephant** | *Elephas maximus indicus* | Declared in **October 2010** under Project Elephant (1992) to boost conservation. |
| **National Pledge** | **"India is my country..."** | Originally composed in Telugu (1962) | Written by **Pydimarri Venkata Subba Rao**; first read publicly in Visakhapatnam schools (1963). |
| **National Vegetable** *(Common Static GK)* | **Indian Pumpkin (Kaddu)** | *Cucurbita maxima / moschata* | Frequently asked in state/static GK quizzes, though not a formal constitutional symbol on *india.gov.in*. |

> **Critical SSC Exam Traps on National Symbols:**
> - **Does India have a National Language?** **NO.** The Constitution recognizes **no national language**; **Hindi (Devanagari)** and **English** are the **Official Languages of the Union** (Article 343), and the 8th Schedule lists **22 Scheduled Languages**.
> - **Does India have an official National Game?** **NO.** The Ministry of Youth Affairs & Sports clarified via RTI that **Field Hockey is NOT officially declared** the National Game of India (though commonly misbelieved).
> - **National Reptile:** King Cobra (*Ophiophagus hannah*) is widely cited in static GK books, though not formally listed under official National Identity Elements on *india.gov.in*.

---

## Indian States and UTs Quick Reference

| State/UT | Capital | Largest City | Official Language | Area Rank | Key Geographic Feature |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Andhra Pradesh** *(State)* | Amaravati | Visakhapatnam | Telugu *(2nd: Urdu)* | **7** *(State: 7)* | 2nd longest mainland coastline (~974 km); Krishna–Godavari Delta; Eastern Ghats (*Arma Konda*); Sriharikota Island. |
| **Arunachal Pradesh** *(State)* | Itanagar | Itanagar | English | **14** *(State: 14)* | Easternmost state (**Kibithu**); **Namdapha NP**; Dihang/Brahmaputra entry gorge; Diphu & Bomdi La passes; lowest population density among states (17/km²). |
| **Assam** *(State)* | Dispur | Guwahati | Assamese *(Bodo in BTR; Bengali in Barak Valley)* | **16** *(State: 16)* | **Brahmaputra Valley**; **Majuli** (largest river island); **Kaziranga** & **Manas** National Parks; Digboi (oldest oil refinery). |
| **Bihar** *(State)* | Patna | Patna | Hindi *(2nd: Urdu)* | **12** *(State: 12)* | **Gangetic Alluvial Plain**; **Kosi** ("Sorrow of Bihar"), Gandak & Son rivers; **Kanwar Lake** (oxbow); highest population density among states (1,106/km²). |
| **Chhattisgarh** *(State)* | Raipur *(Nava Raipur)* | Raipur | Hindi, Chhattisgarhi | **9** *(State: 9)* | **Mahanadi Basin** ("Rice Bowl of Central India"); **Bastar Plateau**; **Chitrakote Falls**; Bailadila iron ore mines. |
| **Goa** *(State)* | Panaji | Vasco da Gama | Konkani *(Marathi additional)* | **31** *(State: 28 — Smallest State)* | Konkan Coast between Western Ghats & Arabian Sea; **Mandovi & Zuari** estuaries; **Dudhsagar Falls**. |
| **Gujarat** *(State)* | Gandhinagar | Ahmedabad | Gujarati, Hindi | **5** *(State: 5)* | **Longest coastline in India**; **Rann of Kutch** (salt marsh); **Gir National Park** (Asiatic Lions); Narmada & Sabarmati mouths. |
| **Haryana** *(State)* | Chandigarh | Faridabad | Hindi *(2nd: Punjabi)* | **21** *(State: 20)* | Indo-Gangetic fertile plain; **Ghaggar–Hakra** seasonal basin; Morni Hills (Shiwaliks); **Sultanpur NP**. |
| **Himachal Pradesh** *(State)* | Shimla *(Summer)*, Dharamshala *(Winter)* | Shimla | Hindi *(2nd: Sanskrit)* | **18** *(State: 17)* | Western Himalayas (Pir Panjal & Dhauladhar); **Rohtang, Shipki La, Bara-lacha La** passes; Beas, Sutlej, Ravi, Chenab; **Great Himalayan NP**. |
| **Jharkhand** *(State)* | Ranchi | Jamshedpur | Hindi *(+16 additional incl. Santali, Mundari, Bengali, Odia, Urdu)* | **15** *(State: 15)* | **Chota Nagpur Plateau** ("Ruhr of India"); **Parasnath Hill** (highest peak); **Hundru Falls**; Subarnarekha & Damodar rivers; **Betla NP**. |
| **Karnataka** *(State)* | Bengaluru | Bengaluru | Kannada | **6** *(State: 6)* | Deccan Plateau & Western Ghats (*Mullayanagiri* highest peak); **Kunchikal & Jog Falls**; **Bandipur & Nagarhole**; Kaveri, Krishna & Sharavati. |
| **Kerala** *(State)* | Thiruvananthapuram | Thiruvananthapuram *(Kochi largest UA)* | Malayalam, English | **23** *(State: 21)* | **Malabar Coast** & **Vembanad** backwaters (*kayals*); **Anamudi** (highest peak in South India); **Silent Valley & Periyar**; first state hit by SW Monsoon. |
| **Madhya Pradesh** *(State)* | Bhopal | Indore | Hindi | **2** *(State: 2)* | **Malwa Plateau, Vindhya & Satpura ranges** (*Dhupgarh* peak); **Narmada–Son** source (Amarkantak); highest forest cover by area & maximum National Parks (incl. **Kanha, Bandhavgarh, Kuno**). |
| **Maharashtra** *(State)* | Mumbai *(Summer)*, Nagpur *(Winter)* | Mumbai | Marathi | **3** *(State: 3)* | **Deccan Trap** basalt plateau & **Sahyadri** (Western Ghats, *Kalsubai* peak); **Lonar Crater Lake**; **Godavari & Krishna** origins; Konkan Coast. |
| **Manipur** *(State)* | Imphal | Imphal | Meitei (Manipuri), English | **25** *(State: 23)* | Purvanchal Hills surrounding central **Imphal Valley**; **Loktak Lake** & **Keibul Lamjao** (only floating NP). |
| **Meghalaya** *(State)* | Shillong | Shillong | English *(Khasi, Garo associate)* | **24** *(State: 22)* | **Shillong Plateau** (Garo, Khasi, Jaintia hills — part of Peninsular block); **Mawsynram & Cherrapunji** (wettest places on Earth); **Nohkalikai Falls**. |
| **Mizoram** *(State)* | Aizawl | Aizawl | Mizo, English, Hindi | **26** *(State: 24)* | Lushai (Mizo) Hills (*Phawngpui / Blue Mountain*); Tropic of Cancer passes through; **Vantawng Falls**; high forest percentage (~85%+). |
| **Nagaland** *(State)* | Kohima | Dimapur | English | **27** *(State: 25)* | **Naga Hills** (*Mount Saramati* highest peak); Doyang & Dhansiri rivers; **Intanki NP**. |
| **Odisha** *(State)* | Bhubaneswar | Bhubaneswar | Odia | **8** *(State: 8)* | Utkal Coastal Plains & Eastern Ghats (*Deomali* peak); **Mahanadi & Hirakud Dam**; **Chilika Lake** (largest lagoon); **Bhitarkanika & Simlipal** (**Barehipani Falls**). |
| **Punjab** *(State)* | Chandigarh | Ludhiana | Punjabi | **20** *(State: 19)* | Fertile alluvial **"Land of Five Rivers"** (Bari & Bist Doabs); Harike Wetland (confluence of Beas & Sutlej); 0% forest-free intensive agriculture hub. |
| **Rajasthan** *(State)* | Jaipur | Jaipur | Hindi | **1** *(State: 1 — Largest State)* | **Thar Desert** (Great Indian Desert); **Aravalli Range** (*Guru Shikhar*); **Sambhar Lake**; **Luni River**; **Keoladeo, Ranthambore & Sariska**. |
| **Sikkim** *(State)* | Gangtok | Gangtok | English, Nepali, Sikkimese (Bhutia), Lepcha *(+8 additional)* | **30** *(State: 27)* | **Kangchenjunga** (8,586 m — highest Himalayan peak in India); **Teesta River**; **Nathu La & Jelep La**; **Tso Lhamo** (highest lake); 1st 100% organic state. |
| **Tamil Nadu** *(State)* | Chennai | Chennai | Tamil *(English additional)* | **10** *(State: 10)* | **Coromandel Coast** (winter NE monsoon rain); **Nilgiri Hills** (*Doddabetta*); **Kanyakumari** (mainland southernmost tip); Kaveri Delta; **Pulicat Lake**. |
| **Telangana** *(State)* | Hyderabad | Hyderabad | Telugu *(2nd: Urdu)* | **11** *(State: 11)* | **Deccan (Telangana) Plateau**; drained by **Godavari & Krishna** rivers; **Nagarjuna Sagar & Kaleshwaram**; Hussain Sagar & Singareni coalfields. |
| **Tripura** *(State)* | Agartala | Agartala | Bengali, English, Kokborok | **28** *(State: 26)* | Surrounded on **three sides by Bangladesh**; Jampui Hills; Gomati River; Rudrasagar Lake (Neermahal). |
| **Uttar Pradesh** *(State)* | Lucknow | Kanpur *(City)* / Lucknow *(UA)* | Hindi *(2nd: Urdu)* | **4** *(State: 4)* | **Most populous state**; vast **Ganga–Yamuna Doab** & Terai plains; **Prayagraj** Sangam; shares borders with **8 states + 1 UT (Delhi) + Nepal** (maximum neighbors!). |
| **Uttarakhand** *(State)* | Dehradun *(Winter)*, Bhararisain/Gairsain *(Summer)* | Dehradun | Hindi *(2nd: Sanskrit)* | **19** *(State: 18)* | Central Himalayas (*Nanda Devi, Kamet*); **Gangotri & Yamunotri** glaciers; **Panch Prayag**; **Tehri Dam**; **Jim Corbett & Valley of Flowers NPs**. |
| **West Bengal** *(State)* | Kolkata | Kolkata | Bengali, English *(+regional languages incl. Nepali in hills)* | **13** *(State: 13)* | Stretches from **Darjeeling Himalayas** (Sandakphu) to **Sundarbans Delta** (world's largest mangrove forest); **Hooghly & Damodar** rivers; **Farakka Barrage**. |
| **Andaman & Nicobar Islands** *(UT)* | **Sri Vijaya Puram** *(renamed from Port Blair in Sept 2024)* | Sri Vijaya Puram | Hindi, English | **29** *(UT: 3)* | 572+ islands in Bay of Bengal; **Saddle Peak**; **Indira Point** (southernmost point of India); **Barren Island** (only active volcano); **10° Channel**. |
| **Chandigarh** *(UT)* | Chandigarh | Chandigarh | English | **35** *(UT: 7)* | Joint capital of **Punjab & Haryana**; planned by **Le Corbusier** at Shiwalik foothills; **Sukhna Lake**. |
| **Dadra & Nagar Haveli and Daman & Diu** *(UT)* | Daman | Silvassa | Gujarati, Hindi, English, Marathi, Konkani | **33** *(UT: 5)* | Formed 26 Jan 2020 by merging two UTs; coastal enclaves bordering Gujarat & Maharashtra; Daman Ganga River. |
| **Delhi (NCT)** *(UT)* | New Delhi | Delhi | Hindi, English *(2nd: Punjabi, Urdu)* | **32** *(UT: 4)* | Surrounded by Haryana & UP; **Yamuna River** & **Delhi Ridge** (northern extension of Aravallis); highest population density in India (11,320/km²). |
| **Jammu & Kashmir** *(UT)* | Srinagar *(Summer)*, Jammu *(Winter)* | Srinagar | Kashmiri, Dogri, Urdu, Hindi, English | **22** *(UT: 2)* | Kashmir Valley (Karewa soils, saffron) & Pir Panjal; **Wular Lake** (largest freshwater lake) & **Dal Lake**; **Jhelum & Chenab**; **Dachigam NP**. |

> **Area Rank Note:** Rankings above show **Combined Rank (1 to 36)** across all States and UTs based on administered area, alongside **State-only (1–28)** or **UT-only (1–8)** rank in parentheses. Ladakh's administered area is **59,146 km²** (Rank 17 overall / 1st among UTs; total claimed area 166,698 km²) and Jammu & Kashmir's administered area is **42,241 km²** (Rank 22 overall / 2nd among UTs).
